### 6. clement-of-alexandria — Clement of Alexandria


**Checked:** the headline quote against the ANF text of *Stromata* I.5; the Origen-as-student claim and the
"head of the Catechetical School" description against the scholarship on the Alexandrian school; the
surviving-works count against the list of extant treatises; canonization status; dates. Verified by the
coordinating session, not a subagent.

| Field | Claim as written | What the sources say | Verdict | Sources |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `life[0]` — "His most famous student was **Origen**." | Stated as fact | **Disputed.** It rests on Eusebius (*HE* VI), and "modern historians offer serious objections". Origen **never cites Clement anywhere in his writings**, which is the main ground for doubt; there is also a chronological problem, since Clement appears to have left Alexandria (c. 202) and may already have been dead by the time Alexander of Jerusalem wrote to Origen. Their intellectual affinity is real; the teacher–pupil link is not established. | `contested` → hedge, e.g. `en: "Eusebius names Origen among his pupils, though modern scholars doubt this — Origen never cites him."` / `vi: "Êusêbiô kể Ôrigênê trong số các môn sinh của ngài, dù các học giả hiện đại nghi ngờ điều này — Ôrigênê không hề trích dẫn ngài ở đâu cả."` | [Encyclopedia.com, Clement of Alexandria](https://www.encyclopedia.com/people/philosophy-and-religion/roman-catholic-and-orthodox-churches-general-biographies/clement-alexandria), [Stanford Encyclopedia of Philosophy, Origen](https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/origen/), [IEP, Origen of Alexandria](https://iep.utm.edu/origen-of-alexandria/) |
| `facts` "Surviving works: 3 works" / "3 tác phẩm"; `works[]` lists only the trilogy | Implies three surviving works | **Undercounts.** Beyond the trilogy, ***Quis dives salvetur?*** (*Who Is the Rich Man That Is Saved?*) survives **complete**, as do the *Excerpta ex Theodoto* and the *Eclogae propheticae*; other treatises survive in fragments. *Quis dives salvetur?* is a substantial and widely read work, not a scrap. | `corrected` → `facts` Surviving works `en: "The trilogy complete, plus Quis dives salvetur? and two shorter collections"` / `vi: "Trọn bộ ba tác phẩm chính, cùng với Quis dives salvetur? và hai tuyển tập ngắn hơn"`, and add *Quis dives salvetur?* to `works[]` (`vi: "Người giàu có nào được cứu độ?"`) | [Encyclopedia.com, Clement of Alexandria](https://www.encyclopedia.com/people/philosophy-and-religion/roman-catholic-and-orthodox-churches-general-biographies/clement-alexandria), [Cambridge, *Clement of Alexandria* front matter](https://assets.cambridge.org/97805218/37538/frontmatter/9780521837538_frontmatter.pdf) |
| `role` — "**head of** the Catechetical School of Alexandria"; `life[0]` "whom he **succeeded** c. 180 AD" | An institutional office with a succession | Softer than stated. The same scholarship that doubts the Origen link holds that Clement's teaching "probably never had an official character, but remained a private enterprise, in keeping with the pedagogic practice of other philosophers in those days" — i.e. the "school" as a formal institution with a head and a succession list is largely Eusebius's retrojection. Not a flat error (the entry follows the traditional account), but it states as institutional fact something scholars treat as a later construction. | `contested` → keep the traditional description but hedge the succession, e.g. `life[0]` `en: "Studied under Pantaenus and took over his teaching c. 180 AD, though whether the Alexandrian 'school' was yet a formal institution with an official succession is debated."` / `vi: "Học với Pantaenô và kế tục việc giảng dạy của ông khoảng năm 180, dù việc 'trường' Alexandria khi ấy đã là một định chế chính thức có sự kế nhiệm chính thức hay chưa vẫn còn được tranh luận."` | [Encyclopedia.com, Clement of Alexandria](https://www.encyclopedia.com/people/philosophy-and-religion/roman-catholic-and-orthodox-churches-general-biographies/clement-alexandria) |
| `quote` (*Stromata* I.5) | "Philosophy... was a schoolmaster to bring the Hellenic mind, **as the Law brought the Hebrews**, to Christ." | Near-verbatim but silently smoothed. ANF reads: "For this was a schoolmaster to bring **the** Hellenic mind, **as the law, the Hebrews**, to Christ." The entry supplies "Philosophy" for the pronoun (fair, and clearer) but also expands the deliberately elliptical "as the law, the Hebrews" into "as the Law brought the Hebrews" without marking the insertion. | `corrected` → mark the editorial help, e.g. `en: "[Philosophy]... was a schoolmaster to bring the Hellenic mind, as the law, the Hebrews, to Christ."` | [ANF *Stromata* I.5 (newadvent)](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/02101.htm) |
| `facts` "Canonized: No" | Bare "No" | **Confirmed and worth keeping** — Clement was dropped from the Roman Martyrology (his name removed under Clement VIII, the decision upheld by Benedict XIV), which is why the entry's own feast-day row correctly says "no longer on the current Roman calendar". Flagged `confirmed` only so a later pass does not "fix" the "No" to "Yes" on seeing him called "St. Clement" in older literature. | `confirmed` (notable) | [Encyclopedia.com, Clement of Alexandria](https://www.encyclopedia.com/people/philosophy-and-religion/roman-catholic-and-orthodox-churches-general-biographies/clement-alexandria) |

**Not checked:** the Eastern feast of 24 November; the "possibly born in Athens" detail; the flight from
Alexandria in the Severan persecution of 202 (widely reported, not separately sourced here); all six
`sections[]` bodies, which I did not open — **the `more-quotes` section in particular has not been
verified and, on the pattern found in entries 1–3, is the single likeliest place for a further
misquotation in this entry.** `ccc_refs` is empty. Vietnamese `Clêmentê thành Alexandria` matches the
site's own Catechism usage.

**Keep for `sources` retrofit:** [ANF *Stromata* (newadvent)](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/02101.htm) · [Encyclopedia.com, Clement of Alexandria](https://www.encyclopedia.com/people/philosophy-and-religion/roman-catholic-and-orthodox-churches-general-biographies/clement-alexandria)

#### Entry 6 — `sections[]` bodies (completion pass)

**Checked:** all six `sections[]` bodies opened and verified. The `more-quotes` narrative was traced
sentence-by-sentence against the ANF text of *Quis dives salvetur?* ch. 42 (newadvent). The
`context`/`later-influence` claim that the 202 persecution killed Clement's students was checked
against Eusebius *HE* VI.1–4. Clement's flight, place of death, and removal from the Martyrology
were checked against the 1913 Catholic Encyclopedia, the 1911 Britannica, and OrthodoxWiki. The
Hypotyposes/Photius charges against the 1913 CE list and the Brill monograph description. The
trilogy/*Didascalus* question against the 1913 CE, 1911 EB and CUA Press. All three
`further-reading` bibliographic entries were verified and **all three check out** — Osborn,
*Clement of Alexandria*, CUP 2005 (ISBN 0521837537, xviii+324 pp.); Ferguson trans., *Stromateis,
Books 1–3*, Fathers of the Church vol. 85, CUA Press 1991 (ISBN 0813214335); the Roberts–Donaldson
ANF is indeed free at newadvent.org. No rows are needed for `further-reading`.

| Field | Claim as written | What the sources say | Verdict | Sources |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `sections[context]` — "killing several of his own students" / "giết hại một số học trò của chính ngài" | The 202 persecution killed students of **Clement's** | **Not attested.** Eusebius names the martyred pupils — Plutarch, Serenus (×2), Heraclides, Hero, Herais — and attaches them explicitly to **Origen**, after Origen "was in his eighteenth year when he took charge of the catechetical school," dated to "the tenth year of the reign of Severus, while Lætus was governor of Alexandria" (i.e. 202/3, *after* Clement had gone). No source makes them Clement's students. | `unverifiable` → drop the attribution, e.g. `en: "...reached Alexandria in 202 AD, killing several students of the catechetical school and driving Clement himself out"` / `vi: "...lan đến Alexandria năm 202, giết hại một số học trò của trường giáo lý và buộc chính Clêmentê phải chạy trốn"` | [Eusebius, *HE* VI (newadvent)](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/250106.htm) |
| `sections[context]` — "driving Clement himself into exile in Cappadocia, **where he died**" / "chạy trốn sang Cappadocia, **nơi ngài qua đời**" | Death in Cappadocia stated as fact | **Pure conjecture.** 1911 EB: "How long he continued in Alexandria, and when and where he died, are all matters of pure conjecture"; it is only *conjectured* that he joined Alexander in Cappadocia and may have followed him to Jerusalem. Alexander's letter to Antioch (*HE* VI.11), carried by Clement c. 211, was written from **Jerusalem**. Modern summaries: "whether he died in Cappadocia, Antioch, or Jerusalem is unknown." | `contested` → hedge, e.g. `en: "...driving Clement himself out of Egypt — probably to his former pupil Alexander in Cappadocia, though where he died is unknown"` / `vi: "...buộc Clêmentê phải rời khỏi Ai Cập — có lẽ đến với người học trò cũ là Alexanđê ở Cappadocia, dù nơi ngài qua đời thì không ai biết"` | [1911 EB, Clement of Alexandria](https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/1911_Encyclop%C3%A6dia_Britannica/Clement_of_Alexandria), [1913 CE, Clement of Alexandria](https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/04045a.htm), [Eusebius, *HE* VI.11](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/250106.htm) |
| `sections[martyrdom]` — "died of natural causes in Cappadocian exile around 215 AD" / "qua đời vì nguyên nhân tự nhiên trong cảnh lưu đày tại Cappadocia khoảng năm 215" | Manner **and** place of death stated as fact | Same problem, stated harder. The only evidence is negative: Alexander's c. 215 letter to Origen names "the holy Clement, my master and benefactor" among "those blessed fathers who have trodden the way before us," which is read as meaning he was by then dead. Nothing records how or where. "Not a martyr" is safe; "of natural causes **in Cappadocian exile**" is not. | `contested` → e.g. `en: "Clement was not a martyr — he fled the 202 AD persecution rather than face it, and was dead by about 215, though no source records where or how he died."` / `vi: "Clêmentê không phải là một vị tử đạo — ngài đã chạy trốn cuộc bách hại năm 202 thay vì đối diện với nó, và đã qua đời trước khoảng năm 215, dù không nguồn nào ghi lại ngài mất ở đâu hay vì lý do gì."` | [1911 EB](https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/1911_Encyclop%C3%A6dia_Britannica/Clement_of_Alexandria), [Eusebius, *HE* VI.14](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/250106.htm) |
| `sections[martyrdom]` — "while **Eastern Orthodoxy** has continued to honor him" / "trong khi Chính Thống giáo Đông phương vẫn tiếp tục tôn kính ngài" | Eastern Orthodox veneration continues | **Wrong church family.** OrthodoxWiki: "Although Clement is **not** commemorated as a saint in Eastern Orthodoxy, he was formerly commemorated by the Roman Catholic Church." Continuing veneration is in the **Coptic** and Ethiopian (Oriental Orthodox) churches, some Eastern Catholic churches, and Anglicanism — commemorated 4 December. | `corrected` → `en: "...while the Coptic and Ethiopian churches, and Anglicans, have continued to commemorate him"` / `vi: "...trong khi các Giáo hội Cốp-tô và Êthiôpia, cùng Anh giáo, vẫn tiếp tục kính nhớ ngài"` | [OrthodoxWiki, Clement of Alexandria](https://orthodoxwiki.org/Clement_of_Alexandria), [New World Encyclopedia, Clement of Alexandria](https://www.newworldencyclopedia.org/entry/Clement_of_Alexandria) |
| `sections[martyrdom]` — "his feast was eventually removed from the Roman Martyrology **under Pope Clement VIII** on Cardinal Baronius's advice" | One pope, one act | **Sources genuinely disagree**, and one denies the premise. (a) 1913 CE / OrthodoxWiki: dropped when **Clement VIII** revised the Martyrology, on Baronius's advice, upheld by Benedict XIV. (b) Other accounts: removed by **Sixtus V in 1586**, on Baronius's advice. (c) A third line holds his name was **never in** the printed Roman Martyrology at all (first ed. 1583) — it stood in Usuard's medieval martyrology, and the printed RM simply never carried it. Note (a) is largely 1913-CE-derived and is the common ancestor of most of the "consensus" web. | `contested` → hedge the agent, e.g. `en: "...his name was not carried into the printed Roman Martyrology, on Cardinal Baronius's advice (sources differ on whether under Sixtus V in 1586 or under Clement VIII), a decision Benedict XIV later upheld"` / `vi: "...tên ngài đã không được đưa vào Sổ Tử Đạo Rôma ấn hành, theo đề nghị của Hồng y Baroniô (các nguồn khác nhau về việc điều này xảy ra dưới thời Đức Sixtô V năm 1586 hay dưới thời Đức Clêmentê VIII), và Đức Bênêđictô XIV về sau đã giữ nguyên quyết định ấy"` | [1913 CE](https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/04045a.htm), [OrthodoxWiki](https://orthodoxwiki.org/Clement_of_Alexandria), [New World Encyclopedia](https://www.newworldencyclopedia.org/entry/Clement_of_Alexandria) |
| `sections[more-quotes]` — Clement tells it "**not as a story, but as an account**" / "không phải như một truyện kể, mà như một tường thuật" | Presented inside quotation marks as Clement's words | **Matches no published translation.** ANF (Roberts–Donaldson) reads: "listen to a tale, **which is not a tale but a narrative**, handed down and committed to the custody of memory, about the Apostle John." The entry's version reshapes the clause and changes "tale/narrative" to "story/account" while keeping the quotation marks. | `corrected` → quote the ANF, e.g. `en: "Clement introduces it as \"a tale, which is not a tale but a narrative, handed down and committed to the custody of memory\""` / `vi: "Clêmentê giới thiệu câu chuyện như \"một truyện kể mà không phải truyện kể, nhưng là một tường thuật, được lưu truyền và ký thác cho ký ức\""` | [ANF, *Quis dives salvetur?* 42 (newadvent)](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/0207.htm), [Perseus, *Quis dives salvetur* 42](http://www.perseus.tufts.edu/hopper/text?doc=Perseus:text:2008.01.0631:chapter%3D42) |
| `sections[more-quotes]` — "John rode out **alone** to the robbers' hideout" / "Gioan **một mình** cưỡi ngựa đến sào huyệt bọn cướp" | Rode alone | Contradicted by the text. John says: "Let a horse be brought me, and **let some one be my guide on the way**." He then "rode away, just as he was, straight from the church," and on arriving "is arrested by the robbers' outpost." The point the passage makes is that he was **unarmed and old**, not that he was unaccompanied. | `corrected` → `en: "John called for a horse and a guide and rode straight out to the robbers' hideout"` / `vi: "Gioan xin một con ngựa và một người dẫn đường, rồi cưỡi thẳng đến sào huyệt bọn cướp"` | [ANF, *Quis dives salvetur?* 42](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/0207.htm) |
| `sections[more-quotes]` — "entrusted a promising **young convert** to a local bishop **for formation**" / "giao một tân tòng trẻ đầy triển vọng cho một giám mục địa phương để đào tạo" | Already a convert when entrusted | Reversed order. In the text the youth is **not yet baptized** when John hands him over — "This (youth) I commit to you in all earnestness, in the presence of the Church, and with Christ as witness" — and the baptism comes afterwards: "the presbyter taking home the youth committed to him, **reared, kept, cherished, and finally baptized him**." (This matters, because the whole point of the homily is post-**baptismal** sin.) | `corrected` → `en: "...entrusted a promising young man to a local bishop, who reared him and in time baptized him"` / `vi: "...giao một thanh niên đầy triển vọng cho một giám mục địa phương; vị này nuôi dạy và sau cùng đã rửa tội cho anh"` | [ANF, *Quis dives salvetur?* 42](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/0207.htm) |
| `sections[theology]` — the three works are "**stages of one deliberate curriculum**"; the *Stromata* "finally opens into deeper theological reflection" as the third stage | The trilogy as a realized, intended programme | **Genuinely debated.** Clement announced a third work, the *Didascalus* (Teacher), and whether the *Stromateis* is it — or whether the promised work was ever written — is unsettled. 1913 CE: "Some such underlying conception was doubtless before the mind of Clement, but **it can hardly be said to have been realized**." CUA Press's own blurb for the Ferguson volume hedges: the *Stromateis* is "**possibly** the third work in his trilogy." The 1911 EB describes the progression without identifying the *Stromateis* as the promised *Didascalus*. | `contested` → hedge the third leg, e.g. `en: "...and the Stromata (\"Miscellanies\") finally opens into deeper reflection for the spiritually mature — though Clement had announced a third work called the Didascalus, and scholars still debate whether the Stromata is it or whether the promised book was ever written."` / `vi: "...và sau cùng Stromata (\"Tạp Lục\") mở ra những suy tư sâu xa hơn cho những người đã trưởng thành về tâm linh — dù Clêmentê từng loan báo một tác phẩm thứ ba mang tên Didascalus, và các học giả vẫn tranh luận liệu Stromata có phải là tác phẩm ấy, hay cuốn sách được hứa ấy chưa từng được viết."` | [1913 CE](https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/04045a.htm), [CUA Press, *Stromateis, Books 1–3*](https://www.cuapress.org/9780813214337/stromateis-books-1-3/), [1911 EB](https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/1911_Encyclop%C3%A6dia_Britannica/Clement_of_Alexandria) |
| `sections[theology]` — "a now-lost **late** work of his, the Hypotyposes" / "một tác phẩm **muộn** nay đã thất truyền" | Dated late in his career | The *Hypotyposeis* (8 books of scriptural outlines) is securely **lost** and securely **read by Photius**, but neither the 1913 CE nor the 1911 EB assigns it a date, and its place in the Clementine corpus is an open scholarly question (the Ashwin-Siejkowski line of argument is precisely about re-placing it). "Late" is an assumption, not a datum. | `unverifiable` → drop the qualifier: `en: "a now-lost work of his, the Hypotyposes"` / `vi: "một tác phẩm nay đã thất truyền của ngài, Hypotyposes"` | [1913 CE](https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/04045a.htm), [1911 EB](https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/1911_Encyclop%C3%A6dia_Britannica/Clement_of_Alexandria), [Brill, *Clement of Alexandria on Trial*](https://brill.com/display/title/12674) |
| `sections[theology]` — Photius accused the *Hypotyposes* of errors "including on the eternity of matter and the nature of the Son"; work survives "only in Photius's hostile summary"; scholars still debate its fairness | The charge list and the scholarly caveat | **Both correct — do not "fix" later.** The 1913 CE gives Photius's list as "the eternity of matter, the creatureship of the Word, plurality of words (Logoi), Docetism, metempsychosis" — so "eternity of matter" and "the nature of the Son" are both genuinely on it. The 1911 EB already argued Photius had a corrupt copy or misread Clement, and a 2010 Brill monograph is devoted to re-examining each of the eight alleged heresies. Flagging `confirmed` because the claim looks tendentious and is not. | `confirmed` (notable) | [1913 CE](https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/04045a.htm), [1911 EB](https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/1911_Encyclop%C3%A6dia_Britannica/Clement_of_Alexandria), [Brill, *Clement of Alexandria on Trial*](https://brill.com/display/title/12674) |
| `sections[theology]` / VN only — "thượng phụ Byzantine **Photios**" | Greek nominative form, untransliterated | The site's own Vietnamese uses **"Photiô"** throughout `content/cong-dong/constantinople-iv.json` ("thượng phụ Photiô", "Phế truất Photiô, phục hồi Inhaxiô"). This entry is the only place using "Photios" — an internal inconsistency, not a stylistic preference. | `corrected` → `vi: "thượng phụ Byzantine Photiô"` | site file `content/cong-dong/constantinople-iv.json` (lines 22, 44, 53) |
| `sections[context]` — Alexandria "home to the great Library" / "nơi có Thư viện vĩ đại" | The Library as a going concern in Clement's day (c. 180–215) | Softer than stated. Athenaeus c. 200 still refers to the Museum's book collection and its fame, so the claim is not flatly false — but ancient sources give no direct evidence of the Library's existence after the 1st century AD, and its decline through the 2nd century is the scholarly picture. Minor, and only worth a light touch. | `contested` → soften, e.g. `en: "home to the Museum and its famous library"` / `vi: "nơi có Bảo Tàng Viện (Mouseion) và thư viện trứ danh của nó"` | [Open University, *Library of Alexandria*: the destruction of the library](https://www.open.edu/openlearn/history-the-arts/library-alexandria/content-section-3), [TheCollector, The Rise and Ruin of the Great Library of Alexandria](https://www.thecollector.com/great-library-of-alexandria/) |
| `sections[later-influence]` — "Clement's most consequential legacy may simply be **his student: Origen**" (opening sentence) | Restates the teacher–pupil link as fact, and builds the whole paragraph on it | **Cross-reference only — not a new finding.** This is the same claim already recorded as `contested` for `life[0]` in the earlier pass. Noting it because the fix has to be applied *here too*: the section's opening sentence and its VN twin ("Di sản có hệ quả lớn nhất của Clêmentê có lẽ đơn giản chính là học trò của ngài: Ôrigênê") need the same hedge, or the correction to `life[0]` will leave the contradiction sitting in the body text. Origen's *succession to the school* itself is separately attested by Eusebius (*HE* VI.3) and is not in doubt. | `contested` (echo of an existing row) | [Eusebius, *HE* VI.3](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/250106.htm) — plus the sources on the `life[0]` row in `frag-06.md` |
| **ADDENDUM** — `facts[]` "Feast day": "Historically November 23 (West...); November 24 (Eastern)" | Two November dates | Surfaced while verifying `sections[martyrdom]`, so recording it here even though the row is in `facts[]` (the earlier pass listed the feast day under "Not checked"). Every source consulted gives **4 December**: 1913 CE — "his feast fell on the fourth of December"; he stands in Usuard's martyrology; and the Copts and Anglicans still keep him on **4 December**. **23 November is the memorial of Pope St. Clement I of Rome** in the current General Roman Calendar — the two Clements appear to have been conflated. No source found for a 24 November Eastern date. | `corrected` → `en: "Historically December 4 (no longer on the Roman calendar); still December 4 among the Copts, Ethiopians and Anglicans"` / `vi: "Trước đây là 4 tháng 12 (không còn trong lịch Rôma); vẫn là 4 tháng 12 nơi các Giáo hội Cốp-tô, Êthiôpia và Anh giáo"` | [1913 CE](https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/04045a.htm), [New World Encyclopedia](https://www.newworldencyclopedia.org/entry/Clement_of_Alexandria), [Episcopal Church, Clement of Alexandria](https://www.episcopalchurch.org/glossary/clement-of-alexandria/) |

### 7. tertullian — Tertullian

**Checked:** Surface A — the headline `quote` traced to the Latin of *Apologeticum* 50,13 and to the ANF
(Peter Holmes) English; both `more-quotes` quotations traced to *De Carne Christi* 5 and *Apology* 18 in
full text; `dates`, `role`, and the Montanism narrative in `life[0]` / `sections.martyrdom` /
`sections.later-influence` checked against Altaner–Graef's *Patrology*, Barnes, Powell (*Vigiliae
Christianae* 29, 1975) and the tertullian.org archive; the surviving-works count checked against two
independent sources; all four `works[]` titles confirmed genuine; `ccc_refs` is empty as expected; VI
name and work titles grepped across the site's own files and checked against Vietnamese Catholic
sources; both legacy combined datasets compared row by row.
Surface B — the *De Praescriptione* ch. 19 block quote collated word by word against the ANF text at the
very URL the frontmatter cites; the `sources:` URL fetched and confirmed; CCC §113, §83 and §2131 read at
vatican.va; the Tertullian/Origen/Eusebius framing checked against *De Idololatria*, *Contra Celsum* VII
and Eusebius *HE* 7.18.

#### Surface A — Giáo Phụ entry (`content/giao-phu/tertullian.json`)

| Field | Claim as written | What the sources say | Verdict | Sources |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `facts[]` → "Tác phẩm còn lại" / "Surviving works" | "4 tác phẩm" / "4 works" | **Thirty-one** genuine treatises survive. tertullian.org: "Tertullian has left us 31 extant treatises, all in Latin"; readfirst: "Thirty-one are extant". Altaner–Graef, *Patrology*: "Thirty-one treatises have survived in all." (Plus 5 spurious and at least 12 lost.) The `works[]` array is a legitimate 4-item *selection* — it mirrors `key_writings` in the legacy file — but the `facts` row states it as the surviving total. **This is the single worst error in the entry.** Propose vi: `"31 khảo luận còn lại (cùng khoảng 5 tác phẩm ngụy tác và ít nhất 12 tác phẩm đã thất lạc)"`, en: `"31 surviving treatises (plus c. 5 spurious and at least 12 lost)"` | `corrected` | [tertullian.org/works.htm](https://www.tertullian.org/works.htm), [readfirst](https://www.tertullian.org/readfirst.htm), [Altaner–Graef, Patrology, pp. 166–182](https://www.tertullian.org/articles/altaner_patrology_eng.htm) |
| `quote.vi` / `quote.en`, source "Apologeticus, 50" | "Máu các vị tử đạo là hạt giống của Giáo hội." / "The blood of the martyrs is the seed of the Church." | The Latin is **"semen est sanguis Christianorum"** (*Apologeticum* 50,13) — "the blood of **Christians** is seed". ANF renders it exactly so: *"the blood of Christians is seed."* Neither "of the martyrs" nor "of the Church" is in Tertullian; the familiar English is a liberal, centuries-old paraphrase that conflates 50,13 with his remarks on martyrs elsewhere. The chapter number (50) is right. Propose either keeping the famous form but marking it as the traditional paraphrase, or a faithful rendering — vi: `"Chúng tôi càng đông thêm mỗi lần các ông gặt chúng tôi: máu người Kitô hữu là hạt giống."`, en: `"We multiply whenever you mow us down: the blood of Christians is seed."`, source vi `"Apologeticus, 50,13"` / en `"Apologeticus 50.13"` | `contested` | [ANF, Apology 50 (CCEL)](https://www.ccel.org/ccel/schaff/anf03.iv.iii.l.html), [Loeb, Tertullian *Apology*](https://www.loebclassics.com/view/tertullian-apology/1931/pb_LCL250.227.xml), [tertullian.org/works/apologeticum.htm](https://tertullian.org/works/apologeticum.htm) |
| `dates.display` (with `dates.died` = 220) | "c. 155 — 220" | The entry's own `facts[]` row says "sau năm 220 (không rõ năm mất chính xác)" / "after 220 AD" — `display` drops the "after" and reads as a death **in** 220. Altaner–Graef: "He died an old man at Carthage, **after 220**." Propose display vi/en: `"c. 155 — sau năm 220"` / `"c. 155 — after 220"` (and leave `died: 220` as the sort key with a note, since the field is an integer) | `corrected` | [Altaner–Graef](https://www.tertullian.org/articles/altaner_patrology_eng.htm), [Tertullian.org readfirst](https://www.tertullian.org/readfirst.htm) |
| `dates.born` = 155 / `facts[]` "khoảng năm 155" | "c. 155 AD" | Genuinely unsettled. Altaner–Graef: "born at Carthage **c. 160**". Barnes: "nothing forbids the hypothesis that Tertullian was born **c. 170**", and the whole Jerome-derived biography (centurion's son, jurist, priest) is the part Barnes dismantled. "c. 155" sits at the earliest edge of a c. 155–170 spread and is stated without a range. Propose vi: `"khoảng năm 155–170 (các học giả bất đồng)"`, en: `"c. 155–170 (scholars disagree)"` | `contested` | [Altaner–Graef](https://www.tertullian.org/articles/altaner_patrology_eng.htm), [Barnes, *Tertullian: A Historical and Literary Study* (via DACB summary)](https://dacb.org/stories/tunisia/tertullian/) |
| `life[0]`, and repeated in `sections.martyrdom` and `sections.later-influence` | "Khoảng năm 207, ngài rời khỏi Giáo hội chính thống để gia nhập phái Montanô" / "Left the mainstream Church for Montanism around 207"; "rất có thể ngài đã qua đời hoàn toàn ngoài sự hiệp thông với Giáo Hội"; "qua đời ngoài sự hiệp thông với Giáo Hội" | **The most contested claim in the entry, and it is stated three times as settled fact.** The traditional account (Jerome, Augustine) is exactly what modern scholarship has challenged. Powell (*Vigiliae Christianae* 29, 1975) showed there is no evidence of schism in Tertullian's own writings — he always speaks of himself as inside the church; the *disiunctio* of *Adv. Praxean* is "the refutation of any theory of a formal schism from the African Church", and the group was at most an *ecclesiola in ecclesia*. Rankin (1995) corroborates; the tertullian.org archive states plainly: "It is unclear whether this involved actually leaving the church, but his later works are avowedly Montanist." Note that the 207 *date* itself has support — Altaner–Graef: "In 207 at the latest, he broke with the Church" — so the dispute is over **whether there was a formal break**, not over the chronology. Propose hedging all three places, e.g. vi: `"Từ khoảng năm 207 ngài ngày càng gắn bó với phong trào Montanô (\"Lời Tiên Tri Mới\"); truyền thống sau này (Thánh Giêrônimô, Thánh Augustinô) nói ngài đã ly khai khỏi Giáo Hội, nhưng nhiều học giả hiện đại cho rằng chính các tác phẩm của ngài không cho thấy một cuộc ly giáo chính thức nào."` | `contested` | [Powell, *Tertullianists and Cataphrygians*, VC 29 (1975)](https://www.tertullian.org/articles/powell_tertullianists.htm), [tertullian.org readfirst](https://www.tertullian.org/readfirst.htm), [Altaner–Graef](https://www.tertullian.org/articles/altaner_patrology_eng.htm) |
| `sections.martyrdom` | "các nguồn chỉ đồng ý rằng ngài qua đời tại Carthage vào một thời điểm nào đó sau năm 220, vì nguyên nhân tự nhiên" / "sources agree only that he died in Carthage sometime after 220 AD, of natural causes" | "Sources agree" overstates what exists. There is **no ancient notice of his death at all**; Jerome (*De viris illustribus* 53) reports only that he is said to have lived to a decrepit old age. "At Carthage, after 220" is a modern scholarly reconstruction (Altaner–Graef states it, without an ancient citation), and "of natural causes" is an inference from the absence of any martyrdom tradition, not an attested fact. Propose vi: `"không có nguồn cổ nào ghi lại cái chết của ngài; thánh Giêrônimô chỉ nói ngài sống đến tuổi rất già, và các học giả hiện đại đặt cái chết của ngài tại Carthage vào một thời điểm sau năm 220"` | `contested` | [Altaner–Graef](https://www.tertullian.org/articles/altaner_patrology_eng.htm), [Tertullian.org readfirst](https://www.tertullian.org/readfirst.htm) |
| `sections.martyrdom` (same paragraph) | the "Tertullianist" splinter group, "theo truyền thống sau này (trong đó có Thánh Augustinô)" | The hedge ("theo truyền thống sau này") is **correct and should be kept** — but the conclusion drawn from it ("rất có thể ngài đã qua đời hoàn toàn ngoài sự hiệp thông") is firmer than the evidence. Augustine, *De Haeresibus* 86, is the source; Powell argues the *Tertullianistae* were simply African Montanists, not a sect Tertullian founded, and Barnes has suggested the whole idea was an inference Augustine (and Praedestinatus) drew from the name. Propose keeping the attribution and softening the conclusion | `contested` | [Powell, VC 29 (1975)](https://www.tertullian.org/articles/powell_tertullianists.htm), [Augustine, *De haeresibus* 86 (text + notes)](https://www.tertullian.org/tertullianistae/de_haeresibus.htm) |
| `role.vi` / `role.en` | "Theo truyền thống được coi là linh mục tại Carthage, dù **một số học giả** nghi ngờ điều này" / "though **some scholars** doubt formal ordination" | The hedge is on the wrong side of the consensus. Altaner–Graef: "It is **very improbable** that he was a priest, as Jerome asserts." Jerome is the sole source and is the part of the biography modern work has rejected. Propose vi: `"Thánh Giêrônimô gọi ngài là linh mục tại Carthage, nhưng phần lớn học giả hiện đại cho rằng điều này rất khó xảy ra"`, en: `"Called a priest of Carthage by Jerome, but most modern scholars think this very improbable"` | `corrected` | [Altaner–Graef](https://www.tertullian.org/articles/altaner_patrology_eng.htm), [Tertullian.org readfirst](https://www.tertullian.org/readfirst.htm) |
| `sections.more-quotes` — the *De Carne Christi* quotation | "điều đó **[hoàn toàn]** đáng tin" / "it is **[utterly]** credible" | The brackets misfire. The Latin is *"Et mortuus est dei filius: **prorsus** credibile est, quia ineptum est"* — *prorsus* ("utterly") **is in the text**, so bracketing it presents part of Tertullian's own sentence as an editorial insertion. Propose simply removing the square brackets. Separately: the entry renders *ineptum* as "unfitting" / "không hợp lẽ", which is defensible and arguably better than ANF, but ANF — the translation on the two sites this very entry recommends in `further-reading` — reads *"it is by all means to be believed, because it is **absurd**."* Since the section's whole point is that "absurd" is not Tertullian's word, naming the edition would prevent a reader who follows the link from thinking the entry got it wrong | `corrected` | [ANF, *On the Flesh of Christ* 5 (New Advent)](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/0315.htm), [tertullian.org, *De carne Christi*](https://www.tertullian.org/works/de_carne_christi.htm), [Sider, *Credo Quia Absurdum?*, Classical World 73 (1980)](https://www.tertullian.org/articles/sider_credo.htm) |
| `sections.more-quotes` — "credo quia absurdum" | "ngài chưa bao giờ thực sự viết những lời đó" / "he never actually wrote those words" | **Genuinely correct and should not be "fixed" later.** The formula is a post-Tertullian (early-modern/Enlightenment) distortion of *De Carne Christi* 5; the nearest real phrases are *prorsus credibile est, quia ineptum est* and *certum est, quia impossibile*. Flagging so a future editor does not "restore" the popular attribution | `confirmed` | [Sider, Classical World 73 (1980)](https://www.tertullian.org/articles/sider_credo.htm), [tertullian.org, *De carne Christi*](https://www.tertullian.org/works/de_carne_christi.htm) |
| `sections.more-quotes` — "Fiunt, non nascuntur, Christiani" | quoted "từ Apologeticus", no chapter; framed as his answer to Romans who assumed Christian identity was ethnic/hereditary | **Correct, including the framing** — and worth a chapter number. ANF, *Apology* **18**: *"Once these things were with us, too, the theme of ridicule. We are of your stock and nature: men are made, not born, Christians."* The clause "We are of your stock and nature" is exactly the hereditary-identity point the entry describes. Propose adding "18" to the citation | `confirmed` | [ANF, *Apology* 18 (New Advent)](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/0301.htm) |
| `sections.theology` vs `works[]` (same file) | `theology` says "Tác phẩm **Bác Bỏ Lạc Giáo** (Prescription Against Heretics)"; `works[]` says "**Quy Định Chống Lạc Giáo**" | Two different Vietnamese titles for the same work inside one file. `church-fathers-vi.json` uses "Quy Định Chống Lạc Giáo (De Praescriptione Haereticorum)", and Vietnamese Catholic usage runs the same way — "Những Qui Định Chống Lại Lạc Giáo". Propose standardising on **"Quy Định Chống Lạc Giáo"** everywhere (see Surface B, which introduces a *third* form) | `corrected` | [dcvphanxicoxavie.com, *Sự Thành Hình Triết Học Kitô Giáo*](https://dcvphanxicoxavie.com/vn/Triet-Hoc/Su-Thanh-Hinh-Triet-Hoc-Kito-Giao.html), site's own `content/giao-phu/church-fathers-vi.json:185` |
| `life[1].vi` only | "từ vựng thần học Latinh mà Kitô giáo Tây phương sẽ sử dụng **trong 1.500 năm sau đó**" | The figure appears only in the VI text (the EN has no time span) and contradicts the file's own `theology` section, which says the vocabulary became the language "every subsequent Latin council and theologian used" — i.e. it is still in use, not superseded after 1,500 years. **Cross-file consistency** — `church-fathers.json` (id 7) and `church-fathers-vi.json` (id 7) were compared
row by row and **contain no contradictions** with the per-slug file: same dates, same `role` hedge, same
paraphrased `key_quote` ("Apologeticus, 50"), same "left the mainstream Church for Montanism around 207",
`feast_day: null`, `canonized: false`, `doctor_of_the_church: false`. Two consequences for the owner:
(a) the quote fix and the Montanism hedge must be applied in **all three** files, not just the per-slug
one; (b) neither combined file carries the "4 surviving works" claim — they list a 4-item `key_writings`
selection — which is where that error looks to have come from.

**Vietnamese terminology** — `Tertulianô` is used consistently across `tertullian.json`,
`church-fathers-vi.json` and both Giải Đáp files, so there is no internal inconsistency to fix. The site's
own Catechism (`content/content.json`) is no help: it cites him twice (ids 228, 1015) but the Vietnamese
translation drops the attribution both times, leaving only the English "Tertullian". Vietnamese Catholic
sources use several forms (`Tertullianô`, `Te-tu-li-a-nô`, and the French-derived `Turtullien` at
simonhoadalat.com), so `Tertulianô` is a plausible attested-family spelling — but I could not confirm it
against a single authoritative Vietnamese Catholic source within budget. No correction proposed.

`ccc_refs` — empty, as expected. `era: "ante-nicene"` — correct and uncontroversial. `works[]` — all four
named works exist and are genuinely Tertullian's; none are spuria.

#### Surface B — Giải Đáp citations (HAND-OFF TO SESSION 3 — do not apply)

**Headline: the capstone Sola Scriptura quotation is CLEAN.** I collated the Vietnamese of the block
quote word by word against the ANF text of *De Praescriptione Haereticorum* ch. 19 at the exact URL the
frontmatter cites, and it holds: the ellipsis marks a real omission of one sentence and nothing else is
dropped, nothing unbracketed has been added, the bracketed gloss is legitimate, the chapter number is
right, the URL is the right work, and **both** cited Catechism paragraphs say what the Q&A says they say.
The four findings below are a mistitled work, a soft date, an ordering claim, and the weak third leg of a
different Q&A — none of them touch the argument itself.

Origen: *Contra Celsum* VII.62–66, where Celsus' charge that Christians "cannot tolerate temples, altars, or images" is one **Origen does not deny** — so for Origen the sentence *understates*: he defends an aniconic cult, not merely wariness of idols, and a reader who checks will find more than the sentence promises. **Eusebius:** the only real basis for calling him an opponent of images is the *Letter to Constantia*, which survives **only** through the iconoclast synod of Hieria (754) florilegium as re-quoted in the acts of Nicaea II (787) in order to be condemned, and whose authenticity is disputed. Meanwhile Eusebius' own *Historia Ecclesiastica* 7.18 is a positive early witness: *"we have learned also that the likenesses of his apostles Paul and Peter, and of Christ himself, are preserved in paintings, the ancients being accustomed…"* — which supports **this Q&A's own thesis** far better than Eusebius supports the objection | `contested` | Drop Eusebius from the wariness list and put him on the other side, e.g.: `Chứng từ như của các ông Tertulianô hay Ôrigênê cho thấy Giáo Hội luôn cảnh giác với ngẫu tượng — nhưng chính ông Êusêbiô, ở thế kỷ 4, lại ghi nhận rằng "hình ảnh các Tông đồ Phaolô và Phêrô, và cả hình ảnh chính Đức Kitô, được lưu giữ trong các bức tranh" từ thời xưa (Lịch sử Giáo Hội VII,18) — nhưng dòng chảy chung…` | [Tertullian, *On Idolatry* (New Advent)](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/0302.htm), [Eusebius, *Church History* VII.18 (New Advent)](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/250107.htm), [Mango's translation of the *Letter to Constantia* + transmission history](https://www.roger-pearse.com/weblog/2021/06/14/eusebius-letter-to-constantia-an-english-translation-by-cyril-mango/), [Origen, *Contra Celsum* VII (New Advent)](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/04167.htm) |

Not a factual error — a gap. `refs_ccc: [2131]` **is verified**: CCC §2131 reads *"Basing itself on the mystery of the incarnate Word, the seventh ecumenical council at Nicaea (787) justified against the iconoclasts the veneration of icons — of Christ, but also of the Mother of God, the angels, and all the saints"*, which the Vietnamese block quote at line 29 matches exactly | `unverifiable` (gap, not error) | add a `sources:` block citing *De Idololatria*, *Contra Celsum* VII, Eusebius *HE* VII.18 and Nicaea II | [CCC 2131, vatican.va](https://www.vatican.va/content/catechism/en/part_three/section_two/chapter_one/article_1/iv_you_shall_not_make_for_yourself_a_graven_image___.html) |

**Not checked:**
- **Dating of the individual `works[]`.** The `latin` and `date` fields are all empty strings, so nothing
  is asserted and nothing can be wrong — but the audit angle "which works belong to the Montanist period"
  is therefore only half answered. *Apologeticus* (197), *De Praescriptione* (c. 203) and *De Baptismo*
  (c. 198–200) are pre-Montanist; **Adversus Praxean is generally placed in his Montanist period
  (c. 210–213)**, which is exactly the work the entry leans on for *Trinitas / persona / substantia*. The
  `theology` section already anticipates this ("his positions on the Trinity and Christology were not
  among the things Montanism changed"), but **I found only one source for the Adversus Praxean dating this
  session and could not triangulate it** — flagging rather than asserting.
- The claim that Tertullian *coined* `persona` and `substantia` as against *repurposing* existing Latin
  legal/philosophical vocabulary. The entry hedges correctly ("đặt ra hoặc tái sử dụng" / "coined or
  repurposed") and *Trinitas* as a first Christian Latin use is well attested, so I did not spend budget
  splitting the other two.
- `sections.context` and `sections.later-influence` were read but their softer historical framing (Latin
  as a late working language of Christian thought; Augustine engaging Tertullian directly; "sporadic
  persecution" in North Africa) was not individually source-checked.
- `portrait` — the file labels it "Chân dung truyền thống" / "Traditional portrait", which is the right
  hedge (no contemporary likeness of Tertullian exists); the Wikimedia PD status itself was not verified.
- Vietnamese spelling `Tertulianô` — see the note above; internally consistent, but not confirmed against
  an authoritative Vietnamese Catholic source.

**Keep for `sources` retrofit:** [tertullian.org — complete works, Latin texts and scholarly articles](https://www.tertullian.org/) · [Altaner–Graef, *Patrology*, pp. 166–182 (Tertullian)](https://www.tertullian.org/articles/altaner_patrology_eng.htm) · [ANF, *The Prescription Against Heretics* (New Advent)](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/0311.htm)

### 8. hippolytus-of-rome — Hippolytus of Rome

| Field | Claim as written | What the sources say | Verdict | Sources |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `quote.en` wording | "We give thanks to you, God, through your beloved Child Jesus Christ, whom in the last times you sent to us as savior and redeemer and angel of your will." | The wording is a faithful rendering of the Latin Verona Palimpsest text of the *Traditio apostolica* anaphora: *"...quem in ultimis temporibus misisti nobis salvatorem et redemptorem et angelum voluntatis tuae."* "Child" correctly renders Lat. *puer* / Gk. *pais*. No wording problem. | `confirmed` (wording only — see next row for the attribution) | [Verona Palimpsest (only Latin witness, c. 494)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Verona_Palimpsest), [Anaphora of the Apostolic Tradition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anaphora_of_the_Apostolic_Tradition) |
| `quote.source` (vi/en) | "Truyền Thống Tông Đồ, lời nguyện Thánh Thể (nền tảng của Kinh Nguyện Thánh Thể II hiện nay)" / "Apostolic Tradition, Eucharistic prayer (basis of modern Eucharistic Prayer II)" — presented flatly as Hippolytus's own words | Bradshaw–Johnson–Phillips (Hermeneia, Fortress 2002) hold the *Apostolic Tradition* is "neither the work of Hippolytus nor of any other individual" but "a composite document made up of a number of layers and strands of diverse provenance and compiled over a period of time," with the anaphora reaching final form as late as the mid-4th c. and possibly not Roman at all. The entry's own `later-influence` section says exactly this — so the headline `quote` contradicts the entry's own body. | `contested` → hedge the source, e.g. en: "*Apostolic Tradition*, Eucharistic prayer — a text traditionally ascribed to Hippolytus, though modern scholarship treats the ascription as doubtful (see 'Later influence'); it inspired Eucharistic Prayer II"; vi: "Truyền Thống Tông Đồ, lời nguyện Thánh Thể — văn bản theo truyền thống được gán cho Hippôlytô, nhưng học thuật hiện đại nghi ngờ sự quy gán này (xem mục 'Ảnh hưởng về sau'); là nguồn cảm hứng cho Kinh Nguyện Thánh Thể II" | [Bradshaw/Johnson/Phillips, *The Apostolic Tradition: A Commentary* (Hermeneia)](https://www.fortresspress.com/store/product/9780800660468/The-Apostolic-Tradition), [Baldovin, "Hippolytus and the Apostolic Tradition: Recent Research and Commentary," *Theological Studies* 64 (2003)](https://theologicalstudies.net/wp-content/uploads/2022/08/64.3.3.pdf) |
| `sections[more-quotes]` — the quotation | "for they thought that the end was already come... Let us not, then, brethren, as though we were now living in the end of the world, be puffed up." | **Not traceable.** I searched (i) the full text of T. C. Schmidt's translation of the *Commentary on Daniel* — the only complete English translation, and the Commentary survives complete only in Old Slavonic + Greek fragments — and (ii) the whole of *ANF* vol. 5 (all Hippolytus + Cyprian + Novatian, 4.3 MB plain text). Neither contains "the end was already come", "living in the end of the world", or any "puffed up" sentence resembling this; every "puffed up" in Schmidt is about Nebuchadnezzar/Belshazzar/the Antichrist. | `unverifiable` → remove the quotation, or replace it with the real text (see next row) and mark any retained wording `[cần kiểm chứng]` | [Schmidt, *Hippolytus of Rome's Commentary on Daniel* (Gorgias, 2nd ed.) full text](https://ebin.pub/hippolytus-of-romes-commentary-on-daniel-9781463244378.html), [ANF vol. 5 full text (CCEL)](https://www.ccel.org/ccel/s/schaff/anf05/cache/anf05.txt), [Gorgias Press ed.](https://www.gorgiaspress.com/hippolytus-of-romes-commentary-on-daniel) |
| `sections[more-quotes]` — what the passage is | "a striking pastoral aside where he pictures a bishop staying up through the night with an anxious congregation awaiting what they feared was the end of the world" | The passage (*Comm. Dan.* 4.18–4.19) is not an imagined scene and the leader is not a sympathetic figure — Hippolytus reports **two recent real incidents** and condemns both leaders. Syria (4.18.2–3): a church leader "having not laboriously read the godly Scriptures... was deceived and he himself also deceived others," and "persuaded many of the brothers, with [their] wives and children, to go out into the desert in order to meet with Christ", who were nearly killed as robbers by the governor. Pontus (4.19.1–7): a leader "believing dreams which he saw" announced "after one year the judgment will happen"; the hearers "with weeping and lamenting begged the Lord night and day"; they let "their lands and fields to be desolate", sold their possessions, and a year later "he himself was shamed as a deceiver... They, who vainly sold their possessions, were found later begging." Hippolytus's verdict (4.20.1): "These things happen to ignorant and simple men, as many as do not attend precisely to the Scriptures." So the "night" vigil belongs to the *congregation*, and the bishop is the cause of the panic, not the pastor calming it. | `corrected` → rewrite around the real text, e.g. en: "…reports two recent panics — in Syria a church leader 'persuaded many of the brothers, with [their] wives and children, to go out into the desert in order to meet with Christ', and in Pontus another, 'believing dreams which he saw', announced that 'after one year the judgment will happen', so that his people 'with weeping and lamenting begged the Lord night and day' and let 'their lands and fields to be desolate'. A year later 'he himself was shamed as a deceiver'." (vi to match) | [Schmidt trans., *Comm. Dan.* 4.18–4.20 (full text)](https://ebin.pub/hippolytus-of-romes-commentary-on-daniel-9781463244378.html) |

| `sections[martyrdom]` | "**both men resigned their claims** to the Roman bishopric before or during their imprisonment **specifically to heal the schism**" / "cả hai người đều từ bỏ tuyên bố của mình về ngai tòa giám mục Rôma trước hoặc trong thời gian bị giam cầm, chính là để hàn gắn cuộc ly giáo" | Only **Pontian's** abdication is documented — the Liberian Catalogue dates it precisely to **28 September 235** ("*IIII kal. Octob.*"), making him the first pope to resign. **No source records Hippolytus resigning anything.** Even the 1913 Catholic Encyclopedia only infers it: "Shortly before this or soon afterwards Hippolytus... became reconciled to the Roman Church, and with this the schism he had caused came to an end." The reconciliation is a reasonable inference from Pope Fabian's later translation of both bodies and from Hippolytus's entry in the Roman *Depositio Martyrum* — it is not attested testimony. **In fairness: Britannica does assert it** ("Before dying as martyrs, both resigned to allow for a successor, St. Anterus (235–236), thus ending the schism"), and the Vietnamese hagiography at TGP Sài Gòn says Hippolytus "no longer demanded to be pope and advised his followers to obey the legitimate Pontiff" — so this is standard reference-work narrative, but it rests on inference from the Liberian Catalogue + Depositio, not on a source that says it. | `contested` (the mutual-resignation detail) → keep the reconciliation but drop the invented mechanism, e.g. en: "Pontian abdicated on 28 September 235 so Rome could elect a successor; the sources do not say what Hippolytus did, but the Roman church soon after honoured him as a martyr and Pope Fabian had both bodies brought home, from which the tradition of his reconciliation is drawn."; vi to match | [1913 CE, "Pope St. Pontian"](https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/12229b.htm), [Liberian Catalogue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberian_Catalogue) |

And the *shared feast* is not ancient continuity: before 1969 Pontian was kept on **19 November** and 13 August belonged to a Hippolytus fused with the legend of St Lawrence's jailer; the 1969 reform put them together again on the ancient date. | `facts[Surviving works]` | "3 tác phẩm" / "3 works" | **Wrong on any reading, and it is not a researched figure** — it is simply `works.length` of this entry's own three-item `works[]` array (the parent audit confirms this defect in all 30 entries). Far more than three pieces of the Hippolytan corpus survive: *On Christ and Antichrist* (complete in Greek, c. 202), the *Commentary on Daniel* (complete, via Old Slavonic), the *Commentary on the Song of Songs* (the earliest surviving Christian commentary on the Song, preserved in two Georgian MSS, a Greek epitome, a Paleo-Slavonic florilegium and Armenian/Syriac fragments), *Against Noetus*, the *Chronicon*, *On the Blessings of Isaac, Jacob and Moses*, the *Refutation of All Heresies* (Books IV–X in a single 14th-c. Athos codex; Book I under Origen's name), the *Apostolic Tradition* (indirectly, via the Verona Latin and Eastern church orders), plus many exegetical fragments. But no honest integer exists, because the corpus is exactly what the one-author/two-author dispute is about. | `corrected` → replace the number with a hedge, e.g. en: "A sizeable corpus survives — but how much of it is by one 'Hippolytus' is disputed"; vi: "Một khối tác phẩm đáng kể còn lại — nhưng bao nhiêu trong số đó thuộc về một 'Hippôlytô' duy nhất thì vẫn còn tranh cãi" | [ANF vol. 5, extant works & fragments of Hippolytus](https://www.ccel.org/ccel/s/schaff/anf05/cache/anf05.txt), [Yancy Smith, *The Mystery of Anointing: Hippolytus' Commentary on the Song of Songs* (Gorgias)](https://www.amazon.com/Mystery-Anointing-Hippolytus-Translations-Comprehensive/dp/1463202180), [Refutation of All Heresies — MS transmission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Refutation_of_All_Heresies) |

| `works[]` — authorship, all three | Three works listed flatly as his, with no `works_note` (`works_note` is `""` in both languages) | All three are exactly the disputed ones. (a) *Apostolic Tradition* — ascription to Hippolytus now widely doubted (Bradshaw–Johnson–Phillips, above). (b) *Refutation of All Heresies* — Nautin's division, accepted by Simonetti, Brent and Cerrato, assigns the *Refutatio*, *De pascha* and *Chronicon* to a **Roman** author and the exegetical commentaries to a **different** author; the BMCR review notes this division "has since attained scholarly support." (c) *Commentary on Daniel* — Cerrato argues the commentator worked in **western Asia Minor**, not Rome. So the site's three headline works may well be by two or three different people. Given the entry has a `works_note` field sitting empty, this is the natural place to say so. | `contested` → fill `works_note`, e.g. en: "The 'Hippolytan' corpus is disputed. | `sections[later-influence]` — the statue | "the surviving **lower half** of the statue is now recognized as female... before being creatively reidentified as Hippolytus **centuries later**" | Substantially right, but imprecise on two points. (a) The identification as Hippolytus was made in **1551 by Pirro Ligorio**, who found the badly damaged statue between the Via Nomentana and the Via Tiburtina and had the head, upper torso and arms **newly carved**; only the lower portion and the inscribed chair are ancient. The female reading rests on the single pre-restoration drawing and on the "feminine undergarments... long frills flowing" below the toga — it is the leading view, not a settled recognition, and is still being argued (Louvain Studies 44 [2021]; *Vatican Library Review* 2 [2023]). (b) The ancient reuse is separate from Ligorio's: the chair inscriptions (works list + a paschal table computing past and future Easters) were cut c. **222 AD** onto an already-existing statue. Guarducci proposed Themista of Lampsacus; **Brent rejected that identification** and argued instead that the community reworked it into a figure of *Sophia* — so "possibly Themista" should stay flagged as one hypothesis among several. Statue moved to the Vatican in **1959**. | `contested` → tighten, e.g. en: "…only the lower part and the inscribed chair are ancient — Pirro Ligorio, who unearthed the statue in 1551, had the head and torso newly carved and named the result Hippolytus. The chair inscriptions — a works list and a table for calculating Easter — were cut around 222 and are genuine ancient evidence." | [Biblical Archaeology Society, "A Mysterious Figure"](https://library.biblicalarchaeology.org/sidebar/a-mysterious-figure/), [Handl, "A Heavily Bearded Philosopher in Women's Underwear," *Louvain Studies* 44 (2021) 340–364](https://andras.handl.hu/resources/hippolytus-bibliography/), ["The Vatican Library's 'Statue of Saint Hippolytus' Revisited," *Vatican Library Review* 2 (2023) 103–117](https://brill.com/view/journals/vlr/2/1/article-p103_6.xml) |

| `sections[context]` — Callixtus | "Callixtus, a former slave who had himself **once been convicted of a financial crime**" / "Callixtô, một cựu nô lệ **từng bị kết án về một tội tài chính**" | Two problems. (a) The loss of the money was **not a criminal conviction**: per the *Philosophumena*, Carpophorus entrusted him with money, he ran a bank in which "brethren and widows lodged money, all of which Callistus lost," fled, was caught at Portus and "consigned to the punishment reserved for slaves, the *pistrinum*, or hand-mill" — a master's domestic punishment. His actual **judicial sentence, to the Sardinian mines, came from the prefect Fuscianus after a disturbance in a synagogue**, not from the finances. (b) The **only source is Hippolytus himself** — the *Refutation* IX, written by the man the entry is profiling. The 1913 CE flags this ("their sources were bitter enemies"; Döllinger and De Rossi contested the details, and Hippolytus does not actually say Callixtus lost the deposits through his own fault). | `facts[Canonized]` | "Có" / "Yes" | True in substance but the field is anachronistic: there was no canonization process in the 3rd century — Hippolytus is a **pre-Congregation** saint by ancient cult, entered under 13 August in the Roman *Depositio Martyrum* of 354. The combined-file nickname ("the only antipope venerated as a saint") is also only true *since 1947*: Antipope Felix II stood in the Roman Martyrology under 29 July until Pius XII listed him as an antipope in the *Annuario Pontificio* in 1947 and he was removed. | `contested` (mild) → say "Pre-Congregation (tôn kính từ xa xưa)" rather than a bare "Yes"; if the "only antipope venerated as a saint" line is kept anywhere, add "today" | [New Liturgical Movement, "The Strange Case of the Antipope Venerated as a Saint"](https://www.newliturgicalmovement.org/2020/07/the-strange-case-of-antipope-venerated.html), [Britannica, *Felix (II)*](https://www.britannica.com/biography/Felix-II) |
| `sections[context]` — schism length | "a schism that lasted **through three legitimate papacies**" / "kéo dài qua ba triều đại giáo hoàng hợp pháp" | Correct on the arithmetic: Callixtus I (217–222), Urban I (222–230), Pontian (230–235) — three. Britannica states it the same way. Keep. | `confirmed` (do not "fix" to two or four) | [Britannica, *Saint Hippolytus of Rome*](https://www.britannica.com/biography/Saint-Hippolytus-of-Rome) |
| `sections[context]` — Greek | "one of the last major Roman theologians to write extensively in Greek" | Holds up, and the entry's hedge ("one of the last") is better than the usual flat claim. Standard statements are stronger: he is often called "the last Roman theologian to write in the language of the New Testament", and the 1913 CE explains the consequence — he "wrote in Greek", so "the Romans lost interest in his writings" once Greek died out in Rome. | `confirmed` | [1913 CE, "St. Hippolytus of Rome"](https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/07360c.htm), [Crossroads Initiative, "St. Hippolytus of Rome"](https://www.crossroadsinitiative.com/media/articles/st-hippolytus-of-rome/) |
| `sections[theology]` | "Hippolytus's own Logos theology described the Son as coming into distinct personal existence at a particular point, arguably subordinate to the Father in a way later Nicene theology would not accept either" | Unflattering but accurate, and it should be protected from a well-meaning later "softening": the standard account is that Hippolytus "stood uncompromisingly for a real difference between the Son (Logos) and the Father, but represented the Son as a Divine Person almost completely separate from God (Ditheism) and at the same time altogether subordinate to the Father (Subordinationism)". His own defence is in *Contra Noetum*: "when I speak of 'another', I do not mean two Gods, but as it were light from light, water from its source, a ray from the sun." The section's naming of Modalism = Monarchianism = Sabellianism and the opponents (Noetus, Epigonus, Cleomenes, Sabellius) also checks out. | `confirmed` (notable) | [1913 CE, "St. Hippolytus of Rome"](https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/07360c.htm), [Catholic Answers Encyclopedia, "Hippolytus, Saints"](https://www.catholic.com/encyclopedia/hippolytus-saints) |
| `sections[further-reading]` | "Alistair Stewart-Sykes, trans., *On the Apostolic Tradition* (Popular Patristics Series, St. Vladimir's Seminary Press, 2001)"; "Allen Brent, *Hippolytus and the Roman Church in the Third Century* (Brill, 1995)" | Both bibliographic entries are real and correctly described. Brent's full subtitle is *Communities in Tension before the Emergence of a Monarch-Bishop* (Supplements to Vigiliae Christianae 31, Brill 1995) — worth adding, because the subtitle is itself the argument the entry is citing it for. | `confirmed` | [Brent (Brill 1995), Internet Archive](https://archive.org/details/hippolytusromanc0000bren), [WorldCat record](https://search.worldcat.org/title/832674709) |

**Checked:** `quote` traced word-for-word to the Latin Verona Palimpsest text of the *Traditio apostolica* anaphora, and its ascription checked against the Bradshaw–Johnson–Phillips Hermeneia commentary and Baldovin's *Theological Studies* survey; the `more-quotes` quotation hunted through the complete text of Schmidt's translation of the *Commentary on Daniel* and the full 4.3 MB plain text of *ANF* vol. 5 (both downloaded and grepped, not skimmed) and **not found**; `dates` against Britannica and the 1913 CE; feast day against the current General Roman Calendar (Catholic Culture), the *Depositio Martyrum* of 354 and the Byzantine synaxarion (OCA/Sanidopoulos); the antipope/schism/martyrdom narrative against the Liberian Catalogue, the 1913 CE "Pope St. Pontian" and "Pope Callistus I", Britannica, and Brent's monograph; the one-vs-two-Hippolytus question and the *Refutatio*'s authorship via the BMCR review of Cerrato's *Hippolytus between East and West*; the statue via the Biblical Archaeology Society, Handl's *Louvain Studies* 44 (2021) study and the *Vatican Library Review* 2 (2023) article; all three `works[]` for authorship and one for language labelling; the Vietnamese names against TGP Sài Gòn, Giáo phận Kon Tum and Giáo phận Vĩnh Long (never vi.wikipedia) plus a grep of the site's own `content/`; and both combined datasets `church-fathers.json` / `church-fathers-vi.json` (id 8) for contradictions. `era: "ante-nicene"` and `role`'s "Priest/bishop in Rome" are right for a figure active c. 200–235; `ccc_refs` is **empty**, so nothing to verify there.

**Not checked:** (1) The **`portrait`** block — I did not verify that `/images/church-fathers/hippolytus-of-rome.jpg` is in fact public domain or that "Traditional portrait" describes it; note that if the image is the Vatican statue, the caption "Chân dung truyền thống / Traditional portrait" is undercut by this entry's own `later-influence` section. (2) The **Refutation's doxographical content** — I verified its authorship debate and manuscript history but not the accuracy of anything the entry implies about what it contains. (3) **Alistair Stewart-Sykes's actual position** in the 2001 introduction (the entry says it covers the authorship debate; I confirmed the book exists and is correctly cited, not what it argues). (4) Whether **`works_note` being empty** is a site-wide pattern or specific to this entry. (5) The rest of `church-fathers.json` / `church-fathers-vi.json` beyond id 8.

**Keep for `sources` retrofit:** [Bradshaw, Johnson & Phillips, *The Apostolic Tradition: A Commentary* (Hermeneia, Fortress 2002)](https://www.fortresspress.com/store/product/9780800660468/The-Apostolic-Tradition) · [Baldovin, "Hippolytus and the *Apostolic Tradition*: Recent Research and Commentary," *Theological Studies* 64 (2003)](https://theologicalstudies.net/wp-content/uploads/2022/08/64.3.3.pdf) · [BMCR 2003.10.02, review of Cerrato, *Hippolytus between East and West* (Oxford Theological Monographs)](https://bmcr.brynmawr.edu/2003/2003.10.02/)

### 9. origen — Origen of Alexandria

**Checked:** Headline `quote` traced to *De Principiis*, Preface §8 in two independent full texts (New Advent /
earlychristianwritings, Roberts–Donaldson ANF); the second quotation in `sections[more-quotes]` hunted through the
complete ANF text of *Contra Celsum* Books I and VI (word-searched for "Creator of all things", "infirmity",
"assurance") without success; `dates` against Stanford Encyclopedia, IEP and the 1913 Catholic Encyclopedia;
the 543/553 condemnation against the council's own canon text (EWTN), Wikipedia-via-Price (2009) and the
Ramelli/afkimel dossier; the castration story against Eusebius *HE* VI.8 plus Chadwick/McGuckin's scholarship;
`facts` "surviving works" against the 1913 CE enumeration of the extant corpus; biography against Eusebius *HE* VI
directly. Cross-checked `church-fathers.json` (id 9) and `church-fathers-vi.json` (line 230ff). VN terminology
grepped against `content/content.json`. **Surface B:** all three Q&As, every quotation traced to a named work and
edition, every `sources:` URL fetched, all three `refs_ccc` verified at vatican.va and against the site's own
Catechism text, and — the point the task singles out — the Rufinus-vs-Origen question resolved by locating which
ancient translator preserves each infant-baptism passage and how far the Tura papyrus Greek actually reaches.

#### Surface A — Giáo Phụ entry (`content/giao-phu/origen.json`)

| Field | Claim as written | What the sources say | Verdict | Sources |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `facts[]` → "Tác phẩm còn lại / Surviving works" | `"3 tác phẩm" / "3 works"` | Badly understates. The extant corpus includes *Contra Celsum* (complete, in Greek), *De Principiis* (Rufinus's Latin + *Philocalia* Greek), 8 books of the *Commentary on Matthew* in Greek, 9 books of the *Commentary on John* in Greek, the *Commentary on Romans* (Rufinus), the *Commentary on the Canticle* (Rufinus), *On Prayer*, *Exhortation to Martyrdom*, letters, plus **~21 homilies in Greek, 118 in Rufinus's Latin and 78 in Jerome's Latin**. The number 3 is simply the length of this entry's own `works[]` array, mislabelled as a survival count — and it also implies the *Hexapla* survives, which it does not (fragments only). | `corrected` → propose `vi: "Còn lại rất nhiều: Chống Celsô (nguyên văn Hy Lạp), Về Các Nguyên Lý Đầu Tiên (chủ yếu qua bản Latinh của Rufinô), các chú giải Mátthêu và Gioan, hơn 200 bài giảng, cùng nhiều tác phẩm khác"` / `en: "Substantial: Contra Celsum (Greek), On First Principles (mainly in Rufinus's Latin), the Matthew and John commentaries, 200+ homilies, and more"` | [1913 CE, "Origen and Origenism"](https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/11306b.htm), [Homilies on Luke (FOTC 94) — 39 homilies in Jerome's Latin](https://dokumen.pub/homilies-on-luke-978-0813217444.html) |
| `sections[more-quotes].body` | `"it is a good thing to trust the Creator of all things regarding those things which we do not yet understand, rather than through infirmity of understanding lose the confident assurance we have already received."` — presented in quotation marks, attributed to *Against Celsus*, **with no book/chapter and no edition** | **Could not be traced.** The full ANF text of *Contra Celsum* Book I contains **no occurrence of "infirmity" and no occurrence of "assurance"**; Book VI chs. 10–13 likewise contain neither, nor "Creator of all things". The argument the section *summarises* is genuinely Origen's — *CC* I.9 ("if it were possible for all to leave the business of life, and devote themselves to philosophy…") and I.10–11 (faith is rational because all human undertakings rest on it: sailing, marriage, sowing) — but this sentence matches no translation I can find. Do not silently swap in a different quote. | `unverifiable` → propose either dropping the quotation marks and keeping the (accurate) paraphrase as the site's own summary, or replacing it with a traceable *CC* I.9–11 sentence **with book+chapter+edition**; flag `[cần kiểm chứng]` meanwhile | [Contra Celsum I, ANF, full text](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/04161.htm), [Contra Celsum I, Roberts–Donaldson](https://www.earlychristianwritings.com/text/origen161.html), [Contra Celsum VI, ANF](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/04166.htm) |
| `quote.en` / `quote.vi` | `"The Scriptures were written by the Spirit of God, and have a meaning... not only that which is obvious, but also another which is hidden from the majority of readers."` | The **passage and citation are real** — *De Principiis*, Preface §8. But the standard ANF/Roberts–Donaldson wording is: *"That the Scriptures were written by the Spirit of God, and have a meaning, **not such only as is apparent at first sight**, but also another, **which escapes the notice of most**."* The site's version is a loose re-rendering of no named edition, and the `...` marks an ellipsis where nothing is actually elided. Separately worth knowing: **the Preface survives only in Rufinus's Latin**, not in Greek. | `contested` → propose restoring the standard rendering and naming the edition, e.g. `en: "The Scriptures were written by the Spirit of God, and have a meaning, not such only as is apparent at first sight, but also another, which escapes the notice of most."`, `source.en: "On First Principles, Preface 8 (ANF trans., from Rufinus's Latin)"` | [De Principiis Preface, ANF](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/04120.htm), [Roberts–Donaldson text](http://www.earlychristianwritings.com/text/origen121.html) |
| `sections[later-influence].body` | "whether the specific anti-Origenist anathemas were issued by the ecumenical council itself **or by a separate, earlier local synod whose decisions later got attached to the council's record**" | The instinct to hedge is right, but this states only **one** of the competing reconstructions and omits the two facts that matter most. (a) There really was an earlier local action: Justinian's edict to Patriarch Menas, **9 anathemas, 543**, ratified by a local council at Constantinople. (b) But the **15** anti-Origenist anathemas are today most commonly assigned to **553 itself** — Richard Price's critical edition of the *Acts* (2009) is behind the position now generally accepted, that *"the bishops signed the canons condemning Origenism before the council formally opened."* Neither reconstruction is "an earlier local synod attached to the record". (c) The entry never mentions that **Origen IS named by name in the council's own canon/anathema 11** — "Arius, Eunomius, Macedonius, Apollinaris, Nestorius, Eutyches and Origen" — although in the surviving Latin acts his name appears **nowhere except** canon 11, which is why that mention has itself been argued to be a later interpolation. | `corrected` → propose replacing the hedge with the actual state of the question, naming 543 (Justinian/Menas, 9 anathemas), 553 (15 anathemas, pre-conciliar session per Price), and canon 11's naming of Origen together with the interpolation argument — i.e. represent the dispute rather than gesture at it | [Canon XI text, EWTN](https://www.ewtn.com/catholicism/library/second-council-of-constantinople-11563), [Second Council of Constantinople — citing Price 2009, *Acts*, 270–286](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Council_of_Constantinople), [Synod of Constantinople (543)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Synod_of_Constantinople_(543)), [Roger Pearse, on the anathemas](https://www.roger-pearse.com/weblog/2009/08/07/the-anathemas-against-origen-at-the-2nd-council-of-constantinople/) |

One of the two has to give. `church-fathers.json` line 342 is blunter still: *"Several speculative opinions formally condemned as heretical at the Second Council of Constantinople (553 AD)"*, and its `canonization_note` grounds the non-canonization in that condemnation. | `corrected` → propose hedging `life[1]` to match, e.g. `vi: "…về sau bị kết án sau khi ngài qua đời (gọi là 'thuyết Ôrigênê'), gắn với Công đồng Constantinôpôli II (năm 553) — dù tình trạng công đồng chính xác của các lời tuyên án ấy vẫn còn được các sử gia tranh luận"`; and apply the same hedge in `church-fathers.json` / `church-fathers-vi.json` | as above |
| `dates.born` / `dates.display` / `facts` "Sinh" | `184` / `"c. 184 — c. 253"` / `"khoảng năm 184"` | The major reference works give **185** or **185/6**, not 184: Stanford *"Origen (c. 185–c. 253)"*; IEP *"born around 185/6 C.E."*; 1913 CE *"born 185"*. On the death date the sources genuinely split: Stanford *"died as Gallus came to the throne in 253"*; 1913 CE *"253 or 254"*; IEP *"254/5 C.E."* — the tension is inside Eusebius, who implies a birth c. 185 but says Origen died in his seventieth year. | `contested` → propose `display: "c. 185 — c. 253/254"`, `born: 185`, and `facts` values `vi: "khoảng năm 185"` / `en: "c. 185 AD"`, `vi: "khoảng năm 253–254"` / `en: "c. 253–254 AD"`. Same fix needed in `church-fathers.json` (`birth_year: "c. 184 AD"`). | [Stanford Encyclopedia, "Origen"](https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/origen/), [IEP, "Origen of Alexandria"](https://iep.utm.edu/origen-of-alexandria/), [1913 CE](https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/11306b.htm) |

Ἀδάμας = "unconquerable, unbreakable"; **Eusebius** connects the name to his firmness against heretics; **Jerome** (*De viris illustribus*, "Origen, surnamed Adamantius") is the other ancient anchor; it is also standardly read as the invincible force of his arguments and his unconquerable ascetic endurance. | `corrected` → propose dropping "simply/chỉ", e.g. `vi: "…'Adamantius' — người thép — trở thành biệt danh thường trực của ngài: vừa vì khối lượng ngài đã viết, vừa vì sự kiên vững không thể lay chuyển của ngài và sức mạnh không thể bẻ gãy trong lập luận"` | [Jerome, *De viris illustribus* — "Origen, surnamed Adamantius"](https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/Nicene_and_Post-Nicene_Fathers:_Series_II/Volume_III/Lives_of_Illustrious_Men/Jerome/Origen,_surnamed_Adamantius), [Wace, *Dictionary of Christian Biography*, "Origenes"](https://www.ccel.org/ccel/wace/biodict.html?term=Origenes%2C+known+as+Origen) |

Eusebius's own account has Demetrius appointing Origen directly. | `contested` → propose `vi: "Theo Êusêbiô, ngài là học trò của Clêmentê thành Alexandria và (rất có thể) kế vị ngài làm trưởng Trường Giáo lý — một điểm giới học giả hiện đại còn dè dặt"` / `en: "According to Eusebius he studied under Clement of Alexandria and, probably, succeeded him as head of the catechetical school — a succession modern scholarship treats cautiously"` | [Stanford Encyclopedia, "Origen"](https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/origen/), [Eusebius, *HE* VI](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/250106.htm) |
