(Of the three Cappadocians only Basil and Gregory of Nazianzus are Doctors.) Both the per-slug entry and both legacy datasets get this right and agree with each other. | `confirmed` | [Vatican City State — 10 January, St Gregory of Nyssa, Father of the Church](https://www.vaticanstate.va/en/state-and-government/general-informations/saint-of-the-day/3440-january-10-saint-gregory-of-nyssa-father-of-the-church.html) |
| `facts[]` "Lễ kính / Feast day" (and identically in both legacy files) | "9 tháng 3 (Tây phương); 10 tháng 1 (Đông phương)" / "March 9 (Western); January 10 (Eastern)" | **Out of date on the Western side.** 9 March was the date in the *older* Roman Martyrology; the current Vatican-published commemoration is **10 January**, the same day as the East — the Vatican City State saint-of-the-day page is headed "**January 10**: Saint Gregory of Nyssa, Father of the Church". The revised *Martyrologium Romanum* (2001, editio typica altera 2004) moved him to 10 January to align with the Byzantine date; 9 March survives in the Episcopal/Anglican calendar. (He has no day in the General Roman Calendar at all — he is a Martyrology commemoration only, unlike Basil and Nazianzen who share 2 January.) Suggested: `vi: "10 tháng 1 (Sách Các Thánh Rôma hiện hành, trùng với lịch Đông phương; trước 2001 là 9 tháng 3)"` / `en: "January 10 (current Roman Martyrology, same as the Eastern date; March 9 before 2001)"`. | `corrected` | [Vatican City State, 10 January](https://www.vaticanstate.va/en/state-and-government/general-informations/saint-of-the-day/3440-january-10-saint-gregory-of-nyssa-father-of-the-church.html), [Gregory of Nyssa — feast dates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gregory_of_Nyssa) *(pointer; the underlying change is the 2001 Martyrologium Romanum)* |

Thus, no limit would interrupt growth in the ascent to God, since no limit to the Good can be found nor is the increasing of desire for the Good brought to an end because it is satisfied." I could not open a numbered full text to re-count paragraph **239** independently (the one open PDF failed on a bad certificate), so the *wording and work* are verified but the *exact section number* rests on the translation the entry is quoting. Listed so a later pass does not "fix" a sound quote; if anyone wants belt-and-braces, check §239 in a print copy of Malherbe–Ferguson. | `confirmed` (section number not independently re-counted) | [Malherbe & Ferguson, The Life of Moses](https://svspress.com/gregory-of-nyssa-the-life-of-moses/) |
| `sections[].id = "more-quotes"` | Claims "Concepts create idols; only wonder comprehends anything" is **not** Gregory's wording but a modern paraphrase associated with Jürgen Moltmann, and gives the real text from *The Life of Moses* | **The entry's debunking is itself correct — a good catch that should be preserved.** Moltmann quotes "Concepts create idols. Only wonder understands" in *The Spirit of Life* (Fortress, 1992), citing PG 44, col. 377. Gregory-of-Nyssa specialist **Prof. Giulio Maspero** (Pontificia Università della Santa Croce) states the quote "is not literal". The literal text the entry gives — "every concept that comes from some comprehensible image, by an approximate understanding and by guessing at the divine nature, constitutes an idol of God and does not proclaim God" — is Gregory's real wording. One refinement worth noting: Maspero traces the sense to *In Canticum* (GNO VI, 358,12–359,4) as well, so the idea is not confined to *The Life of Moses*. | `confirmed` | [Maspero on the quote's provenance](https://liberlocorumcommunium.blogspot.com/2022/08/concepts-create-idols-only-wonder.html), [Moltmann citation trail](https://www.oca.org/reflections/fr.-steven-kostoff/only-wonder-grasps-anything) |

And the restoration was not automatic on Valens's death: the Catholic Encyclopedia has him "wander[ing] from town to town, until the death of Valens in 378", after which the new emperor **Gratian published an edict of toleration** and Gregory returned "received with joy". Neither point contradicts the entry; both would make it more precise. | `confirmed` (with the 375 Ancyra precursor and Gratian's edict as refinements) | [Cath. | `dates` / `facts[]` Born, Died — cross-check against the 1913 Catholic Encyclopedia | "c. 335 — c. 395" | **The entry is right and the most-reprinted source on the open web is wrong — flagging so nobody "corrects" it downward.** The 1913 Catholic Encyclopedia says "Date of birth unknown" and that he "died after 385 or 386"; that dating is a century out of date and is the hidden common ancestor of several sites. The Vatican's own page gives born "in Cappadocia **between 335 and 340**" and, since he attended a **synod in Constantinople in 394**, that he "probably died shortly thereafter, **around 395**". The entry's `c. 335 — c. 395` matches current usage. Optional hedge for the birth year: `vi: "khoảng năm 335–340"` / `en: "c. 335–340 AD"`, since the Vatican gives a range where the entry gives a point. | `confirmed` (birth year could be hedged to a range) | [Vatican City State page](https://www.vaticanstate.va/en/state-and-government/general-informations/saint-of-the-day/3440-january-10-saint-gregory-of-nyssa-father-of-the-church.html), [Cath. Encyc. (the outdated one)](https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/07016a.htm) |

The entry's own `works[]` lists three *representative* works, not the survival count. Suggested value: `vi: "Kho tác phẩm lớn — 3 tập trong bộ Patrologia Graeca (PG 44–46); bản phê bình hiện đại (Gregorii Nysseni Opera) gồm khoảng 60 tác phẩm"` / `en: "A large corpus — three volumes of Migne's Patrologia Graeca (PG 44–46); the modern critical edition (Gregorii Nysseni Opera) contains some 60 works"`. | `corrected` | [Gregorii Nysseni Opera Online (Brill)](https://scholarlyeditions.brill.com/gnoo/), [Gregory of Nyssa — Internet Encyclopedia of Philosophy](https://iep.utm.edu/gregoryn/) |
| `sections[].id = "context"` — marriage | "spent a period of his young adulthood working as a teacher of rhetoric and, **apparently, married**" | **The hedge "apparently" is correct and should not be firmed up.** The entire marriage claim rests on Gregory of Nazianzus's letter of condolence on the death of **Theosebia**, and that letter calls her "**your sister** Theosebia" and a "yoke-fellow (*syzygos*) of a priest" — *syzygos* commonly means spouse but is not restricted to marriage. The 1913 Catholic Encyclopedia reads her as his wife ("who must have been his wife"); Orthodox tradition venerates **Theosebia the Deaconess** and reads her as his sister, i.e. also Basil's and Macrina's sister. Modern scholarship is genuinely split. The entry's wording already reflects that; flagged only so no later pass upgrades "apparently married" to "married". | `contested` | [Cath. Encyc., St Gregory of Nyssa](https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/07016a.htm), [Theosebia the Deaconess (OrthodoxWiki)](https://en.orthodoxwiki.org/index.php?title=Theosebia_the_Deaconess) |

### 20. ambrose-of-milan — Ambrose of Milan

**Checked:** Headline `quote` traced clause-by-clause to the Latin of *De sacramentis* IV, 5, 23 (Latin
Wikisource text of the whole six books, plus Deferrari's FOTC 44 rendering) — first clause confirmed,
**second clause found nowhere in the work**; the `more-quotes` "Ubi Petrus, ibi ecclesia" traced to
*Explanatio psalmi 40*, 30 (CSEL 64) and its context read; `dates` against Benedict XVI's catechesis at
vatican.va, Davidson's OECT edition and ODCC-derived reference works; feast day, Doctor status and the
1298 Boniface VIII decree checked; the Thessalonica/Theodosius narrative in `life[]`, `martyrdom` and
`later-influence` checked against Theodoret *HE* V.17 (the actual primary source) and the modern
literature (McLynn, Brown, Washburn); the death-bed quotation checked against Paulinus's *Vita*;
Augustine's baptism date, the antiphonal-hymn claim and *Veni redemptor gentium* checked; the works
count checked against a full catalogue of the corpus; Vietnamese forms grepped against
`content/content.json` and the two legacy datasets. Two minor points that came out **confirmed** and
should *not* be "fixed" later: (a) *De sacramentis* is treated here as Ambrose's own — that is the
correct modern position (Faller 1955, Botte 1961), even though older editions and translations, e.g.
Srawley's 1919 Cambridge volume, print it as "by an unknown author"; (b) Auxentius's death / Ambrose's
election in **374** is right — the rival date 373 (von Campenhausen, Palanque, Homes Dudden) is a real
but superseded position.

#### Surface A — Giáo Phụ entry (`content/giao-phu/ambrose-of-milan.json`)

Petschenig, 1919): "Ubi Petrus, ibi ergo ecclesia; ubi ecclesia, ibi nulla mors sed vita aeterna." But the *immediate context* is not a confession-of-faith argument: Ambrose has just quoted **Mt 16:18, "Tu es Petrus, et super hanc petram aedificabo ecclesiam meam,"** addressed to Peter personally, in a commentary on Ps 40(41):10 about the betrayer. The second half of the gloss (that it is not a direct claim about the bishop of Rome's jurisdiction) is defensible — Ambrose does not mention the Roman see in the sentence — but the first half reads a "faith Peter confessed" argument into a passage that is built on Mt 16:18. | `contested` → hedge to what the text supports, e.g. `vi`: "câu ấy đứng ngay sau lời Đức Kitô nói với Phêrô, 'Con là Phêrô, và trên đá này Thầy sẽ xây Hội Thánh của Thầy' (Mt 16,18); Ambrôsiô không nói gì ở đó về thẩm quyền tài phán của giám mục Rôma, nên câu nói không nên bị kéo giãn xa hơn điều chính ngài đã có ý định" | [*Explanatio psalmi 40*, CSEL 64 — Latin of the maxim](https://catholiceducation.org/en/religion-and-philosophy/ubi-petrus.html), [passage with the Mt 16:18 lead-in](https://theologicalcentrum.org/ubi-petrus-ibi-ecclesia-where-peter-is-there-is-the-church-st-ambrose/) |
| Vietnamese terminology — `sections[].martyrdom` and `sections[].later-influence` | "**Thessalônica**" (2×) | The entry is **internally inconsistent**: `life[1]` uses "**Thêxalônica**" and the section bodies use "Thessalônica". "Thêxalônica" is the site's own form — it is what `content/content.json` (the Vietnamese Catechism content) uses, and what `church-fathers-vi.json` uses (2×). "Thessalônica" occurs nowhere on the site except these two section bodies. | `corrected` → normalise both to "**Thêxalônica**" | site-internal: `content/content.json`, `content/giao-phu/church-fathers-vi.json` (verified by grep, 4 occurrences of "Thêxalônica" vs 2 of "Thessalônica", both in this file) |
| `works[]` entry 2 | "Về Các Mầu Nhiệm / Về Các Bí Tích" — *On the Mysteries / On the Sacraments* listed as **one** work, `latin` and `date` both empty | These are **two distinct works**: *De mysteriis* (one book, c. 390–391) and *De sacramentis* (six books, a stenographic record of Ambrose's Easter-week catechetical sermons). They are closely related — *De sacramentis* follows *De mysteriis* closely — which is why they are often printed together, but they are not one title. | `corrected` → split into two `works[]` rows: *De mysteriis* / "Về Các Mầu Nhiệm" and *De sacramentis* / "Về Các Bí Tích". (Do **not** touch the `Surviving works` count to match the new array length — see the row above.) | [Faller/Botte editions and the relation of the two works](https://presbytersproject.ihuw.pl/index.php?SourceID=1966&id=6), [NPNF2-10 prolegomena, list of extant works](https://www.ccel.org/ccel/schaff/npnf210.iii.vi.html) |
| `works[]` entry 1 `latin` | "De Officiis Ministrorum" | Defensible but not the critical-edition form. Two of the three manuscript families title it simply **"De officiis"**; the longer "De officiis ministrorum" appears only in the third and is, per Davidson, "most possibly a corrective gloss." Ancient citations use the short form (Augustine, *Ep.* 82.21; Cassiodorus, *Inst.* 1.16.4). Davidson's OECT critical edition (Oxford, 2001) is titled *Ambrose: De officiis*. Modern scholarship still uses the long form frequently, so this is not an error — just worth knowing if precision matters. | `contested` → optionally `latin`: "De Officiis (còn gọi De Officiis Ministrorum)" | [Presbyters Project on the MS families and Davidson's judgement](http://presbytersproject.ihuw.pl/index.php?id=6&SourceID=2008), [Davidson, *Ambrose: De officiis*, OECT 2001](https://academic.oup.com/book/25309/chapter/192297427) |
| `ccc_refs` | `[]` | Empty, as site-wide in this section. No finding. | — | — |

Everything else in the entry checked out and is deliberately **not** listed: death "4 April 397, Holy Saturday" (Benedict XVI: "died in Milan in the night between 3 and 4 April 397. It was dawn on Holy Saturday"); the death-bed saying, which is genuinely Paulinus's report in the *Vita Ambrosii*; feast day 7 December (his consecration date, 374 — correct in the current General Roman Calendar, and not his death date); Doctor of the Church (one of the four original Latin Doctors, decreed by **Boniface VIII in 1298**, bull *Gloriosus*, *Sext.* III.22 — worth adding to `facts` if the owner wants the year/pope); governor of Aemilia-Liguria seated at Milan; catechumen at election, baptized and consecrated within about a week (Paulinus: eight days); Augustine baptized at the Easter Vigil, night of **24–25 April 387**; *De officiis* as the first systematic Christian handbook of clerical ethics modelled on Cicero; antiphonal hymn-singing introduced during the 386 basilica standoff with Justina's court (Augustine, *Conf.* IX.7.15); *Veni redemptor gentium* among the four hymns universally accepted as authentic; the Ambrosian Rite still in liturgical use in Milan; the massacre at Thessalonica in **390** and readmission to communion at Christmas 390 after an eight-month penance; and the substance of the Theodosius confrontation (handled by letter — *Ep. extra collectionem* 11 [= 51] — with Ambrose making himself deliberately unavailable), which is exactly right and is the part the popular version gets wrong.

**Cross-file consistency:** `church-fathers.json` (id 20) and `church-fathers-vi.json` (id 20) agree with the per-slug file on every point — including **carrying the same unsupported second clause in `key_quote`** and the same bare "339 AD" / "năm 339". All three files need the quote fix, not just one. The Vietnamese legacy file uses "Thêxalônica" throughout, confirming that form as the site standard.

**Surface B items verified clean (no row needed):**
- **`refs_ccc: [1447]` (line 8)** — verified at vatican.va. CCC 1447 does say what the Q&A takes it to say: "Over the centuries the concrete form in which the Church has exercised this power received from the Lord has varied considerably," that in the early centuries reconciliation for grave sins was tied to a rigorous public penance, and that private, repeatable confession came to continental Europe with the Irish missionaries in the 7th century. The Vietnamese rendering on line 31 tracks the official text; the bolded "**hình thức cụ thể**" is the Q&A's own emphasis, not an alteration.
- **`sources:` block, lines 10–14** — both URLs resolve and point at what their labels claim. [papalencyclicals.net](https://www.papalencyclicals.net/councils/ecum12-2.htm) does carry the constitutions of Lateran IV, and constitution 21 does read "All the faithful of either sex, after they have reached the age of discernment, should individually confess all their sins in a faithful manner to their own priest at least once a year," with the Easter communion requirement and the seal of confession — so the Q&A's characterisation on line 29 (it *regulated*, it did not *invent*) is accurate. The [Catholic Answers "Confession" tract](https://www.catholic.com/tract/confession) is a real patristic anthology and is the proximate source of the Ambrose wording. Note for Session 3: the tract cites the Ambrose passage imprecisely as "*Penance* 1:1"; it is I, 2, 7 — so don't propagate the tract's reference.

**Not checked:** Origen and Cyprian on line 25–26 and the Didache on line 24 (out of scope — Origen assigned to another agent). I did not independently verify the anonymous "các nhà nghiên cứu ghi nhận" quotation on line 29 ("không làm gì hơn là xác nhận lại luật lệ và tập tục đã có trước đó") — it is an unattributed scholarly quotation and needs its own source; flagging it here so it is not read as verified. On Surface A I did not consult Liebeschuetz's translation of *Ep. extra coll.* 11 directly (the letter's content is taken from the secondary literature), and I did not verify the `portrait` provenance claim.

**Keep for `sources` retrofit:** [Benedict XVI, General Audience on St Ambrose, 24 Oct 2007 (vatican.va)](https://www.vatican.va/content/benedict-xvi/en/audiences/2007/documents/hf_ben-xvi_aud_20071024.html) · [*De sacramentis*, Latin text (la.wikisource)](https://la.wikisource.org/wiki/De_sacramentis_(Ambrosius)) · [Fourth Century Christianity — Ambrose's writings in chronological order](https://www.fourthcentury.com/writings-of-ambrose-in-chronological-order/)

### 21. jerome — Jerome

**Checked:** Headline `quote` traced to a **Vatican primary source** (Dei Verbum 25 cites it verbatim with the citation `PL 24,17`) — confirmed. Vulgate scope checked book-by-book against three independent treatments (ISBE/bible-researcher, the Fourth-Century Christianity project, and the Psalter-recension literature). Birth/death dates checked against the Kelly-vs-Cavallera scholarly dispute. Every quotation in `sections[]` verified against a full text: the Preface to the Gospels ("as many forms of texts as there are copies") at NPNF2-06, Letter 57 to Pammachius at newadvent, the "Scorpion" epithet, and Jerome's canon statements in the *Prologus Galeatus* / Preface to the Books of Solomon at NPNF2-06. Trent Session IV (8 Apr 1546) and *Scripturarum thesaurus* (25 Apr 1979, vatican.va) both confirmed. `further-reading` bibliography (Kelly 1975; White 1990) confirmed. Vietnamese name/title confirmed against TGP Sài Gòn and two diocesan sites. Cross-checked `church-fathers.json` / `church-fathers-vi.json` id 21. `ccc_refs` is empty.

#### Surface A — Giáo Phụ entry (`content/giao-phu/jerome.json`)

| `facts[]` "Tác phẩm còn lại / Surviving works" | `"2 tác phẩm" / "2 works"` | **Badly wrong** — and it is simply `works[].length`, matching the systemic pattern found across all 30 entries. Jerome's surviving corpus is one of the largest of any Latin Father: the letter collection numbers 154 letters (c. 123 genuinely his), plus **over 20 biblical commentaries** (the 12 Minor Prophets, Isaiah, Jeremiah, Ezekiel, Daniel, Matthew, Galatians, Ephesians, Titus, Philemon, Ecclesiastes, Psalms), the Vulgate translations themselves, the polemical works (*Adversus Helvidium*, *Adversus Iovinianum*, *Contra Vigilantium*, *Apologia contra Rufinum*, *Dialogus adversus Pelagianos*, *Altercatio Luciferiani*), *De viris illustribus*, the *Chronicon*, three monastic Lives, a body of homilies, and the Hebrew Names / Places / Questions. A scholarly catalogue lists 250+ distinct items. | `corrected` → propose VI: `"Rất nhiều — bản Vulgata, trên 20 bộ chú giải Kinh Thánh, tuyển tập 154 lá thư (khoảng 123 lá đích thực của ngài), cùng các tác phẩm luận chiến, lịch sử và hạnh thánh"` / EN: `"Very many — the Vulgate, over 20 biblical commentaries, a collection of 154 letters (c. 123 genuinely his), plus polemical, historical and hagiographic works"` | [Fourth-Century Christianity: Jerome chart](https://www.fourthcentury.com/jerome-chart/), [RBECS, *The Letters of Jerome*](https://rbecs.org/2012/01/28/the-letters-of-jerome-asceticism-biblical-exegesis-and-the-construction-of-christian-authority-in-late-antiquity/), [Roger Pearse, letters of St Jerome](https://www.roger-pearse.com/weblog/2025/12/06/the-letters-of-st-jerome/) |

**Verified clean (notable, do NOT "fix" later):**
- **`quote`** — `"Không biết Kinh Thánh là không biết Đức Kitô." / "Ignorance of Scripture is ignorance of Christ."`, cited to *Commentary on Isaiah, Prologue*: **exactly right, verified at vatican.va.** Dei Verbum 25 quotes it and footnotes it "St. Jerome, Commentary on Isaiah, Prologue (PL 24,17)". This is the strongest citation in the entry. → [Dei Verbum 25 (vatican.va)](https://www.vatican.va/archive/hist_councils/ii_vatican_council/documents/vat-ii_const_19651118_dei-verbum_en.html)
- **`sections[context]`** — `"tot enim sunt exemplaria paene quot codices"` / "there are almost as many forms of texts as there are copies": genuine, from the Preface to the Four Gospels addressed to Damasus. The commission year 382 is right.
- **`sections[more-quotes]`** — Letter 57 to Pammachius, **395 AD** (date correct), sense-for-sense not word-for-word, **and the explicit exception for Scripture "where even the order of the words is a mystery"** — all three confirmed in the text at newadvent.
- **`sections[theology]`** — Augustine did write directly urging caution on exactly the grounds described (the Septuagint's apostolic use and liturgical reading); confirmed in Ep. 28 (c. 394), Ep. 71 (403) and the Oea/Jonah-gourd episode of Ep. 82.
- **`sections[later-influence]`** — Trent declared the Vulgate authentic on **8 April 1546** (Session IV, verbatim: held "as authentic" in public lectures, disputations, sermons and expositions); the **Nova Vulgata** was promulgated *typica* by John Paul II in *Scripturarum thesaurus*, **25 April 1979**, verified on vatican.va.
- **`sections[martyrdom]`** — not a martyr; buried near the Nativity cave beside Paula (d. 404) and Eustochium (d. 419); relics later translated to Santa Maria Maggiore. All confirmed.
- **`life[0]`** — studied under Gregory of Nazianzus at Constantinople (from 379); contemporary of Augustine. Confirmed.
- **`role`** — "Linh mục, đan sĩ và học giả Kinh Thánh; không phải giám mục" is correct and matches Vietnamese Catholic usage ("Linh mục, Tiến sĩ Hội Thánh").
- **`sections[further-reading]`** — Kelly, *Jerome: His Life, Writings and Controversies*, **Duckworth, London, 1975** ✓. White, *The Correspondence (394–419) Between Jerome and Augustine of Hippo*, **E. Mellen Press, Lewiston, 1990**, Studies in the Bible and Early Christianity vol. 23 ✓. NPNF Ser. II vol. 6, Fremantle ✓.
- **Vietnamese terminology** — `"Giêrônimô"` is the standard Vietnamese Catholic form (TGP Sài Gòn, Tổng Giáo phận Huế, Giáo phận Hải Phòng all use it with "Linh mục, Tiến sĩ Hội Thánh", feast 30/9). `content.json` returns no hits for the name, so there is nothing on-site to be inconsistent with. `"Đamasô"`, `"Grêgôriô thành Nazianzô"`, `"Rufinô"`, `"Bêlem"`, `"Triđentinô"` are all consistent with the site's own usage elsewhere. **No terminology finding.**
- **`ccc_refs`** — empty, as expected site-wide for this section.
- **`era`** — `post-nicene` is correct and uncontroversial.

### 22. john-chrysostom — John Chrysostom

**Checked:** `name.vi` against the site's own Catechism (`content/content.json`) and seven Vietnamese Catholic authorities, plus an independent identification of who "John of Antioch" actually was; `quote` traced to NPNF *Homily 50 on Matthew* §3–4; **both** further quotations in `more-quotes` chased into full texts (one holds, one does not); the 397–398 accession, the 403 Synod of the Oak, the 404 exile, the 407 death and the 438 return of the relics against the Catholic Encyclopedia and primary church historians; `works[]` and the surviving-works count; `ccc_refs` empty; cross-checked against both legacy datasets. Full detail and caveats in the shared block at the end of this fragment.

| Field | Claim as written | What the sources say | Verdict | Sources |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `name.vi` | **"Gioan thành Antiôkia"** (while `name.en` is "John Chrysostom") | **The site's own Vietnamese Catechism uses "Gioan Kim Khẩu"** — 6 occurrences in `content/content.json`, zero occurrences of "Gioan thành Antiôkia" (e.g. "Đó là thánh Cyrillô Giêrusalem, **thánh Gioan Kim Khẩu**, thánh Ambrôsiô…"; "phụng vụ của **thánh Gioan Kim Khẩu**"). Every Vietnamese Catholic authority checked titles him **"Thánh Gioan Kim Khẩu"**: HĐGM VN ("Thứ Hai 24 TN: **Thánh Gioan Kim Khẩu**, Giám mục"), TGP Sài Gòn ("Ngày 13/09: **Thánh Gioan Kim Khẩu**"), TGP Hà Nội, Giáo phận Vĩnh Long, Kon Tum, Long Xuyên, Phú Cường, Hải Phòng — all "Gioan Kim Khẩu, Giám mục, Tiến sĩ Hội Thánh". Where "thành Antiôkia" appears in those articles it is prose describing his birthplace ("Thánh Gioan thành Antiokia, mệnh danh là Chrysostome (Kim Khẩu)"), **never the name/title of the entry**. Worse, **"John of Antioch" is a different man**: John I, **Patriarch of Antioch 428–441**, who arrived late at Ephesus 431 and "opened at his own lodging a rival synod, which defended Nestorius and condemned Cyril", reconciling with Cyril in the Formula of Reunion of April 433. That man belongs to entry **25 (Cyril of Alexandria)**, whose entry currently does **not** mention him at all — so entry 25 cannot be given its Ephesus-431/Formula-of-Reunion detail under its correct Vietnamese name while entry 22 occupies it. | `corrected` → `"vi": "Gioan Kim Khẩu"` | [HĐGM VN](https://hdgmvietnam.com/chi-tiet/xin-ngai-phan-mot-loi-17-9-2018-thu-hai-tuan-24-thuong-nien-b--34247), [TGP Sài Gòn](https://tgpsaigon.net/bai-viet/ngay-13-09-thanh-gioan-kim-khau-47906), [TGP Hà Nội](https://www.tonggiaophanhanoi.org/ngay-13-thang-9-thanh-gioan-kim-khau-kh-349-407-giam-muc-tien-sy-hoi-thanh-le-nho/), [GP Vĩnh Long](https://giaophanvinhlong.net/ngay-139-thanh-gioan-kim-khau-giam-muc-tien-si-hoi-thanh/), [John of Antioch, Cath. Encyc.](https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/08468a.htm), [John I of Antioch, OrthodoxWiki](https://orthodoxwiki.org/John_I_of_Antioch) |
| cross-file: `church-fathers.json` id 22 | `"full_name": "John of Antioch (John Chrysostom)"` (line 812) — the legacy combined dataset that still feeds the homepage/search | Same defect, in English, in the file that feeds search. "John of Antioch" is not an alternative name for Chrysostom; it is the name of Patriarch John I of Antioch (428–441), Cyril's opponent at Ephesus. Chrysostom's standard alternative designations are "John Chrysostom" / "John the Golden-Mouthed" (Ἰωάννης ὁ Χρυσόστομος). | `corrected` → `"full_name": "John Chrysostom"` | [Cath. Encyc., John of Antioch](https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/08468a.htm) |
| `quote` + `quote.source` | "Do you wish to honor the body of Christ? Do not ignore him when he is naked… What good is it if the Eucharistic table is overloaded with golden chalices, when your brother is dying of hunger?" — *Homily on Matthew, 50.3-4* | **Genuine and correctly cited.** NPNF I.10, Homily L (50) on Matthew, §3–4: "Would you do honour to Christ's body? Neglect Him not when naked; do not while here you honour Him with silken garments, neglect Him perishing without of cold and nakedness… For what is the profit, when His table indeed is full of golden cups, but He perishes with hunger? First fill Him, being an hungered, and then abundantly deck out His table also." The site's Vietnamese/English rendering is a modern paraphrase (closer to the wording used in papal/CCC-adjacent citations) rather than the NPNF translation, but the substance, work and section number all check out. Flagged only so a later pass does not "fix" a quote that is in fact sound. | `confirmed` | [NPNF, Homily 50 on Matthew](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/200150.htm) |
| `sections[].id = "martyrdom"` | "a second exile was ordered in 404, he was marched on foot for months across harsh terrain toward the remote frontier outpost of **Pithius**" | **Compresses three years into one journey, and misdates the Pityus order.** The 404 exile did not send him toward Pityus. The Catholic Encyclopedia: "On 24 June, 404, the soldiers conducted Chrysostom a second time into exile," and he was "conducted to **Cucusus**, a secluded and rugged place on the east frontier of Armenia," where he remained (fleeing "for some time to the castle of Arabissus" the following year). Only "**In the summer, 407, the order was given to carry him to Pithyus**" — the fatal forced march is a 407 event, roughly three years after the 404 exile order. Also, the place name is spelled **Pityus** (CE: "Pithyus"); "Pithius" is not a standard form. Suggested hedge/fix: state that the 404 exile sent him to Cucusus in Armenia, and that in the summer of 407 a further order to move him to Pityus on the Black Sea produced the forced march on which he collapsed and died at Comana in Pontus, 14 September 407. | `corrected` | [Cath. Encyc., St. John Chrysostom](https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/08452b.htm), [Synod of the Oak / exile summary](https://orthodoxwiki.org/Synod_of_the_Oak) |
| `facts[]` "Tác phẩm còn lại / Surviving works" | **"3 tác phẩm" / "3 works"** | **Badly wrong — this is just `works[].length`, the confirmed site-wide defect.** Chrysostom's surviving corpus is "perhaps the largest of any Greek church father," filling **eighteen volumes of Migne's *Patrologia Graeca* (PG 47–64)**. The Catholic Encyclopedia alone enumerates **about 238 letters** plus running homily series including **90 homilies on Matthew, 88 on John, 55 on Acts**, and numerous treatises (*De sacerdotio*, *Adversus Iudaeos*, the baptismal catecheses, *On Vainglory and the Education of Children*, etc.). The entry's own `life[]` already says "~700 sermons survive," so `facts[]` contradicts `life[]` **inside the same entry**. Suggested value: `vi: "Kho tác phẩm lớn nhất trong các Giáo Phụ Hy Lạp — khoảng 700 bài giảng và chừng 238 lá thư còn lại (18 tập trong bộ Patrologia Graeca)"` / `en: "The largest surviving corpus of any Greek Father — c. 700 homilies and c. 238 letters (18 volumes of Migne's Patrologia Graeca)"`. | `corrected` | [Cath. Encyc., St. John Chrysostom](https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/08452b.htm), [Fourth Century Christianity — John Chrysostom](https://www.fourthcentury.com/John-Chrysostom/) |
| `sections[].id = "more-quotes"` — quotation 1 | "The rich exist for the sake of the poor. The poor exist for the salvation of the rich" — attributed to "his homilies on the parable of the rich man and Lazarus" | **Could not be traced to any text of Chrysostom.** The line circulates very widely on Orthodox quote sites and social media, always without a locatable citation. I checked (a) the full text of the Second Discourse on the Rich Man and Lazarus (the discourse the entry points at) at tertullian.org — the sentence is **not there**; (b) *Homilies on 2 Thessalonians*, Homily 2, the one citation the open web does attach to it — **not there either**. Chrysostom is also the single most common target of patristic misattribution (an entire scholarly category, "Pseudo-Chrysostom", exists for texts wrongly ascribed to him). The *sentiment* is thoroughly Chrysostomian; the *sentence* is not sourced. Recommend either flagging `[cần kiểm chứng]`, dropping the "most sharply in his homilies on … Lazarus" source attribution, or replacing it with a passage that can be cited. **Do not silently swap in a different quote.** | `unverifiable` | [Four Discourses on the Rich Man and Lazarus, Discourse 2 (full text)](https://www.tertullian.org/fathers/chrysostom_four_discourses_02_discourse2.htm), [Homily 2 on 2 Thessalonians (full text)](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/23052.htm), [Pseudo-Chrysostom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pseudo-Chrysostom) |
| `sections[].id = "more-quotes"` — quotation 2 | "Again Herodias rages, again she dances, again she demands the head of John on a platter," reported by "Socrates Scholasticus and Sozomen" after the silver statue of Eudoxia, 403 | **Both attributions are right, and the entry's hedge is well-founded.** Socrates, *HE* VI.18 ("Of Eudoxia's Silver Statue. On account of it John is exiled a Second Time"): "Again Herodias raves; again she is troubled; she dances again; and again desires to receive John's head in a charger." Sozomen, *HE* VIII.20: "Herodias is again enraged; again she dances; again she seeks to have the head of John in a basin." So the line genuinely rests on two 5th-century historians, not one, and does not survive in a preserved sermon text — exactly as the entry says. Listed only so a later pass does not "correct" a correctly hedged claim. | `confirmed` | [Socrates, HE VI.18](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/26016.htm), [Sozomen, HE VIII.20](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/26028.htm) |
| `apologetics[1]` "Liturgical continuity" | "The Divine Liturgy bearing his name is still prayed **essentially unchanged** in the Byzantine Rite today, continuous for over 1,600 years." / "vẫn được cử hành **gần như nguyên vẹn**" | **Contradicted by the entry's own `later-influence` section**, and by the manuscript evidence. The earliest witness, the **Barberini Euchologion (Codex Barberini gr. 336, c. 780)**, ascribes only **two** prayers to Chrysostom — the Prayer for the Catechumens and the Prayer Behind the Ambo — and the received text acquired major later strata (the Cherubic Hymn is a 6th-c. addition under Justin II, the Creed and Lord's Prayer entered later still). The site therefore says the honest thing in one field and the overstated thing in another, on the same page. Suggested fix: bring `apologetics[1]` into line with the section — e.g. `en: "The Divine Liturgy bearing his name is still the standard Sunday liturgy of the Byzantine Rite, a living tradition rooted in his own Antiochene practice — though the received text grew by later additions rather than being composed by him whole."` | `corrected` | [Barberini gr. 336 (Parenti)](https://womenpriests.org/tradition/parenti-the-barberini-gr336-manuscript/), [Liturgy of St John Chrysostom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liturgy_of_Saint_John_Chrysostom) |
| `facts[]` "Lễ kính / Feast day" | "13 tháng 9" / "September 13" only | **Correct for the Roman calendar, but incomplete for an entry built around the Byzantine Rite.** 13 September is the current General Roman Calendar date (chosen because 14 September, his death day, is the Exaltation of the Holy Cross); from the 13th c. until 1969 the Roman date was **27 January**, the anniversary of the translation of his relics. Byzantine Catholics and the Orthodox keep **13 November**, with 27 January and 14 September also observed. Entry 19 (Gregory of Nyssa) does give both a Western and an Eastern date, so this is also an internal inconsistency of format. Suggested: `vi: "13 tháng 9 (Tây phương; trước 1969 là 27 tháng 1); 13 tháng 11 (Byzantine/Đông phương)"`. | `contested` (incomplete rather than false) | [Feast of St John Chrysostom (EBSCO)](https://www.ebsco.com/research-starters/history/feast-st-john-chrysostom), [New Liturgical Movement, 27 January](https://www.newliturgicalmovement.org/2021/01/the-feast-of-st-john-chrysostom-and.html) |
| cross-file: `church-fathers-vi.json` id 22 | `"full_name": "Gioan thành Antiôkia (Gioan Kim Khẩu)"` | **This is where the defect can be seen happening.** The Vietnamese legacy record at least kept the real name in parentheses; the per-slug `john-chrysostom.json` dropped the parenthesis and kept only the wrong half. Same fix as above: lead with `Gioan Kim Khẩu`. The `nickname` field in the same record already glosses it correctly — `"Kim Khẩu" (Chrysostomos — "Miệng Vàng")` — so the file contradicts itself. | `corrected` → `"full_name": "Gioan Kim Khẩu"` | (internal; see name row above for external sources) |
| `sections[].id = "martyrdom"` (vi) and legacy `controversy_councils` (vi) | "**Công Đồng** Cây Sồi" (= "**Council** of the Oak") | **Category error, not a style choice.** The Synod of the Oak was a **provincial synod** of roughly three dozen bishops convened near Chalcedon in July 403 — never a council in the technical sense the Vietnamese word `Công đồng` carries on this site, which uses it for the ecumenical councils (Nixêa, Êphêsô, Constantinôpôli). Entry 19 of this same section correctly renders the synod that deposed Gregory of Nyssa as "**một thượng hội đồng** gồm các giám mục thù địch", so the section is internally inconsistent. Suggested: `Thượng Hội Đồng Cây Sồi`. The English side already says "Synod of the Oak" correctly. | `corrected` | [Synod of the Oak (OrthodoxWiki)](https://orthodoxwiki.org/Synod_of_the_Oak) |
| `sections[].id = "context"` | Nectarius died 397; Eutropius arranged a quiet summons and removal from Antioch under military escort; archbishop from 398 | **All confirmed.** Nectarius died 27 September 397; "In 397, by the advice of **Eutropius**, the favourite eunuch of Arcadius, Chrysostom was made Archbishop of Constantinople on the death of Nectarius"; "The people of Antioch refused to let him go, and it was not until an **armed escort** was sent to fetch him that he was able to start"; he "was secretly taken from Antioch" and ordained bishop on **26 February 398**. Listed only because the "secret removal under escort" detail reads like embellishment and is in fact well attested. | `confirmed` | [Cath. Encyc., Nectarius](https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/10737a.htm), [Perseus, Dict. of Greek & Roman Biography, "Chrysostomus"](https://www.perseus.tufts.edu/hopper/text?doc=Perseus%3Atext%3A1999.04.0104%3Aentry%3Dchrysostomus-joannes-bio-1) |
| `sections[].id = "later-influence"` | 438 return of relics under Theodosius II, procession led by Patriarch Proclus, his former student; Codex Barberini credits him with only two prayers | **Both confirmed.** The relics returned to Constantinople on **27 January 438** under **Theodosius II** (with his sister Pulcheria, whom the entry omits), in a procession led by **Patriarch Proclus**, John's former disciple, and were deposited in the Church of the Holy Apostles. **Codex Barberini gr. 336 (c. 780)** "ascribes only two liturgical prayers to Chrysostom, namely: a) the Prayer for the Catechumens, and b) the Prayer Behind the Ambo" — exactly as the entry states. Listed because these are the entry's most checkable specifics and they hold up. | `confirmed` | [OCA, Translation of the Relics (27 Jan)](https://www.oca.org/saints/lives/2026/01/27/100327-translation-of-the-relics-of-saint-john-chrysostom-archbishop-of), [Barberini gr. 336 (Parenti)](https://womenpriests.org/tradition/parenti-the-barberini-gr336-manuscript/) |

---

**Checked (detail, both entries):** Both `quote` fields traced to full texts (Chrysostom's *Homily 50 on Matthew* §3–4 at newadvent — genuine and correctly cited; Gregory's *Life of Moses* II.239 — wording verified as Malherbe–Ferguson, section number not independently re-counted). Every further quotation in the `sections` bodies was checked individually: entry 19's "Concepts create idols" debunking verified against Maspero and Moltmann's own citation (the entry is right); entry 22's Herodias line verified in **both** Socrates *HE* VI.18 and Sozomen *HE* VIII.20 (the entry is right to hedge it); entry 22's "The rich exist for the sake of the poor" **could not be traced to any text of Chrysostom**. Dates checked against the Vatican City State saint-of-the-day page, the Catholic Encyclopedia and Britannica-adjacent scholarship, with the 1913 CE treated as one source and flagged where it is out of date. Vietnamese terminology checked by grepping `content/content.json` and against hdgmvietnam.com, tgpsaigon.net, tonggiaophanhanoi.org and four diocesan sites — never Vietnamese Wikipedia. `works[]` titles for both entries exist and are correctly attributed (*Ad Ablabium*, *De vita Moysis*, *De anima et resurrectione*; *Homilies on Matthew*, *Homilies on the Pauline Epistles*, *De sacerdotio*) — no spuria among them. `ccc_refs` is **empty in both entries**, so nothing to check there. Cross-file consistency checked against `church-fathers.json` and `church-fathers-vi.json` for both figures.

**Not checked:** (1) The **exact paragraph number II.239** in *The Life of Moses* — the one open numbered full text failed on a self-signed certificate; the wording and work are verified, the section number is not. (2) Chrysostom's `life[]` figure of "**~700 sermons**" — I confirmed the corpus is 18 volumes of PG and that the CE enumerates 90 homilies on Matthew, 88 on John, 55 on Acts and c. 238 letters, but I did not find a source that states a total sermon count, so "~700" is plausible and uncontradicted but not independently confirmed. (3) Entry 19's claims about **Pseudo-Dionysius's dependence on Gregory** and the **Daniélou/von Balthasar rediscovery** — plausible scholarly commonplaces, not verified this session. (4) Entry 19's *apokatastasis* claim and the Origen-condemnation asymmetry — the historical facts (Gregory taught a form of universal restoration; Origen was condemned in 553; Gregory never was) are standard, but I did not verify them against sources this session, so treat them as unchecked. (5) The **six remaining sermons** of *On Wealth and Poverty* beyond Discourse 2, which I could not search in full text — the "rich exist for the sake of the poor" line could in principle be in one of them.

**Keep for `sources` retrofit:** **19** — [Vatican City State, St Gregory of Nyssa (10 Jan)](https://www.vaticanstate.va/en/state-and-government/general-informations/saint-of-the-day/3440-january-10-saint-gregory-of-nyssa-father-of-the-church.html), [Gregorii Nysseni Opera Online (Brill)](https://scholarlyeditions.brill.com/gnoo/), [Internet Encyclopedia of Philosophy — Gregory of Nyssa](https://iep.utm.edu/gregoryn/). **22** — [NPNF, Homily 50 on Matthew](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/200150.htm), [Socrates Scholasticus, HE VI.18](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/26016.htm), [TGP Sài Gòn, Thánh Gioan Kim Khẩu](https://tgpsaigon.net/bai-viet/ngay-13-09-thanh-gioan-kim-khau-47906).

### 23. augustine-of-hippo — Augustine of Hippo

**Checked:** Surface A — headline `quote` traced to the Latin of *Conf.* I.i.1 (`fecisti nos ad te et inquietum
est cor nostrum donec requiescat in te`) at thelatinlibrary; **both** further quotations in the `more-quotes`
section traced to the Latin (`Conf.` VIII.7.17 and X.27.38) and then compared against the named published
translations; all `dates`, `facts[]` rows and `life[]` claims (birth 13 Nov 354 Thagaste, nine years a
Manichee, garden conversion 386, Rom 13:13–14 / *tolle lege*, baptism by Ambrose Easter 387, death 28 Aug 430
under siege) against the Stanford Encyclopedia of Philosophy and the Latin of *Conf.* VIII.12; the
`martyrdom` section's Possidius claims against the Latin and English of *Vita Augustini* 29/31; `works[]`
dates against O'Donnell (Georgetown) and Britannica-adjacent sources; the surviving-works count against
Benedict XVI at vatican.va; Vietnamese terms against the site's own Vietnamese Catechism in
`content/content.json` and Vietnamese Catholic publishers; cross-file against
`content/giao-phu/church-fathers.json` and `church-fathers-vi.json` (id 23). Surface B — CCC 2066 and
2129–2132 fetched verbatim at vatican.va; the Augustinian, Talmudic and Philonic divisions of the Decalogue
checked against a reference-work-based survey and a Jewish source; Luther's own numbering read off the Small
Catechism at bookofconcord.org; the dating of Augustine's *Quaestiones in Heptateuchum* II.71 checked.
`ccc_refs` in the Giáo Phụ entry is **empty**.

---

Boulding: "Late have I loved you, Beauty so ancient and so new, late have I loved you! Chadwick (OUP 1991) reads instead: "Late have I loved you, beauty so old and so new: late have I loved you. And see, you were within and I was in the external world and sought you there, and in my unlovely state I plunged into those lovely created things which you made." Every distinctive word the entry uses ("so ancient and so new", "Lo, you were within", "shapely things", "rushed headlong", "misshapen") is Boulding's and none is Chadwick's. The chapter reference X.27.38 is correct. | `corrected` → change the credit only, not the text: `en` → `(Confessions X.27.38, Boulding translation)`; `vi` → `(Tự Thuật X.27.38, theo bản dịch Boulding)`. NB this also sits oddly with `further-reading`, which recommends Chadwick 1991 as "the standard modern scholarly translation" — either credit Boulding here and add her edition to further-reading, or re-quote the passage in Chadwick's actual words. | [translation comparison, Boulding vs Chadwick vs Bourke](https://epistleofdude.wordpress.com/2019/05/14/a-comparison-of-different-translations-of-augustines-confessions-late-have-i-loved-you/), [Boulding, *The Confessions*, New City Press 1997 (WSA I/1)](https://www.amazon.com/Confessions-Works-Augustine-Translation-Century/dp/1565480848), [Chadwick 1991 OUP identified as the "unlovely state / lovely created things" rendering](https://www.thegospelcoalition.org/blogs/trevin-wax/what-translation-augustines-confessions/) |

Standard bibliographies count **more than one hundred separate surviving titles** on top of the letters and sermons. Augustine is among the most voluminously preserved of all Latin authors; "3 works" is the single most misleading number in the entry. | `corrected` → `vi`: "Hơn 100 tác phẩm còn lại, cùng hơn 300 lá thư và gần 600 bài giảng"; `en`: "Over 100 surviving works, plus more than 300 letters and nearly 600 sermons". The English translation at tertullian.org likewise reads "**the shortest** penitential Psalms of David". Separately, the *fixed set of seven* penitential psalms (6, 32, 38, 51, 102, 130, 143) is first attested as a closed group in **Cassiodorus, *Expositio Psalmorum*, 6th century** — i.e. after Augustine's death — so attributing "the seven" to Possidius is anachronistic. **Confirmed and worth *not* "fixing" later (genuinely checked, genuinely right):**

- Headline `quote` + citation. *Conf.* I.i.1 Latin: "tu excitas ut laudare te delectet, quia **fecisti nos ad te et inquietum est cor nostrum donec requiescat in te**." Both the English and the Vietnamese are faithful; "Confessions, I.1" is the correct locus. ([Latin, thelatinlibrary](https://www.thelatinlibrary.com/augustine/conf1.shtml))
- The `da mihi castitatem et continentiam, sed noli modo` quotation itself, verbatim Latin, at *Conf.* VIII.7.17 — only the "in Carthage" locator is unsupported (row above).
- All `dates` / `facts[]` Born & Died rows: 13 Nov 354, Thagaste; 28 Aug 430; feast 28 August (Memorial, General Roman Calendar); Doctor of the Church; Hippo Regius = modern Annaba. Consistent internally, with the legacy files, and with SEP.
- `context` section: garden at **Milan**, **386**, "*tolle lege*", opened to **Romans 13:13–14**, **nine years** a Manichee, baptism by **Ambrose** at **Easter 387**. The Latin of *Conf.* VIII.12 confirms "de vicina domo… quasi pueri an puellae, nescio: tolle lege, tolle lege" and the Rom 13:13–14 text; SEP confirms the chronology and the nine Manichee years. ([SEP](https://plato.stanford.edu/entries/augustine/), [Latin *Conf.* VIII](https://faculty.georgetown.edu/jod/latinconf/8.html))
- `martyrdom` section: died of fever during the Vandal siege, "in the third month of the siege"; siege lasted "almost fourteen months"; Possidius **was** present ("while we stood by and watched and prayed"). All in Possidius, *Vita* 29 & 31. ([tertullian.org](https://www.tertullian.org/fathers/possidius_life_of_augustine_02_text.htm))
- `later-influence`: "Luther was an Augustinian friar before he was a reformer" — he entered the Augustinian house at Erfurt on 15 July 1505, professed 1506, lived there as a friar until 1511.
- `ccc_refs`: **empty** — nothing to verify.
- Cross-file: no contradiction between `augustine-of-hippo.json` and `church-fathers.json` / `church-fathers-vi.json` id 23 on any date, name, feast, see, quote or apologetics claim. The legacy files are in fact *more* accurate on the *City of God* date (they describe it as written **after** 410 rather than dating it to 410) and carry a nickname the per-slug file drops ("Doctor of Grace" / "Tiến Sĩ Ân Sủng").

#### Surface B — Giải Đáp citations (HAND-OFF TO SESSION 3 — do not apply)

Both files are in `content/giai-dap/`. **Neither file has a `sources:` frontmatter block at all** — there is nothing to resolve or check for either. The three Augustine-dependent passages are below.

| Q&A file | Line | Claim as written | What the sources say | Verdict | Proposed VI replacement | Sources |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `so-sanh-muoi-dieu-ran-cong-giao-tin-lanh.md` | **21** | "…theo cách chia của **thánh Augustinô** từ thế kỷ thứ 5 — **cũng là cách của người Do Thái xưa**: gộp lệnh 'không có thần nào khác' và lệnh 'không tạc tượng thờ' thành **một điều răn thứ nhất**…, rồi tách lệnh về sự tham lam thành **hai** điều răn cuối riêng biệt" | The equation with "the ancient Jewish way" does not hold, and the Jewish tradition is **not uniform**. There are two ancient Jewish schemes, and Augustine's matches **neither** as a whole: (a) the **Philonic** division (Philo and Josephus, 1st c. — the *oldest*) takes Ex 20:3 as the first commandment and 20:4–6 as a **separate second**, and keeps coveting as **one** — i.e. it is the scheme the *Reformed/Greek* churches use, not the Catholic one; (b) the **Talmudic** division (3rd c., "adopted by all Jews") makes the preface "I am the LORD thy God" the **first** of the ten *dibrot*, combines Ex 20:3–6 as the **second**, and likewise keeps coveting as **one**. Augustine "listed the ten commandments by beginning with the Talmudic second saying as the first commandment; and, following the word order of Deuteronomy 5:21, making the ninth commandment a prohibition of the coveting of a neighbor's wife, and the tenth a prohibition of coveting his house and other property." So Augustine agrees with the Talmudic scheme on **one** point (merging "no other gods" with "no graven images") and departs from it on **two** (he folds the prologue into the first commandment rather than counting it; he splits coveting in two). A Jewish source confirms the Talmudic shape: first = "I am the Lord thy god, who brought thee out of the land of Egypt"; coveting = one, "Thou shalt not covet anything that belongs to thy neighbor." **The century is correct**: the locus classicus is *Quaestiones in Heptateuchum* II, q. 71, composed **419–420**. | `corrected` — the "cũng là cách của người Do Thái xưa" clause overstates the overlap and, read against the next bullet, implies the Jewish precedent sits on the Catholic side when the *oldest* Jewish scheme (Philo/Josephus) actually sits on the Protestant side. | Replace the first bullet with:<br><br>`- **Người Công Giáo** (và người Luther) theo cách chia của **thánh Augustinô** — được ngài trình bày trong *Quaestiones in Heptateuchum* (khoảng năm 419-420, tức đầu thế kỷ V): gộp lệnh "không có thần nào khác" và lệnh "không tạc tượng thờ" thành **một điều răn thứ nhất** (cả hai cùng chống lại việc thờ ngẫu tượng), rồi tách lệnh về sự tham lam thành **hai** điều răn cuối riêng biệt (không tham vợ người ta / không tham của cải người ta). Truyền thống Do Thái theo sách Talmud cũng gộp hai lệnh đầu làm một như thánh Augustinô, nhưng khác ở hai điểm: lời mở đầu "Ta là Đức Chúa, Thiên Chúa của ngươi" được kể là điều răn **thứ nhất**, và lệnh về sự tham lam vẫn để **nguyên một** điều răn.` | [survey of the Philonic / Talmudic / Augustinian divisions, citing *Quaestionum in Heptateuchum* II.lxxi](https://www.bible-researcher.com/decalogue.html), [Jewish Virtual Library, The Ten Commandments](https://www.jewishvirtuallibrary.org/the-ten-commandments), [dating of *Quaestiones in Heptateuchum* to 419–20](https://www.oxfordreference.com/view/10.1093/acref/9780199299164.001.0001/acref-9780199299164-e-105) |
| `nguoi-cong-giao-co-tho-nguong-tuong-khong.md` | **26** | "…chỉ đánh số các điều răn theo một truyền thống cổ (**của người Do Thái và thánh Augustinô**) mà người Luther cũng dùng." | Same defect, more compressed: the parenthesis presents "người Do Thái" and "thánh Augustinô" as one tradition. They are not — see the row above. This is the overview page for the whole cluster, so it is the sentence most readers meet first. | `corrected` | Replace the parenthesis: `…chỉ đánh số các điều răn theo cách phân chia cổ của **thánh Augustinô** (một cách chia phần nào trùng với truyền thống Do Thái theo sách Talmud) mà các Giáo hội Luther cũng dùng.` | as above |
| `so-sanh-muoi-dieu-ran-cong-giao-tin-lanh.md` | **21** (parenthetical) and **32** | line 21: "(không tham vợ người ta / không tham của cải người ta)" presented as the ninth-then-tenth order · line 32: "chính người **Luther**… cũng dùng **đúng cách đánh số như** người Công Giáo" | Lutherans follow the same *division* (merge the first two; split coveting) but **not the same order of the last two**. Luther "preferred to follow the word order of Exodus 20", so in the Small Catechism the **Ninth** is "Thou shalt not covet thy neighbor's house" and the **Tenth** is "Thou shalt not covet thy neighbor's wife, nor his man-servant…" — the reverse of the Catholic ninth/tenth, which follow Deut 5:21. Also worth Session 3's awareness for line 32's rhetorical point: Luther's Small Catechism prints the First Commandment simply as "**Thou shalt have no other gods**" and does not reproduce the graven-image clause at all. CCC 2066's own careful phrasing — "It is also that of the Lutheran confessions" (of the *division*) — is the safer formulation than "đúng cách đánh số như". | `contested` — the claim is right about the division and wrong about "đúng… như" being exact. | line 21: leave the pair but drop the implied numbering, e.g. `…rồi tách lệnh về sự tham lam thành **hai** điều răn cuối riêng biệt (một về vợ người ta, một về của cải người ta)`. line 32: `Nói cách khác, chính các Giáo hội **Luther** — phát xuất từ phong trào Cải Cách — cũng theo đúng **cách phân chia** này (dù thứ tự hai điều răn cuối có đảo lại, vì Luther theo thứ tự chữ của sách Xuất Hành).` | [Luther's Small Catechism, Ten Commandments (bookofconcord.org)](https://bookofconcord.org/small-catechism/ten-commandments/), [Marlowe, "he preferred to follow the word order of Exodus 20"](https://www.bible-researcher.com/decalogue.html), [CCC 2066 (vatican.va)](https://www.vatican.va/archive/ENG0015/__P78.HTM) |

**Surface B items that check out clean** — reported per the brief, since a verified load-bearing citation is a useful result:

- **`so-sanh-…` line 28, the GLHTCG 2066 blockquote.** Verbatim correct in both directions. Latin-rite English at vatican.va: "The division and numbering of the Commandments have varied in the course of history. the present catechism follows the division of the Commandments established by St. Augustine, which has become traditional in the Catholic Church. It is also that of the Lutheran confessions. the Greek Fathers worked out a slightly different division, which is found in the Orthodox Churches and Reformed communities." The Vietnamese as quoted in the Q&A matches the site's own Vietnamese Catechism in `content/content.json` **word for word**, including "Các hệ phái Luther cũng theo cách phân chia này." No splice, no unmarked insertion, no bracket problem. ([CCC 2066, vatican.va](https://www.vatican.va/archive/ENG0015/__P78.HTM))
- **`so-sanh-…` line 21, "từ thế kỷ thứ 5".** Correct. *Quaestiones in Heptateuchum* II, q. 71 — the passage where Augustine sets the division out — dates to **419–420**. (Augustine also handles the Decalogue in *Sermo* 8/9, *De decem chordis*, which straddles c. 400; the 5th-century attribution is nonetheless the right one for the locus classicus.)
- **`so-sanh-…` line 22, "một cách đánh số cổ hơn (gắn với truyền thống Hy Lạp)".** Correct and creditably honest — the Philonic division *is* "the oldest method, found in the writings of Philo and Josephus (first century)… adopted by the Greek Church and by Protestants (except Lutherans)." The Q&A does **not** make the common apologetic mistake of claiming the Catholic numbering is the older one.
- **`so-sanh-…` line 19, "bản văn Kinh Thánh — Xuất Hành 20 và Đệ Nhị Luật 5 — không tự đánh số các điều răn."** Correct, and the premise on which all three good sources rest.
- **`refs_ccc` — both files verified at vatican.va.** `so-sanh-…` `refs_ccc: [2066]` ✔ (quoted above). `nguoi-cong-giao-…` `refs_ccc: [2129, 2130, 2131, 2132]` ✔ — 2129 "The divine injunction included the prohibition of every representation of God by the hand of man"; 2130 God "ordained or permitted the making of images that pointed symbolically toward salvation by the incarnate Word"; 2131 Nicaea II (787) "justified against the iconoclasts the veneration of icons"; 2132 "The Christian veneration of images is not contrary to the first commandment which proscribes idols. Indeed, 'the honor rendered to an image passes to its prototype.'" All four say what the Q&A takes them to say, including the §2132 citation at line 32. ([CCC 2129–2132, vatican.va](https://www.vatican.va/archive/ENG0015/__P7F.HTM))
- **`refs_scripture`** — `Xh 20,2-17` / `Đnl 5,6-21` are the two Decalogue pericopes; `Xh 20,4-5` matches the text blockquoted at line 28 of the overview file. Correct.
- **`nguoi-cong-giao-…` line 38 — Luther's *Against the Heavenly Prophets* (1525).** Checked although outside the Augustine assignment, since it is a hard fact (title + year) in the same file. Confirmed: *Against the Heavenly Prophets in the Matter of Images and Sacraments*, **1525**, written against Andreas Karlstadt's iconoclasm; Luther "bitterly condemned their iconoclasm" and held that "according to the Law of Moses no other images are forbidden than an image of God which one worships. A crucifix… or any other holy image is not forbidden." The Q&A's summary is accurate.

Someone should.

These are widely repeated scholarly commonplaces; I did **not** run them down to a citable source and they are not flagged above. If the owner wants them defended rather than assumed, they need a pass.
- `sections[].theology` — Pelagius's own position ("grace mainly meant helpful teaching and example") is the standard characterisation drawn from Augustine's anti-Pelagian works; I did not check it against surviving Pelagian texts, where the picture is more contested than the polemical sources suggest.

Unlike the other load-bearing Fathers, neither Q&A quotes Augustine's *words* at all — they cite his *numbering scheme*, and the only quotation in the passages assigned to me is of CCC 2066, which I verified verbatim in both languages. There is therefore no VI→EN→Latin chain to audit and no editorial-bracket or splice risk in these three citations.

### 24. john-cassian — John Cassian

**Checked:** Main `quote` traced to the actual NPNF (Gibson) text of *Conference* 1.4 and confirmed near-verbatim; the `more-quotes` discretion saying checked against the Latin of *Collatio* II and against NPNF; `dates`, birthplace, feast day and canonization status against the 1913 Catholic Encyclopedia, Britannica, Catholic Answers and the OCA; all works checked for existence and authorship (and one missing work found); the Orange II / "Semi-Pelagian" account checked against the canons, the *Decretum Gelasianum* (Latin, tertullian.org) and Backus & Goudriaan in the *Journal of Ecclesiastical History*; RB 73 checked against the text of the Rule; both entries in `church-fathers.json` / `church-fathers-vi.json` compared.

| Field | Claim as written | What the sources say | Verdict | Sources |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `facts[]` "Surviving works" | `2 works` / `2 tác phẩm` | Three works survive complete: *De institutis coenobiorum* (12 books), *Collationes* (24 conferences), and *De incarnatione Domini contra Nestorium* (7 books, c. 430) — the third is missing from `works[]` entirely, and the count is just `works[].length` (site-wide defect). Propose `3 tác phẩm` / `3 works` **and** adding the third work: `{vi: "Về Mầu Nhiệm Nhập Thể của Chúa, chống Nestôriô", en: "On the Incarnation of the Lord, Against Nestorius", latin: "De Incarnatione Domini contra Nestorium", date: "c. 430"}` | `corrected` | [Latin text (Documenta Catholica Omnia)](http://www.documentacatholicaomnia.eu/03d/0360-0435,_Cassianus,_De_Incarnatione_Christi_Contra_Nestorium_Haereticum_Libri_Septem_[Schaff],_EN.pdf), [BiblIndex / CSEL 17 edition](https://www.biblindex.org/en/work-editions/de-incarnatione-domini-contra-nestorium/corpus-scriptorum-ecclesiasticorum-latinorum-17), [NPNF II.11 notes](https://media.sabda.org/alkitab-12/ccel/fathers/npnf211/jcassian/nestnote.htm) |
| `works[]` — missed cross-link | *De Incarnatione* absent | Worth knowing: Cassian wrote *De Incarnatione contra Nestorium* (c. 430) **at the request of the Roman archdeacon Leo — the future Leo the Great, entry 26 of this same set.** The site links neither entry to the other. | `confirmed` | [NPNF II.11 notes](https://media.sabda.org/alkitab-12/ccel/fathers/npnf211/jcassian/nestnote.htm) |
| `facts[]` "Region / See" + `sections.context` | "Born Scythia Minor (modern Dobruja, Romania)" stated flatly | Genuinely contested. The sole ancient witness is Gennadius, *De viris illustribus* 62, "natione Scytha"; some scholars read that as a confusion with Scetis in Egypt and argue for a Gallic/Provençal origin. Most modern scholarship (Stewart, Ramsey) accepts Scythia Minor, but flatly is too strong. Propose hedging: "có lẽ sinh tại Scythia Nhỏ (nay là Dobruja, Rumani) — nguồn gốc còn tranh luận" / "probably born in Scythia Minor (modern Dobruja, Romania); his origin is debated" | `contested` | [Britannica](https://www.britannica.com/biography/Saint-John-Cassian), [CE 1913](https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/03404a.htm) |
| `sections.theology` — Orange II | "corrected without personal condemnation of Cassian himself at the Second Council of Orange in 529" | Correct as far as it goes (Orange II condemned propositions, not persons, and never names Cassian), **but the entry omits the one place Rome *did* censure him by name**: the *Decretum Gelasianum* (early 6th c.), in its list of non-received books, reads "opuscula Cassiani presbyteri Galliarum **apocrypha**" — Cassian's works listed among the apocrypha. Also unmentioned: Prosper of Aquitaine's *Contra collatorem* attacked Conference 13 directly in Cassian's own lifetime. Propose adding one clause so the untidy history isn't tidied up. | `contested` (incomplete) | [Decretum Gelasianum, Latin text (tertullian.org)](https://www.tertullian.org/decretum.htm), [CE 1913 on Cassian](https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/03404a.htm) |
| `sections.theology` — the label | "eventually labeled 'Semipelagianism'" | Confirmed and correctly hedged — worth *not* "fixing" later. The term is not ancient: it was coined by Theodore Beza c. 1556 and spread through the Molinist controversy, whose polemicists named Cassian and Faustus of Riez as its exponents. Modern scholarship calls its retrospective use anachronistic. The entry's "eventually labeled" is right; the 1913 Catholic Encyclopedia's "since the Middle Ages" is not. | `confirmed` | [Backus & Goudriaan, *Journal of Ecclesiastical History* (PDF)](https://files01.core.ac.uk/download/pdf/85212815.pdf), [Muller, MAJT 29 (2018)](https://www.midamerica.edu/uploads/files//pdf/journal/01mullerjournal2018.pdf) |
| `facts[]` "Canonized" | `Có` / `Yes` | Misleading against the entry's own `martyrdom` section, which correctly says he "was never formally canonized by Rome through any recognized process." Both cannot stand as written. He is a saint by immemorial cult and **is** entered in the Roman Martyrology on 23 July, but there was no canonization. Propose `Được tôn kính là thánh theo lòng sùng kính lâu đời (không qua án phong thánh chính thức)` / `Venerated as a saint by immemorial cult — never formally canonized` | `corrected` | [CE 1913](https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/03404a.htm), [Catholic Answers Encyclopedia](https://www.catholic.com/encyclopedia/john-cassian) |
| `facts[]` "Feast day" + `sections.martyrdom` | "July 23 (regional only)"; "the Eastern Orthodox Church ... keeps his feast on its universal calendar" (no date) | Two gaps. (a) 23 July is not merely local: he is in the **Roman Martyrology** under 23 July (kept with an octave at Marseille); saying "regional only" understates it. (b) The Byzantine feast is **29 February (28 February in common years)**, not 23 July — an odd and quotable fact the entry leaves out while asserting the Eastern calendar keeps him. Propose naming the date. | `corrected` | [CE 1913](https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/03404a.htm), [OCA, Ven. John Cassian the Roman](https://www.oca.org/saints/lives/2001/02/28/100623-venerable-john-cassian-the-roman) |
| `sections.later-influence` | "Benedict ... tells his own monks near its very end (chapter 73) to turn to **Cassian's** Institutes and Conferences ... a direct, **named** endorsement" | RB 73 recommends "the Conferences of the Fathers, their Institutes and their Lives; there is also the rule of our holy father Basil" — it names the *works*, **not Cassian**. Benedict never names Cassian anywhere in the Rule (RB 42 likewise says only *Collationes*). The identification is a scholarly one, universally accepted but not on the page. Propose deleting "named": e.g. "…hãy tìm đến *Các Đàm Luận của các Giáo Phụ* và *Các Định Chế* — tức các tác phẩm của Cassianô, dù Bênêđictô không nêu tên ngài" / "…to the *Conferences of the Fathers* and the *Institutes* — Cassian's works, though Benedict does not name him." | `corrected` | [RB 73, SUNY Geneseo text](https://courses.lumenlearning.com/suny-geneseo-humanities1-1/chapter/the-rule-of-st-benedict-chapters-51-73/), [EWTN, Rule of St Benedict](https://www.ewtn.com/catholicism/library/rule-of-st-benedict-11901) |
| `sections.more-quotes` — discretion | "the parent, guardian, and moderator of all the virtues" + Latin *omnium namque virtutum generatrix, custos moderatrixque discretio est* | Latin verified in *Collatio* II (Perseids/Documenta Catholica Omnia); the English is the entry's own rendering, not a published translation, and the standard NPNF (Gibson) reads "**discretion is the mother of all virtues, as well as their guardian and regulator**" (Conference 2, ch. 4). No chapter number is given on the page. Propose citing "Các Đàm Luận II.4 / Conference 2.4". | `confirmed` (citation incomplete) | [Latin, Perseids CTS *Collatio* 2.3–2.5](http://cts.perseids.org/read/latinLit/stoa0076c/stoa001/opp-lat2/2.3.2-2.5.4), [NPNF, Conference 2 (New Advent)](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/350802.htm) |
| cross-file `church-fathers.json` | `controversy_councils`: "corrected at the Second Council of Orange (529 AD), **decades** after his death" | Contradicts the per-slug file's "nearly a century after his death". 435 → 529 is 94 years. The per-slug wording is right; the combined file is loose. | `corrected` | (internal; arithmetic) |

**Not checked:** Vietnamese forms "Gioan Cassianô", "Evagriô Ponticô", "Germanô", "Scythia Nhỏ" against hdgmvietnam / tgpsaigon (they are absent from the site's own Catechism content, and I did not get to the external Vietnamese authorities). The claim that Aquinas "cites Cassian's teaching on **discretion**" in the *Summa* — I confirmed he cites the *Institutes* on the vices/acedia (ST II-II q.35) but did not trace a discretion citation. The Ignatius/*discretio* lineage claim (a historiographical judgement rather than a checkable fact). The 8-vices → 7-deadly-sins condensation. `ccc_refs` is empty — nothing to check.

**Keep for `sources` retrofit:** [Conferences, NPNF / New Advent](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/350801.htm) · [Decretum Gelasianum, Latin text (tertullian.org)](https://www.tertullian.org/decretum.htm) · [Backus & Goudriaan, "'Semipelagianism': The Origins of the Term", *Journal of Ecclesiastical History*](https://files01.core.ac.uk/download/pdf/85212815.pdf)

### 25. cyril-of-alexandria — Cyril of Alexandria

**Checked:** Both quotations traced to full texts (the `quote` to the NPNF II-14 text of the Twelve
Anathemas; the `more-quotes` passage to Cyril's Second Letter to Nestorius / Ep. 4, CPG 5304, in the
same NPNF volume) — both are genuine. Dates, Doctor-of-the-Church year, feast and succession checked
against Benedict XVI's catechesis at vatican.va; the Ephesus 431 mechanics and the Chalcedon
reception against Tanner's *Decrees of the Ecumenical Councils* (conciliar introduction + the
Definition of Chalcedon) and the 1913 CE; the Hypatia material against Socrates Scholasticus HE 7.15,
Damascius *Life of Isidore* (via the Suda) and *Codex Theodosianus* 16.2.42–43; the surviving-works
count against the Migne/PG volume lists, Schaff's *History of the Christian Church*, and the CUA
Press Festal Letters editions. All six `sections[]` bodies were read in full; that is where most of
the findings are. Vietnamese forms grepped across `content/` and checked against tgpsaigon.net,
gpcantho.com, giaophanvinhlong.net, giaophanbaria.org.

| Field | Claim as written | What the sources say | Verdict | Sources |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `sections[].later-influence` | "Twenty years after his death, the Council of Chalcedon (451)" / "Hai mươi năm sau khi ngài qua đời, Công Đồng Calcêđônia (451)" | Cyril died 27 June 444; Chalcedon opened October 451 — **seven** years after his death. "Twenty years" is the gap from Ephesus (431→451), which the `more-quotes` section states correctly ("Chalcedon would rely on twenty years later"). Plain arithmetic error. | `corrected` → en: "Seven years after his death, the Council of Chalcedon (451)…"; vi: "Bảy năm sau khi ngài qua đời, Công Đồng Calcêđônia (451)…" | [vatican.va, Benedict XVI, 3 Oct 2007](https://www.vatican.va/content/benedict-xvi/en/audiences/2007/documents/hf_ben-xvi_aud_20071003.html), [Tanner, Chalcedon 451](https://www.papalencyclicals.net/councils/ecum04.htm) |

The Third Letter (with the Twelve Chapters) is **not** named; scholarship notes Chalcedon "felt free to ignore his Twelve Chapters, despite their inclusion in the acts" of Ephesus. | `corrected` → en: "…formally reading his Second Letter to Nestorius and his letter of reunion to John of Antioch (*Laetentur caeli*) into the council's proceedings…"; vi: "…Thư Thứ Hai gửi Nestôriô và thư hợp nhất gửi Gioan thành Antiôkia (*Laetentur caeli*)…" | [Tanner, Chalcedon — Definition of the faith](https://www.papalencyclicals.net/councils/ecum04.htm), [BMCR review, *Claiming the mantle of Cyril*](https://bmcr.brynmawr.edu/2022/2022.12.06/) |
| `life[1]` (and legacy `known_for` in **both** combined files) | "**Convened** and dominated the Council of Ephesus (431 AD)" / "**Triệu tập** và chủ trì Công đồng Êphêsô (431)" | The council was summoned by **Emperor Theodosius II**, "together with his co-emperor Valentinian III and with the agreement of Pope Celestine I" (imperial letter of 19 Nov 430, convoking for 7 June 431). | `facts[]` "Surviving works" / "Tác phẩm còn lại" | "2 works" / "2 tác phẩm" | This is simply `len(works[])` — the audit-wide pattern, and here it is off by orders of magnitude. Cyril's extant corpus fills **ten volumes of Migne, PG 68–77**: commentaries on the Pentateuch (*De adoratione in spiritu et veritate*, *Glaphyra*), Isaiah, the Twelve Minor Prophets and John; a homiletic commentary on Luke; fragments on the Psalms and Pauline epistles; the *Thesaurus*; *Dialogues on the Trinity*; *Against Julian* (10 of 20 books survive); *Five Tomes Against Nestorius*; **29 surviving Festal (Paschal) Letters** (CUA Press FOTC 118 + 127); and a large letter collection. Roughly two-thirds of the extant corpus is biblical exegesis. | `corrected` → en: "A very large corpus — ten volumes of Migne (PG 68–77)"; vi: "Bộ tác phẩm đồ sộ — 10 tập trong Patrologia Graeca (PG 68–77)". | `sections[].martyrdom` | "**no ancient source claims Cyril personally ordered the murder**" / "không có nguồn cổ đại nào khẳng định Cyrilô đích thân ra lệnh giết người" | One ancient source does exactly that. **Damascius**, *Life of Isidore* (c. 530, preserved in the *Suda*), says that when Cyril saw the crowd at Hypatia's door and learned whose house it was, he "was struck with envy" and "immediately began plotting her murder." Damascius is a hostile pagan Neoplatonist writing a century later and modern historians generally discount him — which is the entry's actual point, but the sentence as written is factually false. | `corrected` → en: "no *contemporary* source claims Cyril ordered the murder; the one ancient text that does — the pagan philosopher Damascius, writing a century later and preserved in the *Suda* — is a hostile polemic that modern historians generally discount"; vi correspondingly. | [Damascius, *Life of Isidore* on Hypatia (text, UMass Lowell)](https://faculty.uml.edu/ethan_spanier/Teaching/documents/TheLifeofHypatia.pdf), [*The Killing of Hypatia*, Lapham's Quarterly](https://www.laphamsquarterly.org/roundtable/killing-hypatia) |
| `sections[].martyrdom` | "he **faced no real consequence, from either the Church or the state**, for the climate his own conduct had helped create" | Overstated in the state's half. | `sections[].context` | "a mob of Christians **widely believed to include Parabalani**" | Socrates names the ringleader as "**Peter the reader**" and a mob of "Christian populace"; John of Nikiu (7th c., Coptic, who *approves* of the killing) names "Peter the magistrate" and "a multitude of believers". **No ancient source connects the parabalani to Hypatia's death**; the identification is a modern inference resting largely on the timing of CTh 16.2.42 in 416. "Widely believed" is defensible hedging but understates the gap. | `contested` → suggest "…by a Christian mob led, per Socrates, by a reader named Peter — often, though on no ancient testimony, identified with the Parabalani". | [Socrates HE 7.15 (Fordham)](https://sourcebooks.web.fordham.edu/source/hypatia.asp), [CTh 16.2.42 (Latin Library)](https://www.thelatinlibrary.com/theodosius/theod16.shtml) |
| `dates.display` / `dates.born` / `facts[]` "Born" | "c. 375 — 444" · `born: 375` · "c. 375/376 AD" | False precision.
