The entry's `display` ("c. 375") and `facts` ("375/376") also disagree with each other on the second figure. | `contested` → propose display "c. 375/378 — 444" and facts en "c. 375–380 (scholars differ; McGuckin: c. 378)" / vi "khoảng năm 375–380 (học giới chưa thống nhất; McGuckin: khoảng 378)". `born` integer stays 375 as an index value. | [vatican.va, Benedict XVI, 3 Oct 2007](https://www.vatican.va/content/benedict-xvi/en/audiences/2007/documents/hf_ben-xvi_aud_20071003.html), [McGuckin, *St Cyril of Alexandria and the Christological Controversy* (SVS Press) — cited for c. 378](https://www.mdpi.com/2077-1444/15/6/688) |
| `sections[]` — **omission**: Ephesus mechanics | The entry's only account of Ephesus is `life[1]`: "Convened and dominated… affirmed Mary's title as Theotokos." Nothing on what actually happened. | The mechanics the brief asks about are entirely absent. Tanner: Nestorius was summoned three times, refused, was judged, and 197 bishops subscribed at once on 22 June — before the Roman legates or the easterners arrived. The legates on arriving joined Cyril and confirmed the sentence. **John of Antioch and the easterners then arrived, refused communion with Cyril, and set up a rival council which deposed Cyril and Memnon of Ephesus**; the Cyrilline council excommunicated John and his party at its fifth session on 17 July. Theodosius "approved neither" council; per the 1913 CE the imperial count treated Nestorius, Cyril and Memnon alike as "lawfully deposed" and held them in custody. Pope Sixtus III approved the decrees after 31 July 432. | `corrected` → propose adding a short factual paragraph covering the rival synod, the mutual depositions and the emperor's initial refusal to ratify either side. | [Tanner, Ephesus 431 — introduction](https://www.papalencyclicals.net/councils/ecum03.htm), [1913 CE, *St. Cyril of Alexandria*](https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/04592b.htm) |
| `sections[]` — **omission**: Formula of Reunion (433) | Not mentioned anywhere in the entry. `later-influence` jumps straight from 431 to 451. | The reunion is a documented, dated act: **23 April 433**, Cyril and John of Antioch made peace, John sending Paul of Emesa to Alexandria as plenipotentiary; Cyril's letter accepting John's profession (*Laetentur caeli*) is one of the documents Tanner prints with Ephesus, and it is one of the two Cyrilline letters Chalcedon later received. Benedict XVI: "By 433 Cyril was nevertheless already able to achieve a theological formula of compromise and reconciliation with the Antiocheans." What Cyril conceded: he accepted an Antiochene profession using **"union of two natures"** and "two natures" language. What he did **not** concede: he never withdrew the Twelve Anathemas — hence the lasting ambiguity that Chalcedon resolved by receiving the reunion letter and quietly passing the Anathemas over. Omitting this is what leaves the `later-influence` narrative able to claim the Third Letter was received at Chalcedon. | `corrected` → propose adding the 433 reunion, with the date 23 April 433 and both halves (what was conceded, what was not). | [Tanner, Ephesus 431 — introduction and Formula of Union](https://www.papalencyclicals.net/councils/ecum03.htm), [vatican.va, Benedict XVI, 3 Oct 2007](https://www.vatican.va/content/benedict-xvi/en/audiences/2007/documents/hf_ben-xvi_aud_20071003.html) |
| `works[1]` description in **both** legacy files (`church-fathers.json`, `church-fathers-vi.json`) | "Second and Third Letters to Nestorius — **Formally endorsed as authoritative statements of orthodox Christology at Ephesus**" / "Được chính thức công nhận là tuyên bố chính thống về Ngôi Vị Đức Kitô tại Công đồng Êphêsô" | Only the **Second** Letter was so endorsed — "declared by the fathers to be in agreement with Nicaea". Cyril's twelve anathemas (i.e. the Third Letter) were merely "**read at Ephesus and included in the proceedings**", and were **not officially approved** by the council. This is the same error the per-slug `later-influence` makes about Chalcedon, so a fix must be applied in all three files. | `corrected` → restrict the endorsement claim to the Second Letter; say of the Third that it was read into the acts but not formally approved. | [Tanner, Ephesus 431 — introduction](https://www.papalencyclicals.net/councils/ecum03.htm), [Tanner, Chalcedon — Definition](https://www.papalencyclicals.net/councils/ecum04.htm) |
| Entry-wide: whether the entry distinguishes what Ephesus **defined** from popular belief | `life[1]`: the council "affirmed Mary's title as Theotokos"; `more-quotes` correctly notes the Second Letter was "read into and formally adopted by the Council of Ephesus itself". | Half-right, and the missing half is the popular misconception. Ephesus promulgated **no christological creed or definition of its own**: it condemned Nestorius, approved Cyril's Second Letter as agreeing with Nicaea, and in its decree on faith of the sixth session (**22 July 431**) forbade producing any creed other than that of Nicaea. The technical christological definition people commonly credit to Ephesus is **Chalcedon's**. The entry never says this. | `corrected` → propose one added sentence in `theology` or `later-influence`: Ephesus issued no new creed; it condemned Nestorius and ratified Cyril's Second Letter, and forbade any creed other than Nicaea. | [Tanner, Ephesus 431 — documents and decree on faith](https://www.papalencyclicals.net/councils/ecum03.htm) |
| Vietnamese spelling, entry-wide | `name.vi` and `apologetics` use **"Cyrillô"** (4×); every `sections[]` body uses **"Cyrilô"** (26×). | Both forms are attested in Vietnamese Catholic usage ("Cyrilô" at dongten.net), so neither is wrong in itself — but the file is internally inconsistent, and out of step with the rest of the site: `content/content.json` (3×), `cyril-of-jerusalem.json` (25×), `ambrose-of-milan.json` (1×) and **both** legacy datasets all use "Cyrillô", as do tgpsaigon.net, gpcantho.com, giaophanvinhlong.net and giaophanbaria.org for this saint's 27 June feast. | `corrected` → normalise the 26 `sections[]` occurrences to **"Cyrillô"**. | [TGP Sài Gòn, 27/6 Thánh Cyrillô Alexandria](https://tgpsaigon.net/bai-viet/ngay-27-6-thanh-cyrillo-alexandriatien-si-hoi-thanh-48352), [GP Cần Thơ, 27/6](https://gpcantho.com/ngay-27-thang-06-thanh-cyrillo-alexandria-giam-muc-tien-si-hoi-thanh/), [GP Vĩnh Long](https://giaophanvinhlong.net/thanh-cyrillo-alexandrino-giam-muc-tien-si-hoi-thanh-444/) |
| Cross-entry name collision (flagged per task) | This entry never names John of Antioch in Vietnamese — but entry **22** `john-chrysostom.json` carries `name.vi` = **"Gioan thành Antiôkia"** for **Chrysostom**. | The corrections proposed above (Chalcedon's letter "to the Orientals"; the 433 Formula of Reunion) both require naming **Gioan thành Antiôkia** — the Antiochene patriarch, a different man. Applying them while entry 22 stands would make the site call two different Fathers by the same Vietnamese name in the same era. Chrysostom's standard Vietnamese Catholic name is **Gioan Kim Khẩu**. Fix entry 22 first. | `corrected` (in entry 22, not here) → `name.vi`: "Gioan Kim Khẩu". Flagged so entry 25's fixes are not applied before it. | in-repo: `content/giao-phu/john-chrysostom.json`, `content/giao-phu/church-fathers-vi.json` |
| `quote` (citation precision only) | source: "First Anathema Against Nestorius" / "Chương Kết Án Thứ Nhất chống Nestôriô" | **The wording is genuine.** NPNF II-14: "If anyone will not confess that the Emmanuel is very God, and that therefore the Holy Virgin is the Mother of God (Θεοτόκος), inasmuch as in the flesh she bore the Word of God made flesh… let him be anathema." The entry's "Theotokos" instead of "Mother of God" matches the Tanner-style rendering, and the ellipsis elides real text. Only the citation could be sharper: the anathemas are the appendix to the **Third Letter to Nestorius** (Ep. 17, November 430). | `confirmed` (noted so a later pass does not "fix" a sound quote) → optionally extend the citation to "First Anathema, appended to the Third Letter to Nestorius (430)". | [NPNF II-14, *The Twelve Anathemas of St. Cyril Against Nestorius*](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/3810.htm) |
| `sections[].more-quotes` (Second Letter passage) | The long "We do not say that the nature of the Word was changed…" quotation, dated "early 430", said to have been "read into and formally adopted by the Council of Ephesus itself" | **All three elements check out.** The English matches the NPNF text of Cyril's Second Letter to Nestorius (Ep. 4, CPG 5304); the letter is dated late January–mid February 430; and Tanner confirms Ephesus declared it "to be in agreement with Nicaea". | `confirmed` (notable — the entry's densest quotation, and it is sound) | [NPNF II-14, Epistle of Cyril to Nestorius](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/3810.htm), [Fourth Century Christianity, Letter 4 (CPG 5304)](https://www.fourthcentury.com/cyril-of-alexandria-letter-4-2nd-letter-to-nestorius-cpg-5304/), [Tanner, Ephesus 431](https://www.papalencyclicals.net/councils/ecum03.htm) |
| `sections[].theology` (mia physis / Apollinarius) | Cyril's "mia physis tou theou logou sesarkomene" was in fact drawn from a work by **Apollinarius of Laodicea** that Cyril believed to be Athanasius's | **Correct, and worth protecting.** The formula comes from Apollinarius's *Letter to Jovian*, which circulated under Athanasius's name in the Alexandrian archives. One caveat the entry could note: a minority of scholars argue Cyril's use derives from Nicene vocabulary rather than the forgery, and that Cyril cited that text for its use of *Theotokos*, not for the formula. | `confirmed`, with an optional hedge on the minority view | [McGuckin, *St Cyril's Miaphysite Christology and Chalcedonian Dyophysitism* (PDF)](https://classicalchristianity.com/wp-content/uploads/2016/05/St.-Cyril-of-Alexandria%E2%80%99s-Miaphysite-Christology-and-Chalcedonian-Dyophysitism.pdf), [dissenting view (Academia.edu paper)](https://www.academia.edu/80415933/) |

Also confirmed and needing no change: succession to his uncle Theophilus in 412; death 27 June 444;
32-year episcopate (412–444, strictly 31 yrs 8 months — acceptable rounding, and the Vietnamese
sources round the same way); Doctor of the Church **1882 by Leo XIII**; feast **27 June** in the
current General Roman Calendar (Eastern commemorations differ — 9 June, and 18 January with
Athanasius — but the entry claims nothing about them); the expulsion of Alexandria's Jews in 414–415;
Hypatia killed in March 415, dragged from her carriage, stripped, and killed inside the Caesareum
church; the prefect Orestes; the Parabalani as an officially recognised order of laymen for the sick.

**Not checked:** the two `apologetics[]` answers — the Eucharist claim in particular would need a
traced passage (Cyril's *Commentary on John* or the eleventh anathema on the "life-giving flesh") and
I did not locate one; `ccc_refs` is empty (nothing to check); the `further-reading` bibliography's
publication details (Brill 1994 / SVS 2004; Russell, Routledge 2000) were not verified against
publisher records; the claim that the Church of the East spread "as far as India and China"; the
legacy files' nicknames "Pillar of Faith" / "Doctor of the Incarnation"; whether Cyril's Luke
commentary survives principally in Syriac; the portrait's licence and attribution.

**Keep for `sources` retrofit:** [vatican.va — Benedict XVI, catechesis on St Cyril of Alexandria, 3 Oct 2007](https://www.vatican.va/content/benedict-xvi/en/audiences/2007/documents/hf_ben-xvi_aud_20071003.html) · [Tanner, *Decrees of the Ecumenical Councils* — Ephesus 431](https://www.papalencyclicals.net/councils/ecum03.htm) · [NPNF II-14 — Cyril's letters and the Twelve Anathemas](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/3810.htm)

---

### 26. leo-the-great — Leo the Great

**Checked:** Both quotations traced to Leo, *Ep.* 28 (the Tome) in NPNF II.12, and the Sermon 3.4 Petrine passage to the NPNF text of Sermon 3; the Chalcedon acclamation and its qualifications against the Session II acts and the Price & Gaddis scholarship; the Attila episode against Benedict XVI's catechesis on vatican.va, Prosper, Jordanes/Priscus and Hydatius; Doctor-of-the-Church year, death date, feast day and burial against vatican.va and reference works; the `works[]`/`facts[]` works count against the actual surviving corpus; Vietnamese terms compared with the site's own `content/cong-dong/chalcedon.json`; both entries in `church-fathers.json` / `church-fathers-vi.json` compared.

| Field | Claim as written | What the sources say | Verdict | Sources |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `life[1]` and `sections.later-influence` | "Met Attila the Hun **outside Rome** in 452" / "gặp Attila người Hung **ở ngoại ô Rôma**" | **Wrong place.** The meeting was in **northern Italy, at the river Mincio near Mantua** — c. 500 km from Rome. Prosper places it "in agro Venetum … ubi Mincius amne"; Benedict XVI's own catechesis says Leo "met Attila, chief of the Huns, **in Mantua**". Propose: "gặp Attila bên sông Mincio, gần Mantua, miền bắc nước Ý, năm 452" / "met Attila at the river Mincio near Mantua in northern Italy in 452". Same error in the combined `church-fathers.json` (`known_for`). | `corrected` | [Benedict XVI, General Audience 5 Mar 2008 (vatican.va)](https://www.vatican.va/content/benedict-xvi/en/audiences/2008/documents/hf_ben-xvi_aud_20080305.html), [Prosper, *Epitoma chronicon* s.a. 452 (Murray trans., discussion)](https://dokumen.pub/the-fifth-century-chroniclers-prosper-hydatius-and-the-gallic-chronicle-of-452-0905205766-9780905205762.html) |
| `sections.later-influence` | "an embassy **from the bishop of Rome** was still a real factor"; "Leo alone … saves the city" is the popular version | The embassy was an **imperial legation sent by Valentinian III**, of **three** envoys: Leo, the ex-prefect **Trygetius**, and the consul **Gennadius Avienus**. Prosper's specific documented bias is precisely that he credits Leo alone and **omits Trygetius and Avienus** — a sharper and more citable point than the entry's general "hagiographic bias". Propose naming the two co-envoys. | `corrected` | [Fifth-Century Chroniclers (Muhlberger), on Prosper s.a. 452](https://dokumen.pub/the-fifth-century-chroniclers-prosper-hydatius-and-the-gallic-chronicle-of-452-0905205766-9780905205762.html), [Benedict XVI, GA 5 Mar 2008](https://www.vatican.va/content/benedict-xvi/en/audiences/2008/documents/hf_ben-xvi_aud_20080305.html) |
| `facts[]` "Doctor of the Church" | `Có` / `Yes` — no year, no pope | Verified at vatican.va: **Pope Benedict XIV proclaimed Leo a Doctor of the Church in 1754.** The audit spec asks for the year and pope on every Doctor row. Propose `Có — do Đức Bênêđictô XIV tuyên phong năm 1754` / `Yes — declared by Benedict XIV, 1754` | `corrected` | [Benedict XVI, General Audience 5 Mar 2008 (vatican.va)](https://www.vatican.va/content/benedict-xvi/en/audiences/2008/documents/hf_ben-xvi_aud_20080305.html) |
| `sections.theology` | "Leo's Christology **in the Tome** … joined \"without confusion, without change, without division, without separation\"" | Those four adverbs (ἀσυγχύτως, ἀτρέπτως, ἀδιαιρέτως, ἀχωρίστως) are from the **Definition of Chalcedon**, not from Leo's Tome — the quotation marks misattribute a conciliar text to Leo. Propose: "…công thức mà Công đồng Chalcêđônia sau đó đúc kết thành 'không hòa lẫn, không biến đổi, không phân chia, không tách biệt'" / "…which Chalcedon then crystallised in the Definition's 'without confusion, without change, without division, without separation'". | `corrected` | [Encyclopedia.com, Council of Chalcedon](https://www.encyclopedia.com/philosophy-and-religion/christianity/roman-catholic-and-orthodox-churches-councils-and-treaties/council-chalcedon), [Acts of Chalcedon extracts, NPNF (New Advent)](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/3811.htm) |
| `quote` (main) | "Each nature, retaining its own proper character without loss, unites with the other in one Person." Source given only as "The Tome of Leo" | The sense is right and traceable to Tome §3 (*salva … proprietate utriusque naturae et substantiae et in unam coeunte personam*), but it is the entry's own paraphrase, not a published translation; NPNF (Feltoe) reads "**Without detriment therefore to the properties of either nature and substance which then came together in one person, majesty took on humility, strength weakness, eternity mortality.**" Also the citation lacks the section. Propose citing "Thư Tôma (Thư 28), đoạn 3 / Tome of Leo (Letter 28), §3" and, if a citable translation is wanted, the NPNF wording. | `contested` (paraphrase, under-cited) | [Leo, *Ep.* 28 (Tome), NPNF II.12](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/3604028.htm) |
| `sections.more-quotes` + `apologetics[0]` | The Tome read at Chalcedon → the bishops' acclamation, presented as immediate and unanimous ("Phêrô đã nói qua Lêô") | The acclamation is genuine and in the acts — but the entry omits the half that popular accounts drop: **during the reading, three passages of the Tome were challenged as Nestorian by Illyrian and Palestinian bishops; at the motion of Atticus of Nicopolis the council adjourned and a committee under Anatolius of Constantinople was given five days to test the Tome against Cyril (the Twelve Chapters) before it was subscribed.** The committee found it agreed with Cyril, Nicaea and Ephesus. Propose adding a clause — it strengthens rather than weakens the apologetic, since the Tome passed a hostile examination. | `contested` (materially incomplete) | [Romanides, "St Cyril's One Physis … and Chalcedon"](https://greekorthodoxchurch.org/romanity_org_files/htm/rom.08.en.st._cyrils_one_physis_or_hypostasis_of_god_the_log.html), [Acts of Chalcedon, Session II extracts (NPNF)](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/3811.htm), [Price & Gaddis, *Acts of the Council of Chalcedon* (TTH 45)](https://www.cambridge.org/core/services/aop-cambridge-core/content/view/A987B6A925EF37B4D17AC7601C459D06/S0009840X0800098Xa.pdf/) |

Propose filling them in for consistency. | `corrected` | (internal — `content/cong-dong/chalcedon.json`) |
| `sections.later-influence` | "Raphael's 16th-century fresco … **added** an entirely unhistorical detail — Saints Peter and Paul appearing in the sky above Leo, swords drawn" | The detail is unhistorical, but Raphael did not add it — he **depicted a legend that already existed**. Propose "đã vẽ lại một truyền thuyết có sẵn" / "depicted an already-current legend" instead of "added". (Fresco: 1514, Raphael with Giulio Romano, Stanza di Eliodoro — the entry's "16th-century" is right.) | `corrected` | [Vatican Museums, *Encounter of Leo the Great with Attila*](https://www.museivaticani.va/content/museivaticani/en/collezioni/musei/stanze-di-raffaello/stanza-di-eliodoro/incontro-di-leone-magno-con-attila.html) |
| `sections.later-influence` | "the historian **Priscus** … attributing Attila's withdrawal to superstitious fear (his men reportedly recalled that Alaric had died shortly after sacking Rome) and to … plague and food shortages … and a renewed threat from the Eastern Roman army" | Source attribution is muddled. Priscus survives only in fragments and *via* **Jordanes**, *Getica* (c. 551), which is where the Alaric-superstition motif is actually read; the plague/famine and Marcian's Danube counter-attack come from **Hydatius**' chronicle. Propose naming Jordanes (drawing on Priscus) and Hydatius rather than putting all of it on Priscus. | `contested` (mis-attributed) | [Britannica on Jordanes and his use of Priscus](https://www.britannica.com/biography/Jordanes), [Jordanes, *Getica* (ToposText)](https://topostext.org/work/744) |

"c. 400" is acceptable as display but the bare integer `400` is a placeholder, not a datum. The site's own Councils section already has the right form: `content/cong-dong/chalcedon.json` → `"Tông thư của thánh Lêô Cả gửi Flaviano"`, latin `"Tomus ad Flavianum"`. Propose adopting that: `Tông thư gửi Flavianô (Tomus ad Flavianum)`. Same fix needed in `church-fathers-vi.json` (4 places). | `corrected` | (internal — `content/cong-dong/chalcedon.json`, `content/cac-thanh/toma-tran-van-thien.json`) |
| VN terminology — internal inconsistency | Same file spells Chalcedon **"Calcêđônia"** (`life[1].vi`, both `apologetics[].vi`) and **"Chalcêđônia"** (all `sections[].vi` bodies) | The Councils section (`content/cong-dong/chalcedon.json`) uses **"Chalcêđônia"** throughout. Pick one; propose "Chalcêđônia" to match. Likewise `works[0]` says "Flaviano" while the bodies say "Flavianô". | `corrected` | (internal — `content/cong-dong/chalcedon.json`) |
| `facts[]` "Surviving works" + `works[]` | `1 work` / `1 tác phẩm`; `works[]` lists only the Tome | Grossly understated — the count is just `works[].length` (site-wide defect). Leo's surviving corpus is roughly **96–97 sermons and 143 letters** (of which the Tome is *Ep.* 28). Propose `Khoảng 96 bài giảng và 143 lá thư` / `c. 96 sermons and 143 letters`, and adding `Bài Giảng / Sermones (Tractatus septem et nonaginta)` and `Các Thư / Epistulae` to `works[]`. | `corrected` | [Benedict XVI, GA 5 Mar 2008 (vatican.va)](https://www.vatican.va/content/benedict-xvi/en/audiences/2008/documents/hf_ben-xvi_aud_20080305.html), [NPNF II.12 — Leo's Letters and Sermons](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/3604.htm) |

**Confirmed and should NOT be "fixed" later:** the `more-quotes` passage "The same eternal, only-begotten of the eternal begetter was born of the Holy Spirit and the Virgin Mary. His birth in time in no way subtracts from or adds to that divine and eternal birth of his" is a faithful rendering of Tome §2 (Latin: *Idem sempiternus ex sempiterno parente unigenitus, natus est de Spiritu sancto et Maria virgine; quae nativitas temporalis illi nativitati divinae et sempiternae nihil minuit, nihil contulit*) — NPNF renders it differently but the sense is right. The Sermon 3.4 citation is correct (NPNF: "in my humble person he may be recognized and honoured... whose dignity is not abated even in so unworthy an heir"), though the entry's English is again its own paraphrase, not a published translation. Feast day 10 November is correct on the current General Roman Calendar (before 1969 it was 11 April). Death 10 November 461, burial in the vestibule of Old St Peter's as the first pope buried in the basilica complex, and Sergius I's translation of the relics in 688, all check out. The "Robber Council" of 449, Leo's *Latrocinium* label, and Flavian's death shortly afterwards from the violence, all check out.

**Not checked:** Whether Prosper of Aquitaine was in Leo's chancery (bears on how much weight "he was not present" should carry — I could not settle it). The precise wording of the Chalcedon acclamation in Price & Gaddis (I worked from the NPNF Session II extracts plus secondary discussion; the full TTH 45 text is not online). The claim that Leo's Petrine theology "goes well beyond anything in earlier writers like Clement of Rome" (a comparative judgement). The Vandal conquest of North Africa date relative to 440 (Carthage 439 — consistent, but not separately verified). `ccc_refs` is empty — nothing to check.

**Keep for `sources` retrofit:** [Benedict XVI, General Audience, 5 March 2008 (vatican.va)](https://www.vatican.va/content/benedict-xvi/en/audiences/2008/documents/hf_ben-xvi_aud_20080305.html) · [Leo, *Ep.* 28 — the Tome, NPNF II.12](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/3604028.htm) · [Acts of the Council of Chalcedon, Session II extracts (NPNF)](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/3811.htm)

### 27. gregory-the-great — Gregory the Great

**Checked:** Both quotations traced to full texts — the `quote` field is a papal title, not a passage, and the source line says so; the Pastoral Rule quotation verified verbatim against Barmby's NPNF translation (Bk I ch. 1), and the "universal priest" quotation verified verbatim against Barmby's *Registrum Epistolarum* VII.33 (which turns out to be addressed to Emperor Maurice, not to John the Faster). Dates, feast day, and the September-3/March-12 calendar move checked against several independent lives; the Augustine-of-Canterbury mission checked for the send/arrive years and the "forty monks" figure; the Gregorian-chant attribution checked against modern musicology (Apel/Snow, Carolingian synthesis c. 750, the Gregory II hypothesis); *servus servorum Dei* checked for the "every pope since" claim; the *Dialogues*' authorship checked against the Clark controversy and the scholarly response to it. Biography in `context` spot-checked (prefect of Rome 573, apocrisiarius from 579, Pelagius II dead of plague 590, exarch at Ravenna) and holds. `ccc_refs` empty. Cross-file: `church-fathers.json` / `church-fathers-vi.json` id 27 carry the *same* text and so repeat the same issues (the 597 date, the chant credit, "every pope today") — no contradictions between files, but any fix must be applied in all three.


| Field | Claim as written | What the sources say | Verdict | Sources |
|---|---|---|---|---|

| `sections[more-quotes]` (Pastoral Rule quote) | "No one presumes to teach an art till he has first, with intent meditation, learned it… the government of souls is the art of arts!" It is *Registrum Epistolarum* Book VII, Letter 33, **addressed to the Emperor Maurice** — not to John the Faster. Worth citing, since this is the passage Orthodox/Protestant apologists press. | `confirmed` (add citation) | [New Advent, Reg. Ep. VII.33](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/360207033.htm), [Christian History Institute](https://christianhistoryinstitute.org/incontext/article/gregory-the-great) |
| `sections[more-quotes]` / `apologetics[0]` | "began signing his own letters 'servant of the servants of God' … **a title every pope has used since**" / vi "vẫn được mọi Giáo hoàng dùng đến ngày nay" | Overclaim on the "every pope since" half. Gregory was the first to use it *extensively*, "but he was imitated by his successors, **though not invariably till the ninth century**", and "since the twelfth century it is used exclusively by the pope." The same article notes Pope John III already used the phrase in a bull of 570, so Gregory was not strictly the first. (Caveat: the 1913 Catholic Encyclopedia is the common ancestor of most web statements of this, incl. Catholic Answers' reprint — treat as one source, not two.) | `corrected` → e.g. en "…a title his successors gradually took up and that has been used by popes alone since the twelfth century"; vi "…một danh hiệu mà các Giáo hoàng kế nhiệm dần dần dùng theo và từ thế kỷ XII trở đi chỉ dành riêng cho Giáo hoàng" | [1913 Catholic Encyclopedia, "Servus Servorum Dei"](https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/13737a.htm), [Catholic Answers Encyclopedia (reprint of the same)](https://www.catholic.com/encyclopedia/servus-servorum-dei) |
| `works[]` / `facts[]` "Surviving works" | `works[]` lists 3 (Regula Pastoralis, Moralia in Job, Dialogues); `facts[]` says "3 tác phẩm / 3 works" | The count is just `works[].length` — the site-wide defect. Gregory's surviving corpus is far larger: besides those three there are ~850+ letters in the *Registrum Epistolarum* (14 books), 40 *Homiliae in Evangelia*, 22 *Homiliae in Hiezechihelem*, and a commentary on 1 Kings / Song of Songs material of disputed authorship. The letters are the single largest surviving body of his writing and are the source of the "universal priest" passage the entry itself quotes. | `corrected` → drop the numeric count or replace with e.g. en "Regula Pastoralis, Moralia in Job, the Dialogues, ~850 letters, and two collections of homilies"; vi "Tu Luật Mục Vụ, Moralia in Job, Các Đối Thoại, khoảng 850 lá thư, và hai bộ bài giảng" | [New Advent, Registrum Epistolarum (14 books)](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/3602.htm), [New Advent, Reg. Ep. VII.33](https://www.newadvent.org/fathers/360207033.htm) |
| `works[2]` Dialogues (`Các Đối Thoại`) | listed flatly as Gregory's work, no note | Authorship is genuinely contested in modern scholarship: since the 1980s Francis Clark has argued the *Dialogues* is a c. 680 compilation by a forger ("the Dialogist") who spliced in genuine Gregorian passages (~25% of the text). **The majority of Gregory scholars reject Clark** (Paul Meyvaert, Adalbert de Vogüé), so the traditional attribution stands — but the entry asserts where the field hedges. | `contested` → add a `works_note`, e.g. en "The *Dialogues*' Gregorian authorship, though accepted by most scholars, has been disputed since the 1980s (Francis Clark)"; vi "Quyền tác giả của Grêgôriô đối với *Các Đối Thoại*, tuy được đa số học giả nhìn nhận, đã bị Francis Clark đặt vấn đề từ thập niên 1980." | [Meyvaert, "The Enigma of Gregory the Great's Dialogues: A Response to Francis Clark", *JEH*](https://www.cambridge.org/core/journals/journal-of-ecclesiastical-history/article/abs/enigma-of-gregory-the-greats-dialogues-a-response-to-francis-clark1/EFFE1E0DA29BDAD7CCBE060E0C8AF0BD), [The Medieval Review, review of Clark, *The 'Gregorian' Dialogues*](https://scholarworks.iu.edu/journals/index.php/tmr/article/view/15811/0) |
| `life[1]` (Gregorian chant) | en "Credited with organizing Gregorian chant"; vi "Được ghi công tổ chức và **hệ thống hóa** nghi thức thánh ca mang tên ngài — Thánh ca Grêgôriô" | This is the entry's own headline summary and it contradicts the entry's `later-influence` section, which correctly reports that "modern musicology places the core chant repertoire's actual formation later, largely in 8th-century Francia." Scholarly consensus (Willi Apel, Robert Snow) is that the repertoire arose c. 750 from a Carolingian synthesis of Roman and Gallican chant; the naming may honour **Gregory II** (715–731), with authorship retro-assigned to Gregory I. The vi wording "hệ thống hóa" (systematized) asserts more than the en "credited with organizing" and more than the sources support. The `later-influence` treatment is good; the `life[]` bullet is the one that needs the hedge, since it is what the summary card shows. | `corrected` → en "Long *traditionally* credited with Gregorian chant, though modern musicology dates the repertoire to 8th-century Francia (see below)"; vi "Theo truyền thống được ghi công về Thánh ca Grêgôriô, tuy âm nhạc học hiện đại xác định kho tàng này hình thành tại xứ Franc thế kỷ VIII (xem bên dưới)" | [New World Encyclopedia, "Gregorian chant"](https://www.newworldencyclopedia.org/entry/Gregorian_chant), [OrthodoxWiki, "Gregorian Chant"](https://orthodoxwiki.org/Gregorian_Chant) |
| `life[1]` and `sections[later-influence]` — mission date | "Sent St. Augustine of Canterbury to evangelize the Anglo-Saxons in England (**597 AD**)" / "the **597 mission** to England: he personally selected and dispatched Augustine … and roughly 40 monks" | False precision by a year on the *sending*. Gregory selected "a company of about forty" and "the little company set out upon their long journey in the month of **June, 596**"; they landed on Thanet in **spring 597**. So: dispatched 596, arrived 597. The "roughly 40 monks" figure is confirmed. | `corrected` → en "Sent St. Augustine of Canterbury … (dispatched 596, arrived in Kent 597)"; vi "…(sai đi năm 596, đến Kent năm 597)" | [1913 Catholic Encyclopedia, "St. Augustine of Canterbury"](https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/02081a.htm), [OrthodoxWiki, "Augustine of Canterbury"](https://orthodoxwiki.org/Augustine_of_Canterbury) |
| `facts[]` "Tiến sĩ Hội Thánh" / "Doctor of the Church" | just "Có / Yes" | Not wrong, but no year/pope. Gregory is one of the four original Latin Doctors named by **Boniface VIII**; the sources split between **1295** (the decretal *Gloriosus*, ordering the feasts of the four Doctors kept as *duplex*) and **1298** (the year the *Liber Sextus*, which contains it, was promulgated — cap. "Gloriosus", de reliq. et vener. sanctorum, in Sexto, III, 22). | `contested` → hedge, e.g. vi "Có — Đức Bônifaxiô VIII, năm 1295 (sắc lệnh *Gloriosus*, ban hành trong *Liber Sextus* năm 1298)"; en "Yes — named by Boniface VIII, 1295 (decretal *Gloriosus*, promulgated in the *Liber Sextus*, 1298)" | [1913 Catholic Encyclopedia, "Doctors of the Church"](https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/05075a.htm), [Verbum, "The Doctors of the Church: A History"](https://blog.verbum.com/2018/03/the-doctors-of-the-church-a-history/) |
| `apologetics[0].q.vi` / `apologetics[1].q.vi` | "**Thánh Thánh** dạy gì về…" (both rows) | The Father's name has dropped out of the Vietnamese question — it reads "Saint Saint teaches what about…". Entry 28 has the correct pattern ("Thánh Isiđôrô dạy gì về…"). Reported as a data defect, not a style note. | `corrected` → "Thánh Grêgôriô dạy gì về…" | — (internal; compare `content/giao-phu/isidore-of-seville.json` `apologetics[].q.vi`) |

**Not checked:** The `theology` and `further-reading` sections were read but not source-verified line by line (bibliographic details of the Davis 1950 and Straw 1988 editions not confirmed against a catalogue). Not verified: the *Moralia in Job*'s composition dates (the `works[].date` fields are empty, so nothing was claimed). ### 28. isidore-of-seville — Isidore of Seville

**Checked:** The `quote` traced to the Latin text of the *Etymologiae* (LacusCurtius/Penelope and The Latin Library) — the cited chapter is wrong. The `more-quotes` Latin (*Historia est narratio rei gestae…*, Etym. I.41) verified verbatim; the section's own hedge about the "eyewitness" sentence checked and found to be an over-hedge (the sentence is verbatim at I.41.2). Dates, feast day, canonization and Doctor-of-the-Church years verified; Braulio's role (Isidore sent the unedited work to him; Braulio divided it into 20 books after his death) verified against Barney et al. and the prefatory letters; the Fourth Council of Toledo (633) verified for Isidore's presidency, the 86 bishops, the cathedral-schools canon **and the Jewish canons the entry omits**; the "patron of the internet" hedge verified and found accurate; the pre-1000 manuscript count verified (Anspach ~300, a newer survey ~450 — "several hundred" is right); Dante's placement of Isidore in *Paradiso* X confirmed. Vietnamese terminology: "Isiđôrô" is the standard form across Vietnamese diocesan sites (Đà Nẵng, Long Xuyên, Bắc Ninh, Huế, Kon Tum, Đà Lạt) — no finding. `ccc_refs` empty. Cross-file: `church-fathers.json` / `church-fathers-vi.json` id 28 match the per-slug file (same wrong `Etymologiae, I.29` citation, same 2-work list) — no contradictions, but fixes must be applied in all three. Note the entry does **not** use the "last of the Western Church Fathers" framing anywhere, so there was nothing to attribute.

I.29 is *De etymologia* and opens "Etymologia est origo vocabulorum, cum vis verbi vel nominis per interpretationem colligitur" — it contains nothing resembling the quoted sentence. I.7.1** (*De nomine*): "Nomen dictum quasi notamen, quod nobis vocabulo suo res notas efficiat. **Nisi enim nomen scieris, cognitio rerum perit**" — Barney et al.: "For unless you know a name, knowledge of a thing perishes." | `corrected` → change the citation to `Etymologiae, I.7.1`, and consider tightening the rendering: vi "Nếu không biết tên gọi, tri thức về sự vật cũng tiêu tan"; en "For unless you know a name, knowledge of a thing perishes" | [LacusCurtius/Penelope, Etymologiae Bk I (Latin)](https://penelope.uchicago.edu/Thayer/L/Roman/Texts/Isidore/1*.html), [The Latin Library, Isidore Etym. I](https://www.thelatinlibrary.com/isidore/1.shtml) |
| `sections[more-quotes]` — the "eyewitness" hedge | "the widely circulated claim that Isidore also insisted no one may write history 'unless he was present and saw the events' is a paraphrase found in secondary summaries rather than **a sentence directly confirmed word-for-word here**" | **The entry hedges away something that is in fact verbatim in the text**, in the very chapter it is citing. Etym. I.41.2: "Apud veteres enim **nemo conscribebat historiam, nisi is qui interfuisset, et ea quae conscribenda essent vidisset.** Melius enim oculis quae fiunt deprehendimus, quam quae auditione colligimus." (The only nuance needed: Isidore states it as a report of ancient practice, not as a rule he imposes.) | `corrected` → replace the hedge with the actual text, e.g. en "He adds, of ancient practice: 'Among the ancients no one wrote history unless he had been present and had seen the things that were to be written down' (Etymologiae I.41)"; vi "Ngài còn ghi nhận về tập quán người xưa: 'Nơi người xưa, không ai viết sử trừ khi chính người ấy có mặt và đã thấy những điều cần ghi lại' (Etymologiae, I.41)" | [The Latin Library, Isidore Etym. I, ch. XLI](https://www.thelatinlibrary.com/isidore/1.shtml), [LacusCurtius/Penelope, Etymologiae Bk I](https://penelope.uchicago.edu/Thayer/L/Roman/Texts/Isidore/1*.html) |
| `sections[context]` | "his roughly **32-year** episcopate" / "triều đại giám mục kéo dài khoảng **32 năm**" | Internally inconsistent with the entry's own "succeeded Leander as bishop **around 600**" and death in **636** — that is ~35–36 years, not 32. Leander died c. 600–601 and Isidore succeeded him then. | `corrected` → en "his roughly 35-year episcopate"; vi "triều đại giám mục kéo dài khoảng 35 năm" | [Britannica, St. Isidore of Sevilla](https://www.britannica.com/biography/Saint-Isidore-of-Sevilla), [1913 Catholic Encyclopedia, "Toledo (Spain)"](https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/14755a.htm) |
| `sections[context]` + `apologetics[1]` — Fourth Council of Toledo (633) | The council is presented solely as the instrument of "integrating [Arians] fully into Catholic unity"; the apologetics answer cites only "His push at Toledo for diocesan clergy schools." | Both stated claims check out — Isidore presided over Toledo IV (86 bishops, convened by King Sisenand), and it did require bishops to establish cathedral schools. But **the entry omits the council's canons on Jews entirely.** Toledo IV canons 57–66 legislate on Jews: canon 57 forbids future forced baptism yet bars the already forcibly-baptised from returning to Judaism, and **canon 60 orders the children of crypto-Jewish parents removed and raised by Christians** — measures scholarship attributes substantially to Isidore himself. Omitting this from a page that presents the same council as a triumph of learning is this entry's largest factual gap. | `corrected` → add to `context`, e.g. en "The same council also legislated harshly against Jews — forbidding future forced baptisms while barring those already baptised by force from returning to Judaism (canon 57), and ordering the children of crypto-Jewish parents removed and raised by Christians (canon 60)"; vi "Cùng công đồng ấy cũng ra những khoản luật khắc nghiệt đối với người Do Thái — cấm ép buộc rửa tội trong tương lai nhưng đồng thời cấm những người đã bị ép rửa tội trở về Do Thái giáo (khoản 57), và buộc tách con cái của những gia đình Do Thái giữ đạo lén khỏi cha mẹ để giao cho người Kitô hữu nuôi dạy (khoản 60)." | [Nemo-Pekelman, "Divine Justice and Freedom: On Canon 57 of the Fourth Council of Toledo (633)", *Visigothic Symposia* 1 (2017)](https://visigothicsymposia.org/wp-content/uploads/2018/09/nemo-pekelman-divine-justice-and-freedom-english-visigothic-symposium-1-2017.pdf), [Christian History Institute](https://christianhistoryinstitute.org/dailystory/permalink/brilliant-bishop-isidore-held-a-church-council-in-toledo), [1913 Catholic Encyclopedia, "Toledo (Spain)"](https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/14755a.htm) |
| `facts[]` "Tác phẩm còn lại" + `works[]` | "2 tác phẩm / 2 works"; `works[]` = Etymologiae, Sententiae | Same site-wide defect: the count is just `works[].length`. Isidore's surviving corpus runs to roughly two dozen titles, including *De natura rerum*, *Chronica maiora*, *Historia de regibus Gothorum, Wandalorum et Suevorum*, *De ecclesiasticis officiis*, *De viris illustribus*, *Differentiae*, *Synonyma*, *Quaestiones in Vetus Testamentum*, *De fide catholica contra Iudaeos*, *Regula monachorum*, *De ortu et obitu patrum*, and letters. The entry's own `further-reading` cites a translation of *De natura rerum* — a work `works[]` does not list. | `corrected` → drop the numeric count, or e.g. en "~24 surviving works, chiefly the *Etymologiae*, *Sententiae*, *De natura rerum*, the *Chronica*, and the *History of the Goths*"; vi "khoảng 24 tác phẩm còn lại, chủ yếu là *Etymologiae*, *Sententiae*, *De natura rerum*, bộ *Biên niên sử*, và *Lịch sử người Goth*" | [Britannica, St. Isidore of Sevilla](https://www.britannica.com/biography/Saint-Isidore-of-Sevilla), [Barney et al., *The Etymologies of Isidore of Seville*, CUP frontmatter](https://assets.cambridge.org/97805218/37491/frontmatter/9780521837491_frontmatter.pdf) |
| `facts[]` "Tiến sĩ Hội Thánh" / "Được phong thánh" | both just "Có / Yes" | Not wrong, but the year and pope are missing and are well attested: **canonized 1598 by Clement VIII**; **declared Doctor of the Church in 1722 by Innocent XIII**. | `confirmed` (add detail) → vi "Có — Đức Innôcentê XIII tuyên phong năm 1722" / en "Yes — declared by Innocent XIII, 1722"; canonized vi "Có — Đức Clêmentê VIII, năm 1598" | [Britannica, St. Isidore of Sevilla](https://www.britannica.com/biography/Saint-Isidore-of-Sevilla), [Divine Office, Saint Isidore, Bishop and Doctor](https://divineoffice.org/about-0404-saint-isidore/) |
| `sections[later-influence]` — "patron saint of the internet" | "no Vatican document has ever formally declared Isidore patron of the internet … sometimes attributed to a supposed 1997 or 2001-era papal decision, but no such official decree exists" | **Correct — and should not be "fixed" by a later editor.** The patronage was a late-1990s grassroots/press attribution; the widely repeated "John Paul II declared him in 1997" has no supporting Vatican document, and the Pontifical Council for Social Communications' *Ethics in Internet* (2002) does not name him. Flagged only so the accurate hedge is protected. | `confirmed` | [Patheos / Fr. Matthew Schneider, "St. Isidore Is NOT the Official Patron of the Internet"](https://www.patheos.com/blogs/throughcatholiclenses/2020/05/st-isidore-of-seville-is-not-the-official-patron-of-the-internet/), [Aleteia, "A patron saint of the internet — unofficially, though"](https://aleteia.org/2020/05/02/a-patron-saint-of-the-internet-unofficially-though/) |

**Not checked:** The exact Barney et al. English wording of Etym. I.7.1 was confirmed only through a secondary academic citation of it (the Cambridge PDF would not render); the Latin itself is confirmed from two independent Latin texts. Not verified: the claim that the *Etymologiae* is "the most widely copied reference work of the entire Middle Ages" apart from the Bible — plausible and widely repeated, but I found no manuscript-census source that ranks it against all competitors, so treat it as an unquantified superlative. The `further-reading` bibliographic details (Kendall & Wallis, TTH vol. 66, 2016) were not confirmed against a publisher catalogue. The *Sententiae*'s dating and the "Augustinian vein" characterisation in `theology` were not source-checked. Isidore's `role` "Tổng giám mục / Archbishop" was not checked against contemporary Visigothic usage (he is universally called that in modern sources; the 7th-century title was metropolitan bishop of Hispalis).

**Keep for `sources` retrofit:** [The Latin Library — Etymologiae Book I (Latin)](https://www.thelatinlibrary.com/isidore/1.shtml) · [Britannica — St. Isidore of Sevilla](https://www.britannica.com/biography/Saint-Isidore-of-Sevilla) · [Nemo-Pekelman, "Divine Justice and Freedom: On Canon 57 of the Fourth Council of Toledo (633)", *Visigothic Symposia* 1 (2017)](https://visigothicsymposia.org/wp-content/uploads/2018/09/nemo-pekelman-divine-justice-and-freedom-english-visigothic-symposium-1-2017.pdf)

### 29. maximus-the-confessor — Maximus the Confessor

**Checked:** `quote` traced to *Four Hundred Chapters on Love* I.13 in the Berthold translation and both further quotations in `sections[more-quotes]` verified word-for-word against a published text of the *Chapters*; `dates`, feast days (Roman Martyrology 13 Aug / Byzantine 21 Jan) and canonization status against reference works; the origins/`role` question against the Oxford Handbook date-list, the Syriac *Life* of George of Resh'aina and Booth; the trials/exiles and the mutilation against Allen & Neil's edition of the exile dossier and CCSG 39; Constantinople III against the conciliar acts themselves; `works[]` and the surviving-works count against Yale's Maximus guide and encyclopedia entries; the Pyrrhus narrative in `sections[theology]`; `apologetics[]` (both are doctrinal glosses on the mutilation and the Martin I episode, and their factual halves check out); `ccc_refs` empty; cross-checked `church-fathers.json` / `church-fathers-vi.json` id 29 — they mirror this file exactly, no contradictions (they carry the same "exiled to North Africa, Rome" and "first secretary" problems, so any fix must be applied in all three).

| Field | Claim as written | What the sources say | Verdict | Sources |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `life[1]` / `sections[later-influence]` | "được minh oan tại Công đồng Constantinôpôli III (680-681)" / "vindicated at the Third Council of Constantinople" | The definition of faith of Constantinople III names as condemned Theodore of Pharan, Sergius, Pyrrhus, Paul and Peter of Constantinople, Pope Honorius, Cyrus of Alexandria, Macarius of Antioch and Stephen — **Maximus is not named anywhere in the acts**, nor is Pope Martin I. The council adopted his dyothelite theology and thereby vindicated his position, but it did not rehabilitate him by name; he was still regarded with some suspicion at Constantinople. `sections[later-influence]` is already carefully worded ("condemned Monothelitism and affirmed dyothelitism"), but `life[1]`'s bare "vindicated at" reads as a personal, formal rehabilitation the acts do not contain. | `contested` → hedge `life[1]`: vi "lập trường của ngài được công đồng Constantinôpôli III (680-681) xác nhận là chính thống — dù các văn kiện công đồng không nêu đích danh ngài"; en "his position was vindicated at the Third Council of Constantinople (680–681), though the council's acts do not name him" | [Acts, Const. III (papalencyclicals)](https://www.papalencyclicals.net/councils/ecum06.htm), [Third Council of Constantinople (overview)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Third_Council_of_Constantinople) |
| `facts[Tác phẩm còn lại / Surviving works]` | "3 tác phẩm" / "3 works" | The site-wide defect (this is just `works[].length`). Maximus's surviving corpus is very large: reference works put it at **about 90 works**, including **45 letters (Epistulae)**, the *Opuscula theologica et polemica*, *Ambigua ad Ioannem* (71 items) and *Ambigua ad Thomam*, *Quaestiones ad Thalassium* (65), *Quaestiones et dubia*, *Mystagogia*, *Liber asceticus*, the *Four Hundred Chapters on Love*, *Chapters on Knowledge*, commentaries on Ps. 59 and the Our Father, and the *Computus ecclesiasticus*; the trial dossier (*Relatio motionis*, *Disputatio Bizyae*) is transmitted with the corpus. | `corrected` → vi "khoảng 90 tác phẩm còn lại (gồm 45 thư)"; en "c. 90 surviving works (including 45 letters)" — or drop the count and keep `works[]` as a selection | [Springer/encyclopedia summary via search](https://link.springer.com/rwe/10.1007/978-94-024-1151-5_322-2), [Yale Univ. Library, Maximus guide](https://guides.library.yale.edu/ancientchristianity/maximus), [Jankowiak & Booth, "A New Date-List of the Works of Maximus", Oxford Handbook](https://academic.oup.com/edited-volume/38661/chapter/335782714) |
| `role.en/vi`, `sections[context]` | "Monk; formerly first secretary to Emperor Heraclius" / "bí thư thứ nhất của hoàng đế Hêracliô" — stated flatly | This rests **only on the Greek *Life***, which is late (no earlier than the 10th c.) and makes him a Constantinopolitan aristocrat who became *protoasekretis* in 610. The **Syriac *Life* by George of Resh'aina** — contemporary, hostile, and much older — instead has him born at **Hesfin** east of Lake Tiberias in Palestine, with no imperial secretaryship. Current scholarship leans to the Syriac: Jankowiak & Booth's date-list in the *Oxford Handbook of Maximus the Confessor* (2015) explicitly takes "the Syriac *Life* as the principal source for Maximus' life." A third reading (Alexandrian origin) is argued in a recent article reevaluating both *Lives*. The entry presents the Greek-*Life* career as settled fact and never mentions the dispute. | `contested` → hedge, e.g. vi "Đan sĩ; theo tiểu sử Hy Lạp (muộn hơn), trước đó là bí thư thứ nhất của hoàng đế Hêracliô — nguồn này bị nhiều học giả nghi ngờ"; en "Monk; according to the (later) Greek *Life*, formerly first secretary to Emperor Heraclius — a claim modern scholarship treats as doubtful" | [Jankowiak & Booth, Oxford Handbook](https://academic.oup.com/edited-volume/38661/chapter/335782714), [George of Resh'aina / Syriac Life](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_of_Resh%27aina), [Booth, "Maximos the Confessor" (Oxford ORA)](https://ora.ox.ac.uk/objects/uuid:4cb0a3cf-d3bf-495e-9e6b-81a73f64fb0a), ["The Other Life of Maximus… the Case for His Alexandrian Origin", Project MUSE](https://muse.jhu.edu/article/772166) |
| `facts[Nơi hoạt động / Region–See]` | "Constantinôpôli; bị lưu đày đến Bắc Phi, Rôma, và cuối cùng Lazica" / "exiled to North Africa, Rome, and finally Lazica" | **North Africa and Rome were not exiles.** He withdrew to North Africa ahead of the Persian/Arab advance and went to Rome of his own accord (he helped organise the Lateran Synod of 649 there). The actual judicial exiles are: after the trial of **655**, exile to **Bizya in Thrace** (his disciple Anastasius to Perberis); then after the second trial in **662**, exile to **Lazica**, where he died on 13 August 662 at the fortress of **Schemaris**. Bizya — the one exile the word properly fits first — is missing from the entry entirely. | `corrected` → vi "Constantinôpôli; sống tại Bắc Phi và Rôma; bị lưu đày đến Bizya (Thrace, 655) và cuối cùng Lazica (nay là Gruzia, 662)"; en "Constantinople; resident in North Africa and Rome; exiled to Bizya in Thrace (655) and finally to Lazica (modern Georgia, 662)" | [Allen & Neil, *Oxford Handbook of Maximus*, "Life and Times" ch.](https://academic.oup.com/edited-volume/38661), [Allen & Neil, *Maximus the Confessor and his Companions: Documents from Exile* (full text)](https://archive.org/stream/orthodox-christian-library/Orthodox%20Christian%20Library%20-%20V1/Allen%20&%20Neil%20-%20Maximus%20the%20Confessor%20&%20his%20Companions_djvu.txt) |
| `sections[martyrdom]` | "được ghi chép khác thường đầy đủ… nhờ **nhiều nguồn độc lập**, bao gồm **người môn đệ kiêm tiểu sử gia Anastasiô**" / "unusually well documented… thanks to **multiple independent sources**, including **the pupil-biographer Anastasius**" | Two problems. (a) **Not independent.** The 7th-c. dossier — *Relatio motionis*, *Disputatio Bizyae*, the letters of Anastasius, the *Hypomnesticon/Commemoratio*, *Contra Constantinopolitanos* — is edited together as CCSG 39 precisely because it all comes from **one partisan Maximian circle**; scholars describe these as written "from a Maximianist perspective" by authors who "must have belonged to the direct circle of Maximos." Theophanes' *Chronographia* is c. 815, over 150 years later, and stands in that same tradition. So this is one hostile-to-the-emperor tradition, not independent corroboration. (b) **Neither Anastasius wrote a biography.** No *Life* of Maximus comes from a pupil; the *Hypomnesticon* — the document that reports the mutilation — is attributed in CCSG 39 to **Theodosius of Gangra**, and by Allen & Neil to **Theodore Spoudaeus** (668/9). "Người môn đệ kiêm tiểu sử gia Anastasiô" is not a person who exists. | `corrected`/`contested` → vi "được ghi chép khá đầy đủ đối với một nhân vật thế kỷ VII, tuy các nguồn đều xuất phát từ nhóm môn đệ của ngài (bản *Hypomnesticon* năm 668/9 và các thư của hai môn đệ tên Anastasiô), rồi sau đó là sử gia Theophanes (khoảng năm 815)"; en "…relatively well documented for a 7th-century figure, though the sources all come from his own circle of disciples (the *Hypomnesticon* of 668/9 and the letters of the two Anastasii), and then the chronicler Theophanes c. 815" | [Brepols, CCSG 39 *Scripta saeculi VII vitam Maximi Confessoris illustrantia*](https://www.brepols.net/products/IS-9782503403915-1), [Allen & Neil, *Maximus the Confessor and his Companions* (OUP)](https://global.oup.com/academic/product/maximus-the-confessor-and-his-companions-9780198299912), [Booth, "Maximos the Confessor" (Oxford ORA)](https://ora.ox.ac.uk/objects/uuid:4cb0a3cf-d3bf-495e-9e6b-81a73f64fb0a) |
| `sections[martyrdom]` — the mutilation itself | "Bị cắt lưỡi và chặt tay phải" stated as established fact throughout (`life[1]` too) | Reference works consistently phrase this as **"according to tradition"** rather than as documented fact, because it rests on the partisan dossier above plus Theophanes. It is widely accepted and there is some corroborating evidence (relics/skeletal remains in Georgia reportedly missing the right hand), but it is not on the same evidential footing as, say, his trials. The entry's own contrast — calling John of Damascus's severed hand a legend while calling this "genuinely documented" — overstates the gap. | `contested` → keep the account but hedge once, e.g. vi "Theo các nguồn từ nhóm môn đệ của ngài, ngài bị cắt lưỡi và chặt tay phải"; en "According to the sources from his circle of disciples, his tongue was cut out and his right hand severed" | [Britannica, St Maximus the Confessor](https://www.britannica.com/biography/Saint-Maximus-the-Confessor), [Brepols CCSG 39](https://www.brepols.net/products/IS-9782503403915-1) |
| `name.vi` (minor) | "Maximô Tuyên Tín" | HĐGM VN's own usage is **"Thánh Maximô Người Tuyên Tín"** ("Thánh Maximô Người Tuyên Tín, một trong những nhà tư tưởng sâu sắc nhất của Kitô giáo, đã viết:"). The site's own Catechism (`content/content.json`) does not attest the name. The site's shorter "Maximô Tuyên Tín" is intelligible and not wrong, but it is not the form the bishops' conference uses. Low priority. | `contested` → optionally `name.vi` "Maximô Người Tuyên Tín" (and the same in `church-fathers-vi.json`) | [HĐGM VN homily citing "Thánh Maximô Người Tuyên Tín"](https://hdgmvietnam.com/chi-tiet/bai-giang-thu-sau-tuan-thanh-mau-nhiem-thap-gia-toa-sang-54648) |

**Not checked:** the Vietnamese transliterations other than `name.vi` (`Hêracliô`, `Ngụy-Điônysiô Arêôpagitô`, `Cappađôcia`, `Constantinôpôli`, `Grêgôriô thành Nazianzô`, `Latêranô`) were not run against Vietnamese Catholic authorities. The four bibliographic items in `sections[further-reading]` were not individually verified (von Balthasar's revised edition is given as 1962 by the Balthasar–Speyr foundation and as 1961 by other listings — the entry's 1962 matches the foundation, so I left it alone). The `sections[context]` account of miaphysite Christology in Egypt/Syria/Palestine and Heraclius's motives was read for factual errors but not source-verified in detail. `era` "post-nicene" not adjudicated (soft grouping; the legacy file calls him "Later Patristic").

### 30. john-of-damascus — John of Damascus

**Checked:** `quote` located at *On the Divine Images* I.16 in two published translations (Anderson 1980 p.23; Allies 1898) and the longer `sections[more-quotes]` version matched to the Allies text; `dates`, feast day and Doctor status against the *New Catholic Encyclopedia*, the 1913 *Catholic Encyclopedia*, Britannica and TGP Sài Gòn; the iconoclast sequence (Leo III c.726 → Hieria 754 → Nicaea II 787 → 843) against Brubaker & Haldon and NCE; the reliability of the Arabic/Greek *Lives*, the caliphal-service claim and the severed-hand story against NCE, the 1913 CE and Louth (OUP 2002); the Burgundio of Pisa translation (1153/54, at Pope Eugenius III's request, feeding Peter Lombard's *Sentences*) — **confirmed**, no row; the latria/dulia attribution against Augustine and Aquinas; `name.vi` against HĐGM VN, TGP Sài Gòn and Giáo phận Vĩnh Long plus a grep of the site's own Catechism; `apologetics[]` factual halves; `ccc_refs` empty; cross-checked `church-fathers.json` / `church-fathers-vi.json` id 30 — they mirror this file (same "2 works"-equivalent gap, same dates), no contradictions.

| Field | Claim as written | What the sources say | Verdict | Sources |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `facts[Tác phẩm còn lại / Surviving works]` | "2 tác phẩm" / "2 works" | The site-wide `works[].length` defect again, and here the most misleading instance in the section. The *New Catholic Encyclopedia* reports that **J. Hoeck identified 150 titles as authentic works**. The *Fount of Knowledge* is itself a trilogy (*Dialectica* / *On Heresies* / *An Exact Exposition of the Orthodox Faith*), and beyond it and the three *Apologetic Treatises against those who Decry the Holy Images* there survive dogmatic and polemical treatises, exegetical works, homilies (including the three on the Dormition), hagiography, and a large body of liturgical hymnography (canons, the Octoechos material). | `corrected` → vi "khoảng 150 tác phẩm được coi là chân thực (theo mục lục của J. Hoeck), gồm các luận văn, bài giảng và thánh thi"; en "c. 150 works reckoned authentic (Hoeck's catalogue), including treatises, homilies and hymns" — or drop the count | [New Catholic Encyclopedia, "John Damascene, St."](https://www.encyclopedia.com/religion/encyclopedias-almanacs-transcripts-and-maps/john-damascene-st), [Encyclopedia.com, "John of Damascus"](https://www.encyclopedia.com/environment/encyclopedias-almanacs-transcripts-and-maps/john-damascus) |
| `dates` / `facts[Sinh]` / `facts[Qua đời]` / `sections[martyrdom]` | "c. 675 — 749", "khoảng năm 675/676", "4 tháng 12, năm 749" — and the martyrdom section states the death date flatly ("qua đời… vào ngày 4 tháng 12 năm 749") | **Both ends are genuinely disputed.** The *New Catholic Encyclopedia* gives **born c. 645, died c. 750**, and says outright that "the chronology of his life and particularly the date of his death is uncertain," 749 being commonly cited after S. Vailhé. Other reference works give birth c. 676 and death "some time between 754 and 787," or born c. 679 / died 749. The one firm terminus is that he was **dead before the synod of Hieria in 754**. 4 December is the traditional, not the documented, date. The entry's `675/676` and flat `4 tháng 12, năm 749` are false precision — and `dates.born` 675 does not even match the `facts` row's "675/676". | `contested` → vi `display` "khoảng 675 (?) — khoảng 749/750", `facts[Sinh]` "khoảng năm 675/676 (một số nguồn: khoảng 645) [cần kiểm chứng]", `facts[Qua đời]` "theo truyền thống ngày 4 tháng 12, khoảng năm 749/750"; en "c. 675 (?) — c. 749/750", "traditionally 4 December, c. 749/750" | [New Catholic Encyclopedia, "John Damascene, St."](https://www.encyclopedia.com/religion/encyclopedias-almanacs-transcripts-and-maps/john-damascene-st), [Encyclopedia.com, "John of Damascus"](https://www.encyclopedia.com/environment/encyclopedias-almanacs-transcripts-and-maps/john-damascus), [Catholic Encyclopedia (1913), "St John Damascene"](https://www.newadvent.org/cathen/08459b.htm) |

**Not checked:** `facts[Lễ kính]` "4 tháng 12" is right for the current General Roman Calendar (optional memorial) and for the Byzantine calendar, but the entry does not note that the **pre-1969 Roman calendar kept 27 March** (so the 1913 *Catholic Encyclopedia* gives that date) — I confirmed the pre-1969 date but did not chase whether the section wants that noted. The claim in `sections[later-influence]` that Burgundio's translation "became a standard theological textbook at the University of Paris" was not verified as stated — the sources support it as a heavily used *source* for Peter Lombard and Aquinas rather than as a set textbook, and Paris was not yet a university in 1153/54. Vietnamese transliterations other than `name.vi` (`Nixêa`, `Giêrusalem`, `Êugêniô III`, `Phêrô Lombard`, `Tôma Aquinô`, `Lêô III`) were not run against Vietnamese Catholic authorities. Grandfather Manṣūr's negotiation of the surrender of Damascus was not independently verified beyond the entry's own "được cho là / reportedly" hedge, which is appropriate either way. `sections[theology]`'s account of the *Fount of Knowledge* as an anticipation of the Western *summa* was not source-checked (it is an editorial judgement, not a hard fact).

**Keep for `sources` retrofit:** [New Catholic Encyclopedia, "John Damascene, St."](https://www.encyclopedia.com/religion/encyclopedias-almanacs-transcripts-and-maps/john-damascene-st) · [Andrew Louth, *St John Damascene: Tradition and Originality in Byzantine Theology* (OUP 2002)](https://www.journals.uchicago.edu/doi/10.1086/426976) · [HĐGM VN, "7 điều cần biết về vị Giáo phụ cuối cùng"](https://hdgmvietnam.com/chi-tiet/7-dieu-can-biet-ve-vi-giao-phu-cuoi-cung)

## How pass 1 ran

Every entry has been checked at the full standard, `sections[]` bodies included. The six Fathers
quoted inside the Giải Đáp clusters (7, 9, 13, 21, 23 and, added after a re-scope, 10, 16, 20) were
additionally checked on a second surface — their Q&A citations — recorded above under "Surface B".

**Surface B findings are a hand-off to Session 3**, which owns `content/giai-dap`. They are collected
in `docs/handoff-session3-patristic-quotes.md`. This audit applied none of them.

### What pass 1 cost, and what it caught

Two waves of verifier agents were destroyed mid-run by API limits before any output reached disk —
24 entry-verifications lost. The fix was to require every verifier to **create its output file first
and append each row as it was confirmed**, rather than writing at the end. That change is recorded
in the brief's "SAVE AS YOU GO" section and should be carried into the Công Đồng audit.

### Method notes worth carrying forward

- **The `sections[]` bodies are where the worst problems live.** Entries 5 and 6 were once marked
  verified on their summary fields alone; opening their `sections[]` roughly tripled and quadrupled
  their finding counts, and three of entry 6's worst errors were in a single `more-quotes` section
  that had been ticked off unread.
- **Sweep by `/giao-phu/<slug>` cross-link, not by name.** The name-based sweep that scoped the
  load-bearing set missed Cyprian, because the Q&A spells him `Cyprianô` (the site standard) while
  the Giáo Phụ entry spells him `Ciprianô` — finding S4 defeating the tooling built to find it.
- **Mechanical checks found two of the largest defects** (S5, S6) with no web research at all. Run
  those first on Công Đồng.
- **Several findings cut in the site's favour.** Adding the five-day Chalcedon examination of Leo's
  Tome strengthens the papal-authority case; Eusebius is a weak leg in the icons Q&A and citing him
  concedes ground the site need not give up; Isidore's history-writing line was *over*-hedged when it
  is verbatim. A verification pass is not only a list of things to take away.

## Next steps

| # | Step | State |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Pass 1 — verify Giáo Phụ (30) | **complete** |
| 2 | Owner reviews and approves corrections, in batches | **ready** |
| 3 | Pass 2 — apply approved rows to `content/giao-phu` | not started |
| 4 | Session 3 applies the Giải Đáp hand-off | handed off |
| 5 | Công Đồng — 21 councils | **not started** |

**Pass 2 must apply each approved fix in up to three files** — the per-slug
`content/giao-phu/<slug>.json`, `church-fathers.json`, and `church-fathers-vi.json` — because
`lib/churchFathers.ts` still feeds the homepage and search page from the legacy datasets. The
exceptions are noted per finding: S6 touches one file per entry, and `sections[]` fixes have no
counterpart in the legacy files at all. See S3.

**Suggested batching for review**, easiest and highest-confidence first:

1. **S6** — 4 broken Vietnamese strings, mechanical, no research needed.
2. **S5** — the "Surviving works" relabel, one decision applied 30 times.
3. **Fabricated or misattributed quotations** — the highest-risk class: entries 1, 2, 3, 6, 8, 9, 20,
   22, 23, and the citation-only fixes in 15, 28.
4. **Self-contradictions**, where the entry's own prose already refutes its summary field — entries
   8, 15, 24, 26, 27.
5. **Dates, counts and hedges** — the long tail.
6. **S4 Vietnamese names** — 6 entries, but note entry 22 blocks entry 25.

## Note for the follow-on `sources` retrofit

Per the spec this is deliberately **not** folded into the audit. Each entry ends with a **Keep for
`sources` retrofit** line naming the best citations found for that figure, so the roadmap item can
start from those rather than re-researching. Strongest recurring sources: newadvent.org/fathers
(ANF/NPNF), earlychristianwritings.com, tertullian.org, the vatican.va papal catecheses (repeatedly
the decisive source, and repeatedly at odds with the entries), and Britannica / Encyclopedia.com /
New Catholic Encyclopedia for contested date ranges.
